  - two synthetic loops in `tests/video/test_loop_prop_seam_held.py` (one cut two steps long, one cycle, strip `steps: 2`, and one cut one cycle with no `steps`, neither walker's body rising and falling and its feet never passing, so both reports hold their cuts, nothing witnessing either whole cycles): on copies whose reports the test rewrites to witness their cuts (`cut_witness` saying "two steps" by the screen, nothing held), the aligner works as before: the two-step loop returns half way at its second step, which is no suspect (`status` "steps", `steps` 2, candidates `[2]`), and both loops are resampled to the median of their lengths as cut, nothing kept twice as long, the strip of the two-step copy still saying `steps: 2`, the other none; on the loops as cut, `video-cycle-align` stops on both, each named `held` with reason `cycle-unwitnessed`; a count of one cycle for each (`cycles`, the caller's word) aligns them as they are, to the same median length, each suspect `status` "counted"; a count of 2 for the two-step loop (and 1 for the other) still takes one half out of it, and its strip no longer says `steps`; two copies of the loop with no held part, aligned with a count of one cycle each, are written as before: no `cycles_kept` and no `steps` in their rows, their strip metadata with the same keys, and the cut's source (`source_cut`) as the cut wrote it (previously both aligned with no count, the two-step loop's return read as its steps).
  - the synthetic cut of 13 cells with its record of its cells, aligned by `video-cycle-align` to 17 cells, in `tests/video/test_sway_read_on_recut.py` (with `--between rife` (a blend standing in for RIFE) the aligned strip record holds no `cell_record`, `cycle_align.cells_from` gives 17 cells, each `{"source": i}` cell is cell i of the cut pixel for pixel and each `{"between": [i, j], "t": t}` cell is a copy of no cell of the cut, `cycle.source/run.strip.json` is the cut's record with 13 cells, source frames 1 to 13, and `video-sway --read-on-cut` is refused `uncertain` on the pair the kept record gives (source frame 4), naming the first made cell, the loop as it was; with `--between nearest` every one of the 17 cells is one of the cut's, and `--read-on-cut` pairs source frame 4 with the cut's cell 3 at shift (3, 2) and carries it through, `cut_cells` equal to the `cells_from` sources).

### Contract strings

- **Internal: one declaration per contract string**: a key, kind, mode, provider name or key colour that two or more places write, read or branch on was spelt again at each place, so a one-letter typo passed every test (for example the curation server's `extractOk` spelt `extractOK` made the curator warn on every row with the suite green). Each is now declared once and every use names it:
  - curation and composer servers: every key of the JSON the curator and composer read, in `serve_curation.Wire` (112 keys and the `X-Filename` download header) and `serve_compose.Wire` (16 keys, plus `TYPE_DIR`, `TYPE_IMAGE`); the heal report and progress file keys in `frames/extract.py` (`HEAL_*`, `PROGRESS_*`, `PROGRESS_PHASE_COMPONENTS`); `curation.CURATION_KIND`, `REF_ANCHOR`, `REF_BASIS`, `REF_GUIDE`, `BREATHE_PIXEL`, `BREATHE_SMOOTH`.
  - image provider names `PROVIDER_CODEX`, `PROVIDER_GROK`, `PROVIDER_OPENAI` in `gen/base.py`, with `gen.PROVIDERS` and the other provider sets written with them; the curator's two buttons gated by one set, `serve_curation.CURATOR_PROVIDERS`; the reroll default is `gen.HARD_DEFAULT_PROVIDER`; the interpolate default is `interpolate.DEFAULT_PROVIDER` (codex, its own documented choice from the three-way comparison); the HTTP host names `address.OPENAI`, `XAI`, `ANTHROPIC`, `ELEVENLABS`; `retry.ELEVENLABS_BUSY`; `audio.manifest.KIND`, `SFX`, `BGM`; the workflow guide's journey, field and access names in `workflow/catalog.py` and `workflow/access.py`; `video-prompt` takes `gen.video.DEFAULT_MODEL` instead of a second copy; the choice to stop and ask when codex cannot run is `gen.CODEX_UNAVAILABLE_ASK`, which `gen.CODEX_UNAVAILABLE_CHOICES`, `gen.CODEX_UNAVAILABLE_DEFAULT` and the workflow catalogue read; the guide reports that choice under `gen.CODEX_UNAVAILABLE_SETTING`, the name it is saved by; `xai.AUTH_SOURCE_API_KEY` is `xai.AUTH_ENV`; `claude_login.CHILD_DROP` reads `anthropic_api.AUTH_ENV`; the codex binary's name is `codex_provider.BINARY`; `pixel_draw.EFFORTS` is `anthropic_api.EFFORTS`, the copy removed.
  - key colours and modes: `cutout.KEY_TARGETS` with `MAGENTA` and `GREEN` beside it is the one table of key colours (`gen.chroma.KEYS` is built from it, `logo_matte.KEYS` is it, `slice_sheet.CHROMA_KEYS` is removed, the hex values in `prepare`, `unpack-atlas` and the curation server are derived from it); `cutout.KEYS` is cutout's own `--key` set; `OFF, AUTO, PALETTE = DECONTAM_MODES` and `CHROMA_RGB, CHROMA_YCBCR = CHROMA_MODES` are declared in `frames/extract.py`, and `frames.decontam.DECONTAM_MODES` and `video.frames.DECONTAM_MODES` are that same tuple; every `--decontam`, `--chroma-mode` and `--key` choice reads its declaration; `logo_matte.LETTERING_BOX`, `UNMIX`; `prompt_parts.CONFLICT`, `REPEAT`; `gen.chroma.KEY_BACKGROUND_TEXT`, `gen.logo.KEY_COLOUR_WORDS` and a scene report's `key_like_pixels` are keyed by `cutout.GREEN` and `MAGENTA`; `prop_plate.RGB` reads `KEY_TARGETS[MAGENTA]`; `video.frames.DECONTAM_DEFAULT` is `extract.AUTO` and video-frames' decontam branches read `OFF`, `AUTO` and `PALETTE`; `video-frames --alpha-mode` reads `effect.ALPHA_MODES`; the `method` a `gen.chroma` report states reads `extract.CHROMA_YCBCR` and `base.TRANSPARENCY_NATIVE`.
  - kinds: `loop.REPORT_KIND`, `loop.REPORT_PASSED`; `spec.assets.ASSET_KIND`, `SET_SHEET_KIND` (`set_sheet.KIND` names it); `qa.motion.REPORT_KIND`; `video.evidence.SOURCE_KIND`; `nonloop.GAIT_STATES` (the copies in `qa.handed` and `video.batch` removed); `body_plan.BIPED`, `QUADRUPED`, `LEGLESS`; `effect.STATE` read by `video-set` and `video-prompt`; `pixel_gates.SPRITE`, `ICON`, `TILE`, `EFFECT` (`pixel_draw.KINDS` is that tuple); new names `spec.runio.REQUEST_KIND`, `video.frames.REPORT_KIND`, `video.align.REPORT_KIND`, `unpack_atlas.UNPACK_SOURCE_KIND`; `video-canvas` writes `prop_plate.CANVAS_REPORT`, the name the report is read by; the `kind` of a pixel-draw effect strip is `pixel_gates.EFFECT`; a `video-cycle-align` same-foot refusal states `align.RETAKE_FEET_REASON`.
  - other copies: `sprite-request.json` and `curation.json` are read as `runio.REQUEST_FILENAME` and `curation.CURATION_FILENAME` everywhere they are written, opened or checked; `pixel_grid.PNG_MAGIC` is `base.PNG_MAGIC`; `restoration.ARTIFACT_SUFFIXES` is the one table of a restoration's five files (`restoration.ARTIFACTS` is its keys, the demo script reads it); `game_pack.SIDES` is `ui_kit.SIDES`; `video-canvas --facing`, `video-loop --facing` and a lateral view's check in `gen.handedness` read `gen.facing.FACINGS`; `effects.breathe.SIDE_NAMES` is keyed by `extract.FRAME_SIDES`.
  - Values that no test bound before and that now cannot drift: the legless anatomy sentence key, `effect` in `video-set`'s states pinned to their first frame, the pixel-draw pick sheet's tile branch, the loop report kind `video-cycle-align` reads (its typo passed the fast bundle).
- Python callers: every name above is importable. Downstream imports keep the same objects: `sprite_gen.video.frames.DECONTAM_MODES`, `sprite_gen.frames.cutout.KEY_TARGETS`, `sprite_gen.gen.chroma.KEY_BACKGROUND_TEXT`, `set_sheet.KIND`, `pixel_draw.KINDS`, `batch.GAIT_STATES`. Removed: `slice_sheet.CHROMA_KEYS` (use `cutout.KEY_TARGETS`). `clip_prompt.DEFAULT_MODEL` is `gen.video.DEFAULT_MODEL`, imported (the same object). `qa.handed.GAIT_STATES` is now a frozenset (was a tuple); every use is a membership test. `sprite_gen.frames.cutout` now imports `frames.extract` when it loads (for the decontam defaults), so importing it loads more modules than before; `pixel_draw.EFFORTS`, `game_pack.SIDES` and `pixel_grid.PNG_MAGIC` stay importable as the same objects; `loop.FACINGS` is removed (use `sprite_gen.gen.facing.FACINGS`).
- **Curator**: the JS mirror `BREATHE_MODES` moved from `store.js` to `breathe.js`, with `BREATHE_SMOOTH` beside it; `gen-trigger.js`'s provider select mirrors `serve_curation.CURATOR_PROVIDERS`. No screen changes; `BREATHE_ROUND_KEYS` moved from `store.js` to `breathe.js`, beside the refusal that spelt the two keys.
- **Tests**: new `tests/serve/test_curator_wire_keys.py`: every key the curator and composer read is found in the scripts with the object it is read from, built from the declared value; every declared key is read or listed as unread, the unread ones spelt out in the test; the servers write no wire key, and send no header the curator reads, as text; every `Wire.X` the servers name exists. `tests/serve/test_curation_view_contract.py`: the reroll and interpolate buttons follow `CURATOR_PROVIDERS` and their declared defaults, and node runs `gen-trigger.js` to hold its select to `CURATOR_PROVIDERS` in order. `tests/effects/test_breathe_sidecar_roundtrip.py`: the curator's save payload kind is `CURATION_KIND`. `tests/frames/test_cutout.py` and `tests/frames/test_chroma_decontam.py`: every key table is the one table, every `--key`, `--decontam` and `--chroma-mode` flag reads its declaration, `cutout.KEYS` pinned as written. `tests/gen/test_codex_unavailable.py` (the codex-unavailable choices in order), `tests/gen/test_openai_provider.py` (adapter names equal `gen.PROVIDERS`), `tests/audio/test_sound.py` (the two ElevenLabs busy codes), `tests/workflow/test_capabilities.py` (the video model default), `tests/gen/test_prompt_assembly.py` (a repeat note's kind). The two absence guards that searched `serve_curation.py` for a key's spelling (`tests/serve/test_pixel_grid_always_available.py`, `tests/curate/test_logical_height_contract.py`) now search for its name; with the old spelling they would have passed while testing nothing. Red first: at 3.10.1 the new binding tests fail (for example the wire-key test finds 217 keys written as text in `serve_curation.py` and 55 in `serve_compose.py`; 8 `--decontam` parsers held their own copy; `video-set`'s `--key` was a copy). Mutation check: one character changed in a declared value fails a test in every family; before, 16 of 25 sampled curation key changes, the provider adapter name, either ElevenLabs busy code, the video model default and the loop report kind under the fast bundle left the suite green. `tests/frames/test_cutout.py` (no table in the package keys a key colour by its spelling (an AST scan of `sprite_gen`)), `tests/gen/test_codex_unavailable.py` (the guide reports the codex choice under the name it is saved by), `tests/gen/test_pixel_draw.py` (the effort order is the API's), `tests/compose/test_game_pack_v2.py` (a nine-slice's sides are `ui_kit`'s), `tests/gen/test_facing.py` (`video-loop --facing` takes the facings `gen.facing` declares), `tests/effects/test_breathe_reference_key.py` (the JS frame sides and border share equal `extract.FRAME_SIDES` and `BORDER_MIN_FRACTION`), `tests/spec/test_assets.py` (the kinds written to disk are pinned as written), `tests/gen/test_codex_login.py` (the codex binary pinned as written), `tests/video/test_source_restoration.py` (a restoration's five file suffixes pinned as written).

## v3.12.1 - A one-shot cut where no pair returns names its closest return, ratio and moved, and the report gains cycle.closest_return

**Upgrading from 3.12.0: Thresholds, the search window, which clips are cut, and every cut's pictures and report values are the same as 3.12.0.** Only the refusal text changes where no pair of frames returns, and `--report` gains `cycle.closest_return`. The refusal now names the closest return, its frames, its ratio of departure, and that pair's moved instead of reporting `moved 0.00`. Where a pair returns but what lies between is not an action, or where no pair departs at all, the refusal line is the 3.12.0 line as before.

- **Fix: A one-shot cut where no pair of frames returns now names the closest return, its ratio and its moved instead of reporting `moved 0.00`.** In 3.12.0 and earlier, where no pair of frames returned, the refusal said `the clip never leaves its rest pose and returns with observed endpoints (moved 0.00, need 0.4)`. The moved it reported was the largest moved among pairs that had returned, and because none had, it always read 0.00; a body that jumps across the canvas therefore read as one that never moved. Where no pair returns, the refusal now names the closest return: the pair of frames whose return distance over departure is the least of every pair the search read, that ratio, and that pair's moved. After the fixed prefix, the line is `the clip does not come back to a pose it left: the closest return, frames A-B, is R of its departure away, over 0.25 (moved M, need 0.4)`. On a synthetic leap whose body lands 240 px along a 360 px canvas, the new line reads `frames 0-34, is 1.12 of its departure away, over 0.25 (moved 0.36, need 0.4)`. The report written with `--report` gains `cycle.closest_return` with keys `pair`, `ratio`, `seam`, `departure` and `moved`. Where a pair does return but what lies between is not an action (too little contrast or moved, fewer than four active frames, or a departure under three times the steps between its frames), or where no pair departs at all, the 3.12.0 line is printed as before.
- Python callers: `loop.ONE_SHOT_RETURN_MAX` names the return bound (0.25). The `CycleSelectionError` diagnostics of `detect_one_shot` carry `closest_return` where some pair departed.
- **Docs**: [video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md) (section "One-shot actions") documents the return bound `ONE_SHOT_RETURN_MAX`, the refusal line, `cycle.closest_return`, and cutting canvas-crossing jumps with `--cycle once --start S --length L` from the closest return's frames. `SKILL.md` updates its single-play motion guidance with the same command and refusal quote.
- **Tests**: `tests/video/test_one_shot_refusal.py` adds five tests drawn with rectangles and no real footage: (1) a leap whose body crosses the canvas is refused on its closest return, naming the pair with the least return over departure in the searched window and matching `closest_return` in the report; (2) the same leap in place is cut from rest to rest; (3) in place, a take-off crouch and a landing crouch that are two different pictures, ending in the landing stance, is refused on its closest return; (4) a stand moved up to 3 px along and up or down every frame is refused across twelve seeds (the test runs one seed); (5) a clip that never departs is refused with the 3.12.0 line and no `closest_return`. Red first: on 3.12.0, 2 failed and 3 passed (tests 1 and 3 refused with `moved 0.00`); on 3.12.1, 5 passed.

## v3.12.0 - Prop plate painted on the canvas (video-canvas --prop-plate) and erased from keyed frames (video-frames --prop-plate <canvas report json>)

**Upgrading from 3.11.0: runs without the new options produce unchanged outputs: every canvas, frame, strip, GIF, WebP, and report keeps its exact bytes (with only the loop report's `producer.implementation_sha256` changing with source code changes).**

- **New: `video-canvas --prop-plate`** paints a flat magenta plate, RGB (255, 0, 255), on the canvas in front of the subject (`--facing`) standing on the subject's foot row, sized by `canvas.subject_box` (height 0.49, thickness 0.20, near side 0.84 of subject height ahead of the subject's front). Requires a green key and `--report`. Refuses non-green keys, missing `--report`, state `effect`, or plates extending past the canvas edge. The report records `prop_plate` with `box`, `rgb`, `subject`, and `of_subject_height`.
- **New: `video-frames --prop-plate <canvas report json>`** reads the plate box from the canvas report, scales it outward to frame coordinates with a 3 px rim (`erase_box`), and erases the plate from keyed frames before handover. Pixels are erased where red and blue lead green by more than 35 and are within 90 of each other, or where each channel (including alpha) is within 40 of the whole-clip median. Refused runs keep the plate; `video-loop` cuts of erased frames carry no plate; `video-set` does not paint or erase a plate. The report records `prop_plate`.
- Limits: the video model must keep the plate in place; a one-pixel shift leaves grey blend lines down the sides (188 and 95 pixels in synthetic tests). Subject edge pixels tinted magenta by the codec are erased (2 pixels in each of two frames in synthetic tests). No other guard, retry, or measurement is added.
- Python callers: `sprite_gen.video.prop_plate` (`place`, `paint`, `read`, `frame_box`, `erase`; constants `RGB`, `HEIGHT`, `WIDTH`, `GAP`, `RIM`, `MAGENTA_LEAD`, `MAGENTA_BALANCE`, `STILL_TOLERANCE`); `canvas.run_canvas(..., prop_plate=False)`; `frames.run_frames(..., prop_plate=None)`; `frames.key_frames(..., plate=None)`.
- **Docs**: [video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md) section 1 subsection and section 3 pointer; `SKILL.md`; `docs/README.md`.
- **Tests**: `tests/video/test_prop_plate.py` (12 synthetic tests: canvas painting facing right and left, unchanged canvas without option, refusals for bounds, key, and report, frame erasing, one-pixel model shifts, subject crossing preservation, frames outside the box, erase reading the canvas report box, refusal for report without plate or not a canvas report, frame box arithmetic, loop cuts without magenta). Red first: median without alpha lost 47, 47, 136 and 136 outline pixels in frames 8 to 11; with alpha, 2, 2, 0, and 0 codec-tinted pixels. Mutation check: median without alpha fails test 7; no magenta rule fails tests 6 and 12; no median rule fails test 6; rim 0 fails three tests; unscaled box fails two.

## v3.11.0 - Sway regions laid over the figure (--region-over), rims widened for shear (--region-shear), figure membranes (--region-membrane), and recut strip pairing by cell records (--read-on-cut)

**Upgrading from 3.10.1: `video-loop` writes `cell_record` into every strip record `<name>.strip.json`, and `video-sway` gains options that shape a `--region-mask` region alongside `--read-on-cut`. Strips, frames and records keep their bytes by default, but `video-sway --read-on-cut` reads cuts by that record alone and refuses cuts made without it.** New options `--region-over` and `--region-shear` shape the region mask and its rim; both are off by default, and when not given, strips, frames, and records remain byte for byte what they were. With `--region-crease` the wear wears the crease in as before; laid over the figure the region reaches down to the crease again in every cell, changing those runs' output. Runs with `--region-over` and runs with `--region-membrane` draw differently than in 3.10.1; the four new membrane options are off by default and byte for byte as before when not given. New options `--region-over-solid`, `--region-rim-round`, `--region-membrane` and `--read-on-cut` are off by default; when not given, strips, frames, and records remain byte for byte what they were. Runs with `--read-on` alone behave as before, except that its no-match message now names `--read-on-cut`. The refusal for `--region-over` or `--region-shear` given without `--region-mask` keeps refusing the same runs, and its message now names the new options too: "--region-over, --region-over-solid, --region-rim-round, --region-shear and --region-membrane shape a region mask; they need --region-mask" (it was "--region-over and --region-shear shape a region mask; they need --region-mask").

- **New: laying a region over the figure and widening the rim for shear (`--region-over`, `--region-shear`).** `--region-over` lays the region over the figure under it instead of pinning its lower edge, freeing held pixels under the edge (solid or not), moving the band within reach, drawing the layer over the band by alpha, drawing the air by the move, and showing the figure under its lower edge at the move where it lifts. Its soft edge moves with the region (except under the lower edge where touching a solid pixel outside keeps it in the band), its solid outline against air goes with the layer, and `--hold-mask` leaves pixels the region mask does not cover out of the layer and band while pixels it covers move with the region. `--region-shear S` widens the rim across and down so a move one way rises across the rim the other way by at most S px per px (`shear_floor`), preventing tears along the sides. With either option, the ledger records move steps between neighbouring pixels, still strips left standing, and tip outline shapes, and `--region-over` records `lifted_px`. Both require `--region-mask`, are off by default, and leave strips, frames, and records byte for byte what they were when not given. See [video-pipeline](docs/video-pipeline.md) section 6.
- **Changed: wearing the crease row in like every other edge of the mask and reaching down to the crease only when laying a region over the figure (`--region-crease`, `--region-over`).** Removed: the step that filled each crease column back down to the crease row after the mask was worn in (`crease_kept` is gone). Now `--region-crease` ends the mask on the crease before the wear and the wear (`--region-shrink`) wears the whole mask in, the crease row too, like every other edge. So the record's `mask.box` ends `--region-shrink` rows above the crease row (2 rows at the default), as it did before `crease_kept`. New, under `--region-over` only: in every cell the region reaches down to the crease again (`crease_reach`): the crease's rows (its columns and rows on the cell the mask was drawn on; a column between two crease columns takes the nearer one's row, the left one's on a tie) are carried into the cell by the mask's carry there (the column rounded to the nearest, half to even; the row rounded down), and in each crease column where the region holds a pixel, the cell's solid pixels from the region's lowest pixel down to the crease's row join the region. The crease is the picture's own edge, so the layer reaches it in every cell while the wear still wears the mask in everywhere else. Without `--region-over` nothing reaches back down: the worn mask is the region.
- **New: solid under-edges, round rims, figure membranes, and recut strip pairing (`--region-over-solid`, `--region-rim-round`, `--region-membrane`, `--read-on-cut`).** `--region-over-solid` frees only solid pixels (alpha 128 or more) under the lower edge into the band while keeping softer pixels as anchors, and carries the region's soft edge with it under the lower edge. `--region-rim-round` holds the rim round beside the region across the wider of its two widths (`max(Lx, Ly)`), preventing steep downward steps below corners where the lower edge runs lower. `--region-membrane N` moves the figure under the lower edge within N + 1 px as a membrane by solving 4-neighbour figure means in passes instead of conjugate gradients (from a cos² start, at most 1200 passes, leaving no piece out) so the figure stretches rather than slides, drawing membrane pixels by their move and reporting membrane-specific fold refusals. `--read-on-cut STRIP` pairs regions read on another cut of the same clip by matching source frame numbers and applying a whole-pixel shift across crops on unaligned and aligned loops alike. The shift is taken on the frame as filmed, less where each cut's body or motion anchor moved that frame. Each cut is now read by its record of its cells alone: `cell_record` in the strip's record (`<name>.strip.json`), which `video-loop` now writes, bound to the strip by `cell_record.strip_sha256` (the sha256 of the strip PNG), with one entry per cell: `source_frame`, `anchor_move_px` (where the anchor moved that frame before the crop, whole px across and down), `scale` (`size_hold` and `gait_fallback`: the factor each step that holds a clip's frames to one size scaled that frame by, 1 where it did not hold them) and `as_filmed` (true where the cell's pixels at the end of the cut are its pixels anchored before any repair, the receipt `source_projection.before_repair_pixels_sha256` in the cut's report, and no step held the clip's frames to one size). On a loop aligned from its cut it reads the cut's own record, which `video-cycle-align` now keeps beside the cut's frames as filmed (`cycle.source/<name>.strip.json`) and which must be bound to the strip the alignment's origin names (`cycle_align.origin.strip_sha256`). It refuses, no-match, saying which and naming the field: a cut with no `cell_record` (any cut made before this release), a record bound to another strip, a record that does not give its cells as `video-loop` writes them (a field missing or not of its kind, in any cell), a loop aligned from its cut with no kept record, a paired cell a hold scaled or not cut as filmed, and two paired cells that are not the same pixels where they overlap at the shift (a record changed to call a cell as filmed is held to the cell's pixels). The report beside a cut is no longer looked for or read. All four options are off by default and leave strips, frames, and records byte for byte what they were when not given. See [video-pipeline](docs/video-pipeline.md) section 6.
- **New: the membrane's own keys (`--region-membrane-unpin`, `--region-clear-alpha`, `--region-floor-beside`, `--region-own-outline`).** `--region-membrane-unpin` makes what the membrane moves no anchor of the rim either, so the region's move does not fall to none at its first rows under its lower edge. `--region-clear-alpha A` (a whole number from 1 to 127) reads a pixel under alpha A as clear by the sets the region moves and holds and by the membrane's figure while the cell's bytes stay its own, so a speck of dust beside the figure holds nothing. `--region-floor-beside N` sets the lower edge beside the region's columns to the lowest pixel of its columns within N px of that side, not that of its nearest column alone. `--region-own-outline G` grows the region G 4-neighbour steps over the cell's solid pixels within G 4-neighbour steps of a clear pixel before the sets (never under its lower edge's line and never over pixels `--hold-mask` leaves out), moving the outline its carried mask leaves out with the region. All four require `--region-mask`, shape nothing without `--region-membrane` (leaving the same bytes and record, naming none of them), are off by default, and leave strips, frames, and records byte for byte what they were when not given. See [video-pipeline](docs/video-pipeline.md) section 6.
- **Output change: `cell_record` in strip records, `source_projection` for motion anchors, and kept cut strip records.** `video-loop` now writes `cell_record` into every strip record it writes (the fields above). The strip PNG, the GIF, the WebP and the frames of `cycle/` keep their bytes; the strip record gains this one key. A cut's report gains `source_projection` (`recipe`, `wrap_dx_px`, `before_repair_pixels_sha256`) at `--anchor motion` and `--anchor motion-auto` too, with `recipe` `motion-anchor-correction-v1` there; it was written only at `--anchor none` and `body` before (unchanged there). `cell_record` is written in the strip record only, not in the report's copy of it. `video-cycle-align`'s first alignment of a loop now also writes `cycle.source/<name>.strip.json`, the loop's strip record as cut, byte for byte, before it rewrites `<name>.strip.json`; later alignments leave it as it is, and a new cut with `video-loop` removes it with `cycle.source/`. The aligned strip's record does not carry `cell_record` (it is the record of another strip). The pair policy `RECUT_POLICY` is now `same-clip/source-frame/whole-px-shift/2`. At `--out-fps` (3.8.0: cells each made at a time of their own, more cells than the cut has frames), and at `--out-fps` with the air retimed, the report's `held_fill.out_fps` gains `source_frames` (per cell, the frame of the cut it is, or null where it was made), and `cell_record` now reads a retimed cut the same way as a cut at `--out-fps`: `source_frame` is the frame the cell is, and for a cell made between two frames the cut's frame at or before its time (the frame the seam reads it as), with that frame's `anchor_move_px` and `scale`. `as_filmed` is true only where the cell's pixels are that frame's pixels anchored before any repair, so a cell that repeats a frame unchanged is as filmed, and a cell made between two frames, a cell moved onto the parabola or along, and every cell of a cut the retime laid on a larger canvas are not as filmed, and `video-sway --read-on-cut` does not pair them. The cells before the take-off that the retime neither moved nor laid on a larger canvas stay as filmed.
- Python callers: `sway_loop`, `sway_frames`, `read_request` and `read_ask` accept `region_over` (bool; default False) and `region_shear` (float; None: off). New in `sprite_gen.video.sway`: `shear_floor`, `layer_sets`, `lay_over`, `cover_depth`, `still_strip`, `tip_shape` and `move_steps`; constants `OVER_POLICY`, `TIP_COLS`, `TIP_AIR`, `TIP_RING`, `TIP_OFF` and `TIP_SEARCH`; `RegionMask` gains `over`, `shear`, `hold` and `edges()`; `Masked` gains `sheared`, `left_out` and `left_out_in(k)`; `rim_bands` gains `floor` (None: as before).
- Python callers: `sway_loop`, `sway_frames`, `read_request` and `read_ask` accept `region_over_solid` (bool; default False), `region_rim_round` (bool; default False) and `region_membrane` (int; None: off); `sway_loop` and `read_request` accept `read_on_cut` (Path; None: off, refused by `sway_frames`). New in `sprite_gen.video.sway`: `membrane_sets`, `membrane_move(free, figure, own, outline, move, reach)`, `recut_pair`, `recut_ask` and `read_cut`; new in `sprite_gen.video.loop`: `ramp_offset`; new in `sprite_gen.video.motion_anchor`: `laid_at`; constants `OVER_SOLID_POLICY`, `MEMBRANE_POLICY`, `RECUT_POLICY`, `RECUT_DRIFT` and `RIM_ROUND_POLICY`; `RegionMask` gains `over_solid`, `membrane` and `rim_round`; `Request` gains `read_on_cut`; `layer_sets(..., solid_only=False)` gains `solid_only` and returns the new set `below`; `read_on_relation(..., cut=False)` gains `cut`; and `transported_motion(..., paired=None)` gains `paired`. New in `sprite_gen.video.sway`: `cell_record(record, strip_sha256)` (the record of a strip's cells, a ValueError naming what it does not give), `recut_same(pair, cut_cell, cell, read_on)` (refuses two paired cells that are not the same pixels at the shift) and `aligned_from(loop_dir, meta_path, meta)` (the cut's record an alignment kept, or None); `recut_pair(meta, cut, read_on, *, strip_sha256, kept=None)` takes this loop's strip sha256 and the kept record; `read_cut` returns the strip picture under `strip`; `Request` gains `aligned_from`; new in `sprite_gen.video.loop`: constants `CELL_RECORD`, `CELL_SCALE_STEPS` and `MOTION_RECIPE`.
- **Tests**: new file `tests/video/test_sway_region_over.py` covers these cases on synthetic figures (the region test's body and its striped part over the torso's right side and past it over the air, with a dark crease under it on the torso; an arm block beside the part's lower left; a dark outline row under the part where it hangs over the air). Each case first reads what a sway writes with the new options (its cells, its record), so on a release without them the same input fails on what comes out:
  - without the new options nothing of them is read or written (cells and record as before, from Python with them passed off);
  - with `--region-crease` the wear wears the crease in with the rest (the worn mask's lowest row on the torso 2 rows over the crease, its box ending there), nothing under the crease moves, and laid over the figure the region reaches down to the crease again in every cell (its lowest pixel on the crease's row in each crease column);
  - laid over the figure under it, the lower edge moves as much as the part above it does (at gain 2.5 and 1.25), while without `--region-over` the crease pins it;
  - the layer draws the band where it lands, no read of the move taken there, and keeps its bytes elsewhere; the move draws the air (reads are taken there); a read in the air that takes the held figure beside it (the torso's soft side past the band) is walked back, in the air alone and never on the band (4, 9 and 6 reads in three cells, none in the others); nothing outside the region, its soft edge, the air and the band changes; the layer covers no deeper than the band; nothing folds;
  - where the part lifts it shows the figure under it: each pixel there is read at its whole move, none read short, its alpha the source's read there; read wholly on opaque pixels it stays opaque and shows the torso and the crease, not the stripes; beside the torso's soft side one column of the part's, within 2 px of that side, is as soft as its read of that side and the air past it (in 7 of the 12 lifting cells); in the rows the part leaves, its column over the torso's soft side shows thinner than that side in exactly the cells where the part moves toward the torso;
  - its outline against the air goes with it and leaves the air behind, and its outline against the figure (the crease) keeps its bytes;
  - two new cases for `--hold-mask` with `--region-over`: the pixels it names that the region mask covers (a piece in the arm's colour inside the part) are the region's, no anchors, and move with the part (one PNG carried and a folder per cell); the pixels it names that the region mask does not cover keep their bytes in every cell, and on a band (`--band auto` with the same `--hold-mask`) every pixel it names keeps its bytes while the part moves round them;
  - where the part lifts off a piece in the arm's colour right under its lower edge, left out with `--hold-mask`, the part's reads that would take the piece are read from their other pixels (10 in every lifting cell), none of them lifted and all opaque: no hole opens and the piece keeps its bytes;
  - `--region-shear` 0.5 and 0.25 widen the rim to the least it asks, so the drawn move's step across the rim stays within the bound, and less than without it;
  - the still strip is read from the region to its side (beside the arm, the px the mask left short; over the air, 0);
  - the tip is read on the side over the air; a tip moved whole is 0 px off its own shape, its shift found;
  - `move_steps` reads the most step both ways where both pixels are shown;
  - `--frames` gives the strip's cells and the same record;
  - each refusal leaves the loop as it was.
  `tests/video/test_sway_region.py`'s crease case now reads the worn mask's box ending 2 rows over the crease row. `tests/video/test_follow_inspect.py` now names `--region-over` and `--region-shear` among the arguments `video-sway` takes and `video-sway-inspect` does not.
- **Tests**: updates to `tests/video/test_sway_region_over.py` and new file `tests/video/test_sway_read_on_recut.py` cover these cases on synthetic figures (the region tests' body with a striped part over the torso's right side and past it over the air; the read-on runner):
  - over-solid: only solid pixels under the lower edge are free and drawn over; the not-opaque rest stays, an anchor, with bytes kept; the soft edge under the lower edge beside the torso goes with the part, while sliding over without the flag it belongs to the figure;
  - the membrane at reach 3 and 6: under the lower edge within reach + 1 px, never the outline over the band; each pixel's move is the mean of its figure 4-neighbours' to within less than `MEMBRANE_STEP` (what one more pass would move it), and the record names `step_px` and `passes_most`; the seam step down from the part's last row is at most half of the one sliding over; drawn by the move, never laid over;
  - the membrane never moves a piece left out (stops at its sides); without the membrane the layer is as it was;
  - a membrane too short for the move is refused by its name, writing nothing;
  - below the corner of a part whose lower edge falls from its left side, with `--region-rim-round` the move rises down by at most pi/2 of its size over the rim's wider width, and more than that without the flag;
  - the refusals list gains each new option's refusal;
  - without the membrane the four new options shape nothing (the same bytes and record);
  - the four new options' refusals (each without `--region-mask`, and the values `--region-clear-alpha` 0 and 128, `--region-floor-beside` -1, `--region-own-outline` -2) leave the loop as it was;
  - two defect shapes, each red on the engine before this change: a part laid over the figure with a few pixels not opaque (alpha 60 and 30) a few rows under its lower edge over the air and a near-clear speck (alpha 3), with `--region-over-solid`, `--region-membrane 6` and `--region-shear 0.35`: with `--region-membrane-unpin` and `--region-clear-alpha 8` the lower edge over the air moves with the part (lag 0.25 px or under), without them it stays short of the move (0.5 px or more), and under the clear alpha the speck is neither held nor the membrane's; and a part whose outline over the air takes one or two pixels past its mask in a row that changes from cell to cell, with `--band-up 0.4` and `--region-membrane 6`: with `--region-own-outline 2` those pixels are the region's and the side moves by each row's share of the move (lag 0.25 px or under), without it they are anchors and the side bends at a row that changes from cell to cell (0.5 px or more);
  - `--region-floor-beside`: beside a region whose lower edge falls 1 px a column, the lower edge past each side is that of its lowest column within N of that side (directly on `layer_sets`);
  - the frames path gives the strip's cells with `--region-membrane 6`;
  - in new file `tests/video/test_sway_read_on_recut.py`, a cut again at the same frame with a wider crop moves the regions and the mask by the crop's shift and gives the sway on the regions drawn there by hand;
  - a cut again at another start moves the mask onto the cell that is that frame, pixel for pixel, with every pixel outside it kept;
  - a cell elsewhere in either cut that its record calls scaled or not as filmed leaves the pair as it is;
  - a loop aligned from the cut again carries the pair through the alignment; the alignment keeps the cut's strip record byte for byte beside its frames (`cycle.source/<name>.strip.json`), bound to the strip its origin names, and a second alignment keeps it as it was; the aligned strip's record holds no `cell_record`;
  - on a loop aligned from its cut, these are refused, each saying which: a kept record that is gone, a kept record that holds no `cell_record`, a kept record bound to another strip, a paired cell its kept record calls not as filmed, and the read cut's cell drawn between the frames either side with its record changed to call it as filmed;
  - two cuts at `--anchor body` whose ramps move frame 4 by nothing in the first and by -6 px in the second pair that frame where each ramp moved it: shift (0, 0), no drift, the moved mask is the patch where the second cut drew that frame, pixel for pixel; the crops alone set it 6 px apart;
  - two cuts at `--anchor body` whose ramps move no frame (both `wrap_dx_px` 0) pair by their crops alone, as before, the mask landing on the patch there;
  - two cuts at `--anchor motion` (head and torso regions on each cut's first frame) pair the frame where each correction laid it: the shift is the crops' less where the first correction laid that frame plus where the second laid it, not the crops' alone, and the mask lands on the patch there; each cell's `anchor_move_px` is the correction's place and every cell is as filmed; the report's `source_projection` names the motion recipe;
  - `video-loop` records each cell of its strip in the strip's record bound to its PNG: the source frame, the body ramp's move, no hold's scale, as filmed; the record is written beside the strip and not into the report, whose receipt holds one digest per frame;
  - a cell the jump repair gave way (a filmed ghost, no RIFE) is recorded not as filmed, every other cell as filmed;
  - refusals: a frame this loop does not hold, a record with no clip it is cut from, and without `--read-on-cut` the relation's refusal as ever, each leaving the loop as it was;
  - a record of its cells not there or not as `video-loop` writes it is refused naming the field, on either cut: none, not an object, bound to another strip or none, no cells or one too few, a cell's entry, its `source_frame`, `anchor_move_px`, `scale`, and `as_filmed` missing or not of its kind (float, text, boolean, zero, NaN);
  - a paired cell its record calls scaled by the size hold or the gait fallback, or not as filmed, is refused saying which, on either cut;
  - a cell an interpolator made, on either cut, is refused whatever its record says: as `video-loop` records it (not as filmed), and with its record changed to call it as filmed (its pixels are not the other cut's at the shift);
  - a loop aligned from its cut (`video-cycle-align`) named by `--read-on-cut`, and a cut of another clip, are refused, and what the key is refused for leaves the loop as it was;
  - the walk away from the camera of the gait fallback tests, cut by `video-loop` with and without the size hold: the record gives the factor the size hold scaled each cell by, equal to the report's `size_hold.cut_frames`, and calls none of the held cells as filmed;
  - the same walk cut twice by `video-loop` as a walk with the size hold on for one cut, for the other, and for both is refused, leaving the loop as it was, the record of the held cell naming its factor;
  - the same walk held in one cut with every trace of the hold and walk taken out of the report and strip record (the report's `size_hold`, the report's `state`, the report's strip's `state`, the strip record's `state`) is refused by the record of its cells; with the record's factors and `as_filmed` taken out too, refused naming the field; with the strip record's `cell_record` taken out, refused as a record with none; with the record changed to call every cell as filmed, refused by its pixels on either cut;
  - the command line reads the relation `recut`.
  `tests/video/test_follow_inspect.py` now names `--region-over-solid`, `--region-membrane`, `--read-on-cut`, `--region-rim-round`, `--region-membrane-unpin`, `--region-clear-alpha`, `--region-floor-beside` and `--region-own-outline` among the arguments `video-sway` takes and `video-sway-inspect` does not.

## v3.10.1 - A frame made that shows the same picture as a frame beside it is refused; it is made again only on the side away from the picture it showed, or its run kept as filmed

**Upgrading from 3.10.0: Every cut with no held cell, every cut whose frames between are real new pictures, and every `--fill-held off` run give the same cells as 3.10.0.** A run that 3.8.0 to 3.10.0 reported as filled although its frame between repeated a picture beside it is now made at another fraction where one passes, or kept as filmed with a warning. Its `faults` in `held_fill.before.held_runs` list `held`. At `--out-fps` and in an air retimed with `--air-retime`, such cells are now listed in `out_fps.shown_again` or `air_retime.shown_again` with a warning instead of being counted as made. On twelve tests of the suite that run the binary, 3.10.0 runs the binary 146 times and 3.10.1 runs it 188 times because 3.10.0 took frames between that showed a picture beside them again, while 3.10.1 refuses them and makes them again at other fractions. So a cut whose frames between show a picture beside them again asks the binary for more runs, and takes longer, than in 3.10.0.

- **Fix: A frame made that shows the same picture as a frame beside it is refused as `held`, made again only on the side away from that picture, or its run kept as filmed.** In 3.8.0 to 3.10.0, an interpolator that gave back one of the two frames it was handed, or the same picture for two different times, made no frame between at all. The run was recorded `filled: true`, its cells listed in `made`, with no retry and no warning, while poses over cells remained unchanged. A frame made must not read as the same picture as either frame it is made between, nor as the cell delivered just before it; otherwise it receives the fault `held` and is refused. A refused frame is made again at the nearest other fractions of `RETIME_T` within half a cell, but only on the side away from the picture it showed: later where it showed the frame before or the cell before it, earlier where it showed the frame after, and not again where it showed both. Because frames between run from one pose to the next, a fraction nearer a picture it showed shows that picture too. If none passes, the run is kept as filmed with a `warning:` line naming its cells. At `--out-fps` and in an air retimed with `--air-retime`, a cell refused this way is the nearer frame, listed in `shown_again` and named in a `warning:` line. On the synthetic cuts of eight tests that reach the binary through the default fill, 3.10.0 took all 44 frames between it asked for, and 19 showed a picture beside them again. Version 3.10.1 makes 26 cells. Poses over cells are 324/383 as filmed, 341/383 in 3.10.0, and 348/383 in 3.10.1. Runs of the binary on them are 90 in 3.10.0, 124 in 3.10.1, and 214 in a first version that retried a refused frame at every fraction within half a cell on both sides. On a Linux runner with a CPU Vulkan driver, that first version made each of those eight tests run longer than its 120 s limit, so each of them timed out and failed.
- Python callers: New function `held_fill.same_picture(frame, others)`. `held_fill.judge(..., before=)` takes the cell delivered before the frame, and its reading names `shows` (which of `a`, `b` and `before` the frame shows again). Each try the loop report records (`tries`) names `shows` where it showed one.
- **Docs**: [loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md) section 7 documents the rule, the side a refused frame is made again on, their measurements, and the `--out-fps` cells. [video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md) and `SKILL.md` are updated. [release.md](docs/release.md) notes that a test that reaches the binary through a cut's default fill and runs it often enough to pass the default 120 s limit on a CPU's Vulkan driver carries the 900 s limit and pins its runs of RIFE with `rife_runs`.
- **Tests**: `tests/video/test_held_fill.py` (stalled walk with runs of two and three held cells kept as filmed when returning the frame before or after, 24/27 poses/cells, nine frames asked for, pictures matching `--fill-held off`, warnings naming cells 5-6 and 14-16; frame showing both poses refused with no retry; interpolator drawing the half way time making the run of two and trying the second cell of the run of three only later at 0.6667 to 0.8; `--out-fps` 60 from 24 with eight cells listed in `out_fps.shown_again`; synthetic fall refused as `held` at 0.5 and 0.55 and made at 0.6 in changed test `test_a_once_cut_and_a_one_shot_have_their_held_cells_made_too`); `tests/video/test_root_motion.py` (synthetic jump air retimed twice as long with ten cells listed in `air_retime.shown_again` and named in a warning, each tried at 0.5 and then only later). Twelve tests that run the RIFE binary on a synthetic cut (the eight that timed out in the first version: three rows of `tests/video/test_explicit_window.py`, two tests of `tests/video/test_gait_fallback.py`, `tests/video/test_ghost_given_way.py`, one test of `tests/video/test_one_step_cut.py`, `tests/video/test_play.py`; and four more: the step screen test of `tests/video/test_one_step_cut.py`, the checkpoints test of `tests/video/test_wrap_blend.py`, a test of `tests/video/test_once.py`, and a test of `tests/video/test_ghost_screen.py` marked `real_rife` that hands frames to `rife.Rife` itself; the other eleven reach the binary through a cut's default fill) carry `@pytest.mark.timeout(900)` with a comment naming their calls of the binary, and each pins its runs of RIFE (between 6 and 36): eleven through `rife_runs` in `tests/conftest.py` (0 where `rife_located()` finds no binary), the `tests/video/test_ghost_screen.py` test through `Rife.calls`. With no binary found, every count is 0 and the two tests marked `real_rife` skip. On the first version, nine of the twelve fail on the count, with run counts of 18 to 38: the eight, and the checkpoints test of `tests/video/test_wrap_blend.py` (20 against 16). The other three ask for the same runs in both versions. Red first for two test files, `tests/video/test_held_fill.py` and `tests/video/test_root_motion.py`: 10 failed and 46 passed on 3.10.0; 56 passed on 3.10.1. Mutation check over those two files: with the new reading switched off, ten tests fail; without comparison to the cell before, two; without comparison to the frame after, three; without "only later" after the frame before or cell before, eight; without "only earlier" after the frame after, three; with the cell before not counted for the side, two.

## v3.10.0 - Root motion on single-play cuts, their air retimed on a given gravity, length and speed (--air-retime), and a caller-named single play (--cycle once --start --length)

**Upgrading from 3.9.0: a cut played once with a body (`video-loop --cycle once`, `once-return`, `one-shot`, and so `video-set`'s single-play states) gains `root_motion` in its report and strip metadata: each cell's root, what it stands on, and its airs. Its cells and every other key keep their bytes and values. The air is drawn again only when asked (`--air-retime`).**

- **New: root motion on every cut played once.** `root_motion` gives each cell's root (the subject's mass centre, as `inspect-motion`'s `centroid_px`, in cell px), the floor's row (`ground_y`), the runs of cells on the ground or in a contact the caller names (`--contact hand=12:20`, frames of the clip), and each air: its take-off and landing cells, the seconds between them, its apex and the cell it peaks in. With `--meters-per-px M` it adds each root in metres from the first cell's (`root_m`) and each apex in metres. A game reads `root` to place each cell and moves the body on its own physics. `video-loop` prints each air in one line (`air 0.42 s, apex 0.21 m`) and `video-set` puts `loop.air` on each item.
- **New: `video-loop --air-retime gravity=G,air=T,speed=V`** (and `video-set --air-retime`). Each air with its take-off and landing in the cut is given `T` seconds, `G` m/s² and `V` m/s along, any of them, each the clip's own where not given; `G` and `V` need `--meters-per-px`. The air's cells are spread evenly over its new seconds, each the clip's pose at the same share of its air, made with RIFE between two frames where it falls between them (the held-cell fill's frames, judged the same way), and moved whole onto the parabola from the take-off's root to the landing's; the landing and the cells after it move along with it, so a planted foot stays planted. The take-off, the landing and the cells on the ground before it are the frames as filmed. The strip metadata's `air_retime` records each air's seconds and apex before and after, the gravity and speed used, how far along it moved, the cells made and the poses over cells. With no RIFE and a frame to make, the air is cut as filmed and a warning names the motion and the install line. A loop, or a cut with no such air, is cut as filmed and says why. A retimed cut is not restored from its source frames (`video-loop-repair`).
- **New: `video-loop --cycle once --start S --length L`** plays exactly those frames once (`terminal.kind: given`): a move cut out of a longer motion that settles on no pose, such as a vault in a run. Before, a single-play cut refused `--start` and `--length`.
- Python callers: `sprite_gen.video.root_motion` (`parse_retime`, `parse_contacts`, `clip_floor`, `root`, `touching`, `reading`, `read`, `sidecar`, `said`, `apex`, `retime`, `public`); `held_fill.at_times` (a cell at each given time, taken out of `upsample`); `qa.motion.centroid` and `qa.motion.contact_spans` (were `_contact_spans`); `nonloop.on_floor`, `nonloop.GROUND_PX`, `nonloop.given_ending`; `loop.run_loop(..., air_retime=, meters_per_px=, contacts=)`; `batch.run_set(..., air_retime=, meters_per_px=)`.
- **Docs**: [video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md) section 4, "Root motion" (the keys, how a game reads `root`, the mass centre and not a body part, `--cycle once --start --length`, the air retimed, what it takes from sprite-gen); `SKILL.md` (tell the user each air's seconds and apex).
- **Tests**: `tests/video/test_root_motion.py` with `tests/video/jump_inputs.py` (a synthetic jump whose mass centre flies a parabola under a known gravity: the root read within half a pixel, the air's cells, seconds and apex, the ground and a named hand contact; the air doubled peaks at g T²/8 with twice its cells, every cell on the parabola, none shown again, the take-off and the ground before it byte for byte, the landing moved along whole, and through real RIFE (`real_rife`) every between made and judged; the air alone keeps the clip's gravity; no RIFE cut as filmed with the motion named; the six cuts `tests/video/test_held_fill.py` pins keep their pictures as decoded (`tests/picture_digest.py`) and every metadata value but the new key, the keyed clip's file sha read against the run's own files; the refusals; `video-set` hands the handle on).

## v3.9.0 - Provider address environment variables, subscription logins off, and the capabilities verb

**Upgrading from 3.8.0: nothing changes unless you set one of the new variables. Each provider's calls can go to another address (`SPRITE_GEN_OPENAI_BASE_URL`, `SPRITE_GEN_XAI_BASE_URL`, `SPRITE_GEN_ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `SPRITE_GEN_ELEVENLABS_BASE_URL`), `SPRITE_GEN_LOGINS=off` runs every call on its API key, and the new verb `sprite-gen capabilities` prints every verb and argument as one JSON line. Unset, every request goes where it went with the same bytes, and every output keeps its bytes except `sprite-gen --help`, which lists the new verb.**

- **New: provider addresses.** Each of the four providers sprite-gen calls over HTTP has a variable that replaces its documented address (the API root with its version path, `https://api.openai.com/v1`, `https://api.x.ai/v1`, `https://api.anthropic.com/v1`, `https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1`). It is read when each request is made, not at import, so a value set or changed after the engine is imported is followed. Unset, empty or blank is the documented address. It moves every direct call: OpenAI images, edits and vision checks; Grok images, edits and vision checks, the video facing check, the video requests, polls and `video-resume`; Anthropic messages and Message Batches; ElevenLabs sound. A value that is not an `http://` or `https://` address with no query or fragment stops the call naming the variable.
- **New: a URL a provider hands back is fetched through the same address.** With `SPRITE_GEN_XAI_BASE_URL` set, the finished clip is downloaded as `GET <value>/sprite-gen/fetch?url=<the clip's URL, percent-encoded>`, first without a token and once more with the bearer token after a 401 or 403, as before. With `SPRITE_GEN_ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` set, a batch's results file comes the same way with the API key. A relay that forwards the provider's API forwards these too. Unset, the URL is fetched where it points.
- **New: `SPRITE_GEN_LOGINS=off`.** No subscription login is read: the grok login file (and `GROK_HOME`) is not looked at and `XAI_API_KEY` is used, `claude auth status` is not run and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is used, and codex is not usable (`codex login status` is not run; a default codex asks or runs what you chose for a codex that cannot run, naming the variable, and `--provider codex` stops). Each key keeps its rule: set but empty is refused, and a missing key stops the call naming the key and the variable. Unset or empty is `on`, the order as before; any other value is refused.
- **New: `sprite-gen capabilities`.** One JSON line: every verb (domain, description, old names, sub-verbs) and every argument as the CLI's parser holds it (spellings, type, how many values, default, required, choices, groups of which one is given), the providers each verb may call and whether it may be billed (`video-resume` calls xAI and buys nothing), and the provider and login variables. It is read off the parser, so a verb or flag added to the CLI is listed with nothing else written. At this release it is 42,227 bytes, where every `--help` together is 158,857.
- Python callers: new module `sprite_gen.gen.address` (`PROVIDERS`, `base`, `url`, `fetched`, `FETCH_PATH`), `sprite_gen.gen.base.LOGINS_ENV`, `LOGINS_OFF` and `logins_on`, new module `sprite_gen.workflow.capabilities` (`document`, `PROVIDER_CALLS`, `NO_PURCHASE`). Removed: the module constants `xai.API_BASE`, `openai_provider.API_BASE`, `anthropic_api.API_BASE` and `sprite_gen.audio.provider.BASE`, which kept a provider's address where a variable could not move it; `video` no longer copies the xAI address at import.
- **Tests**: `tests/gen/test_provider_address.py` (every call site against a local relay, the address unset, empty and blank, a value changed after import, the relay given the request's bytes, the clip and results file fetched through it, refused values, no module but the registry naming an address and none reading it at import), `tests/gen/test_logins_off.py` (each route on and off, the empty key refusal kept, codex's question and refusals, the access check), `tests/workflow/test_capabilities.py` (the document against the parser and every verb's printed options, a verb or flag added is listed, the providers, the size against every `--help`).

## v3.8.0 - Held cells found and filled with in-betweens by default, and a cut given above its clip's frame rate (--out-fps)

**Upgrading from 3.7.0: `video-loop` (and so `video-set`) now finds neighbour cells of a cut that show the same picture and, by default, makes them again with RIFE's frames between. A cut with no such cell comes out byte for byte as before. A cut with them comes out with its held cells made, its length, beat, first and last cells and wrap unchanged. `--fill-held off` cuts as before. Every cut's report and strip metadata gain `held_fill`.**

- **New: held cells found on every cut.** A frame shown twice or three times in a row (a model that stalls, a rate carried to another by repeating frames, a cut mapped to a higher rate by taking the nearer frame) is read on the analysis thumbnail used to choose the cut and on the frames as cut. A step where at most 0.5 % of the subject's pixels change by more than 0.1 in both readings is the same picture. The report and the strip metadata record `held_fill`: the cells, the poses (the cells less those showing the picture before them again), and the held runs (first cell, how many), before and after. `video-loop` prints `poses/cells` in its summary and `video-set` puts `loop.poses` on each item.
- **New: held cells filled by default (`--fill-held auto`).** Each run's cells are made between the run's first cell and the next pose at their own times (a run of three a third and two thirds of the way), judged as `video-cycle-align`'s frames are (smear, outline, ghost, hole, doubled) and for a part standing still on both sides (a foot planted, a hand on a ledge: its outline must stand where it stood, the fault `contact`). A frame refused is made again at a nearby fraction, else the run stays as filmed with a warning. Kept as filmed: a run longer than three cells (a hold the motion means), the opening and closing pose of a cut played once, a loop's last cells (their next pose is across the wrap), and every run of a cut drawn on twos or threes. In that case, its held-drawings reading (`cycle_drawings`, as `video-cycle-align` reads it) holds 2 or 3 and every run is that long (`held_fill.drawn`): limited animation, as filmed. A clip carried to a higher rate by repeating frames reads a hold too, but its runs are two and three cells by turns, and it is filled. With no RIFE the cells are delivered as filmed, never a failure, and a warning names the motion and `sprite-gen rife install`.
- **New: `video-loop --out-fps F` and `video-set --out-fps F`.** A cut given above its clip's rate is made of frames between at each cell's own time, never frames shown again: a loop keeps its cycle's seconds, a cut played once runs from its first frame to its last. The strip metadata says `out_fps` and `source_fps`, and `source_cut.times` in place of `samples`. With no RIFE the cut is delivered at its own rate with a warning. `video-cycle-align` and `video-stop` refuse such a loop; `video-set` refuses it with the cycle alignment on (pass `--align-cycles off`).
- A cut whose cells were made is read by the seam gate on its rendered cells, is not restored from its source frames (`video-loop-repair`: `source-processing-unsupported`), and `video-cycle-align` reads its drawings on its own cells.
- Python callers: `sprite_gen.video.held_fill` (`same_steps`, `runs`, `reading`, `fill`, `upsample`, `times`, `still_kept`, `judge`, `public`, `said`); `loop.run_loop(..., fill_held=, out_fps=)`; `batch.run_set(..., fill_held=, out_fps=)`.
- **Docs**: [loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md) section 7 (the reading, the fill, the higher rate, the paths that give frames at a higher rate, what it takes from sprite-gen); [video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md) section 4 (the default step: find, fill, read again; poses over cells in one line); `SKILL.md`.
- **Tests**: `tests/video/test_held_fill.py` (synthetic clips and a stand-in interpolator that draws the clip again at the time asked: six cuts with no held cell pinned to their decoded pictures on the engine before the change (commit 886936e); a stalled walk filled at a half, a third and two thirds, every other cell as filmed; `--fill-held off` byte for byte as before; no RIFE delivered as filmed with the motion named; a hold of five kept; a walk drawn on twos kept as filmed, byte for byte as `--fill-held off`; a walk carried from 24 to 60 fps by repeating frames filled to 60 poses in 60 cells; a once and a one-shot cut filled; a planted foot kept still, and a frame between that drags it or cross-fades two poses refused; the ends of a cut played once and a loop's last cells kept; codec-like copies are the same picture and a pixel's move is not; a whole figure moving under a thumbnail pixel is not the same picture; a walk at 60 fps made of 60 poses in 60 cells; a one-shot at 60 fps keeping its first and last frames; no RIFE at its own rate; a rate under the clip's refused; the switches through `video-loop` and `video-set`; restoration, the alignment and a stop refusing what they cannot read).

## v3.7.0 - Breathe row width and height minimum and maximum sizing, and curator breathe editor range inputs

**Upgrading from 3.6.0: a Breathe row's breath can now be sized by the two ends it travels between, across and up, instead of a strength (`width_min`, `width_max`, `height_min`, `height_max`; flags `--width-min`, `--width-max`, `--height-min`, `--height-max`). Every existing sidecar, flag and output keeps its bytes, and so does every normalized sidecar; the curator's breathe editor shows four range inputs in place of the two depth selects.**

- **New: a breath's width and height as minimum and maximum (pixel and smooth).** Each is a share of the original. Width is how much the torso widens at the row where the breath is fullest (pixel: the row where the envelope peaks; smooth: the chest's peak P), negative narrower. Height is how far the head top (pixel) or the chest (smooth) rises, as a share of the body below the neck, negative down. Each lies in -0.20 to 0.20 with minimum at most maximum; a bound left out is 0, and null is left out. A minimum of 0 keeps the breath from going under the original size. The curve's shape and beat (`breaths`, `breath_peaks`, `lag`, `inhale`) are unchanged. In pixel, one gain widens a row by 1 + g and shortens it by 1 / (1 + g), so the top of the curve is (`width_max`, `height_min`) and its bottom is (`width_min`, `height_max`). In smooth, rest is at both minimums and the top of the in-breath is at both maximums, so a minimum other than 0 moves the rest cell off the source.
- A strength converts exactly. A pixel row of `depth` d has height [-0.9009 d, 0.9009 d] (0.9009 is the breath curve's peak, `breathe.WAVE_PEAK`) and width [-a, a], with a = 0.9009 × `depth_x` (or `depth`) × the amplitude norm × the envelope's peak; a smooth row has height [0, `depth`] and width [0, `depth_x`]. Written as minimum and maximum, these bake the same bytes. `sprite-gen breathe` records the row's `range` (`{"width": [min, max], "height": [min, max]}`) in `breathe.json` for that conversion. Pixel rows move whole rows and columns, so the extremes measured on a plate are within one cell of the values written.
- Refused, not silently resolved: a row with both a strength (`depth`, `depth_x`) and minimum or maximum keys, a value out of range or not a number (booleans included), a minimum above its maximum, and minimum or maximum keys on a round chest row. The row strain limit (0.25) is checked at the largest gain the curve reaches, and its refusal names the keys to reduce. The pixel clip refusal now says to reduce the breath's size (depth, or minimum and maximum) where it said depth.
- Python callers: `breathe.WAVE_PEAK`, `breathe.envelope_peak`, `breathe.pixel_gains`, `breathe.breathe_range`, `curation.BREATHE_RANGE_KEYS`, `curation.BREATHE_RANGE_MAX`. `breathe.smooth_lift` is the chest's rise from rest to the top, which `video-sway --breathe` and `--avoid-region` read: (`height_max` - `height_min`) × the basis height for a minimum and maximum row.
- **Curator**: the breathe editor's two depth selects are four inputs (width minimum to maximum, height minimum to maximum, in %, 0.1 % steps, -20 to 20). A strength row shows the range its strength travels; editing one input moves the row to the four keys, the untouched ones carried at their exact values so that axis bakes the same bytes. The inputs are off for a round chest row and, on a pixel strength row, until the anatomy arrives. Loading a sidecar now keeps a strength row's `depth_x`, `axis_x` and `torso_half`, which the first autosave dropped before. A new row inherits a sibling row's minimum and maximum keys as it inherited a depth. The preview mirror bakes minimum and maximum rows byte for byte as the bake does.
- **Tests**: `tests/effects/test_breathe_min_max.py` (synthetic figures: strength rows keep their bytes and normalized sidecar; minimum 0 is never narrower nor taller on any row or phase; the written extremes are what the plate measures, within one cell; a strength's range written as minimum and maximum bakes its bytes; smooth from 0 is the old smooth; refusals and the strain limit), new cases in `tests/effects/test_breathe_js_mirror.py` (minimum and maximum parity, `breatheRange`), the value-shape matrix over the four keys and the kept `depth_x` and band in `tests/effects/test_breathe_sidecar_roundtrip.py`, and the mirrored constants in `tests/effects/test_breathe_reference_key.py`.

## v3.6.0 - Two step walk and run loop cuts with half step warnings, loop search past untracked anchor frames, body turn loop exclusion, round chest breathing with sideways arms, and sway region mass movement

**Upgrading from 3.5.0: `video-loop` walk and run cuts change for two-step cuts and half steps under `--anchor motion-auto`, opening frames the motion anchor cannot track, and body turns, while new `sprite-gen breathe` and `video-sway` options are off by default.** In `video-loop`, a walk or run cut shorter than its state's gait floor, its steps not counted, is now cut two steps long under `--anchor motion-auto` where the clip as filmed comes back nearer two lengths on than one, and says so where it does not (`cycle.half_step` and a warning line); every other cut keeps every byte. A walk or run whose first frames the automatic motion anchor cannot track is now searched after them, as after a lead-in, where it was refused; every clip the anchor tracks from its first frame keeps every cut and byte. A clip whose cut held a turn is now cut where a window without the turn holds a cycle, while clips with no turn, clips that are not read, and clips with a turn and no turn-free cycle under `warn` keep every cut and byte. A pixel Breathe row can now breathe round from the chest, with the arms moving sideways (`chest_round`, `arms_x`), and `sprite-gen breathe` takes cells held still (`--hold-mask`), what the arms hold (`--props-mask`), pieces held behind the body (`--props-behind-mask`), and props grouping (`--props-groups`). All are off by default: every existing sidecar, flag and output keeps its bytes, and so does every normalized sidecar. `video-sway` can move each region as one mass with `--band`. When `--band` is left out, strips, frames, and records remain byte for byte what they were. Passing `--band PX|auto` moves each region's ellipse and an outer margin at full weight, with the weight falling to 0 by cos² over a band of pixels around it, while carrying the region by its own pixels rather than the body's outline lay. The fifth region number `flat` and `--stretch-floor` are refused when `--band` is specified. In the strip's metadata, `follow` gains `band` (and `sway.json` gains `band` for `--frames`), while stdout JSON gains `band` with `band_px`, `margin_px`, and `lay_cells`. The legacy command name `video-follow` accepts the same flags. `video-sway --band` can now hold pixels with `--hold-mask`, leaving them as they are while regions move round them. It is off by default, and when not given, strips, frames, and records remain byte for byte as before. `video-sway` can now move a region's own pixels with `--region-mask`, holding every visible pixel outside it and its soft edge while the weight falls over an inner rim read off the move. It is off by default, and when not given, strips, frames, and records remain byte for byte what they were (with `--band`, with `--band` and `--hold-mask`, and with neither).

- **New: `video-loop` names a half-step cut.** A walk or run loops on an even number of steps (two, one cycle). The half-period guard reads the gait floor (`LoopProfile.min_seconds`) in whole frames, rounded, so a run cut 8 frames long at 24 fps (0.33 s, under the run's 0.35 s) is taken as two steps. Such a periodic cut whose steps nothing counted (no `cycle.steps`) is kept, recorded in `cycle.half_step` (`rule`, `state`, `seconds`, `gait_floor`, `why`, `two_steps`; in the stdout summary too) and named in a warning line that suggests `--steps 1`; the line repeats no refusal's words. Python callers: `loop.half_step`, `loop.half_step_verdict`, `loop.HALF_STEP_RULE`. Docs: [video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md) section 4. **Tests**: `tests/video/test_loop_half_step.py`.
- **Changed: `video-loop --anchor motion-auto` cuts such a cut two lengths long where the clip says it holds two steps.** One step on, the legs are back in place with near and far swapped, and where they are drawn alike the silhouette repeats; the body's turn, the shading across the torso and the folds of the clothes come back only two steps on. Each length of [2L − 1, 2L + 1] is cut as `--steps 1` cuts it, and about each cut's wrap (two frames either side of its first) the frames as filmed are read two lengths on over one length on; the nearest at most 0.85 (`period.STEP_RATIO_MAX`, the step screen's bound) whose seam and seam pop gates pass is taken, recorded `cycle.steps` `{"count": 2, "by": "two-lengths", ...}` and `cycle.two_lengths` (`first_cut`, `tried`, `cut`, `taken`; in the stdout summary too). It is read on the frames as filmed, not on the search's distances, which move every frame by one vertical slope fitted over the clip: a slope tilted by frames that open higher moves frames two steps apart further than one step apart where the body runs in place, so two lengths on read no nearer there. A cut the clip gives no two is kept and named as above, the warning line saying why (`cycle.half_step.two_lengths`). Python callers: `loop.two_lengths_on`, `loop.TWO_LENGTHS_AROUND`. **Tests**: `tests/video/test_loop_two_lengths.py`.
- **Fixed: `video-loop --anchor motion-auto` no longer refuses a walk or run for its opening frames alone.** Where `auto_motion.discover` finds no two regions through the whole clip (`automatic motion anchor needs two separated regions trackable throughout the clip`, or `found no stable textured region`), the analysis is read again on the clip after the fewest frames it opens on, fewer than the window's shortest cut, with which the anchor finds its two regions through the rest (`auto_motion.analyse_after_opening`, new exception `auto_motion.RegionsUnfound`). A clip filmed from a still opens on that still and on the standing pose the body sets off from, and the regions the middle of the clip shows may be out of the anchor's reach there; the refusal came before the search, so the gait fallback never ran. Those frames are left out of the search as a lead-in is (`lead_in.search_from`, every frame number the clip's own), recorded in the report's new `untracked_opening` (`frames`, `first`, `shorter_than`, `refused`) and, once a cut is found, a warning line `video-loop: warning: the automatic motion anchor found its two regions from frame <k> on, not from the clip's first frames: ...` that does not repeat the refusal's words. Where no such opening leaves the clip tracked, it is refused in the same words as before, followed by ` — nor through the clip after any of its first 1 to <n> frames left out (an opening shorter than the shortest cut, <n + 1> frames)`; where the cut the search took holds no two regions when its frames are read again for the correction, the refusal now ends ` — on the cut the search took (frames <a>-<b>)`. Tests: `tests/video/test_auto_motion_opening.py` (a synthetic walker whose first three frames carry its head and torso 90 px forward: refused as filmed before, now cut after them with every frame number the clip's own; the same walker without them unchanged; the lean again mid-clip refused with the new words).
- **New: `video-loop` detects torso turns in walk and run clips and avoids them during the cut search.** A video model asked to walk at the still's angle sometimes turns the body inside the clip: a back-diagonal walk turns to a full back view half way and back. A loop cut across the turn plays the body swinging round. Until now the engine had no reading of this: the cut search could not avoid it, and a clip that held no cycle for it was refused only as `video-loop: no periodic cycle found`, so an app could not name that the body turned as the reason to film it again. The new module `sprite_gen/video/body_turn.py` introduces reading `torso-side-v2` on every walk or run under `--anchor motion-auto`, reading each frame's torso crop against the clip's first frame, the still the clip was filmed from, a standing pose. It does so whether or not the clip opens on a lead-in (the video model reframing the still to the walk's size over the first frames, which the cut search leaves out). It does not read against the first frame after the lead-in: that frame is a stride's pose, and a walk draws the mirror of a stride's pose half a cycle later, so a synthetic walk that does not turn, read against it, read a turn every stride (least -0.34). Read against the still filmed small, the same synthetic walk (its first 8 frames filmed from 0.6 of its size) reads +0.87 at least where it does not turn (+0.92 without the lead-in); turning over frames 30 to 40, its turn over frames 32 to 38, the same frames as without the lead-in, least -0.71 (-0.63 without the lead-in). The torso crop runs from the neck down half the body below it, centred on the head's centre, as wide as the first frame's torso. Each crop is cut about the head's axis between pixels, and the still and every frame are read at their nearest about the axes within `GUARD` (2.5 px) and `TOL` (2 px), `STEP` (0.25 px) apart, so a piece beside the head that moves its centre 1.4 px moves no turn. Under the module constant `MASS_SHARE` = 0.01, the box's top is read as the row with more than a hundredth of the frame's solid pixels above it, and the still's torso width is read about the head's centre from the columns of the still's torso rows with more than a hundredth of those rows' solid pixels beside them. Reading them by mass prevents a piece apart from the body from moving the box or the torso width. The box's bottom is the frame's last solid row, the one end of the box not read by mass: where the legs cross each stride they lay their pixels over each other, and a bottom read by mass (the row with a hundredth of the frame's solid pixels under it, as the top is read) rises with them (on the lead-in walker, a row over its last solid row in most frames and 3 rows over where the legs cross, moving the crop's rows with it), though read either way, the walkers turning over frames 30 to 40 and the 4-frame turns drawn from 8 places, with the lead-in and without it, read their turns over the same frames. Under `HEAD_CORE` = 0.5, the head's centre is read as the middle of the head band's columns that hold at least half the solid pixels of the band's fullest column, before being read again within `HEAD_REACH` as before. This stops a fleck or a cut-loose ornament beside the head from shifting the head's centre and falsely reading a turn across the clip or hiding the walk's turn. The neck is the narrowest row of the first frame between 10 % and 55 % of its height (the topmost on a tie, or row 0.11 on a hooded walker). Each frame's `side` correlation is `(v.s - v.m) / (1 - s.m)`, where each correlation is taken at its nearest about the axes within `TOL`. Under module constants `SIDE_SHOWN_MIN` = 0.08, `TURN_SIDE_MAX` = 0, and `TURN_RUN_MIN` = 4 consecutive frames, candidate windows whose cut holds a frame of a turn are refused with `the body turns (clip frames a-b)`, two-step cuts holding one are left out (`step_count.turned_out`), and held-side windows two lengths long are excluded. A new bound, `HEAD_REACH` = 0.3, re-reads the head's centre (the middle of the solid pixels of the top 15 % of the body) on the pixels within `HEAD_REACH` of the body's height of it until it settles, leaving the plain mass centre as before where no pixel of those rows lies further out. Clips without turns (or unread) are searched once as before with the same cut and bytes. A walk that reads square against a still that shows a side is not read either and is cut the same way, with a warning line (`WALK_SIDE_MIN`). Where no window without the turn holds a cycle, `--body-turn warn` (the default) reruns the cut search as before and keeps the cut with a warning line naming the turn, or refuses as before (`video-loop: no periodic cycle found`) after a warning line if no cut holds a cycle; while `--body-turn refuse` (requiring `--anchor motion-auto`) prints `video-loop: the body turns in the walk` as its first line, prescribes filming it again with the torso and hips kept at the still's angle, hands over nothing, and sets `status: failed`, supporting app retake rules such as `loop-body-turned`.
