The loop follows rife-ncnn-vulkan's `RIFE::process_v4` (MIT, notice in NOTICE). `SPRITE_GEN_RIFE_DEVICE` names `cuda` (default) or `cpu`. `SPRITE_GEN_RIFE_TTA` means the same for both backends. The default augmentation stays `-x -z`. To install: `pip install "sprite-gen[torch]"` (new extra: `torch>=2.13.0,<3`). Then run: `sprite-gen rife install --backend torch` (new flag; defaults to `SPRITE_GEN_RIFE_BACKEND`). This unpacks the model, and makes a check frame through PyTorch on `SPRITE_GEN_RIFE_DEVICE`. It reads the model only, so a Linux ARM machine the binary does not run on is not refused. A CPU-only host installs PyTorch's CPU build first: `pip install torch==2.13.0 --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cpu` (192 MB, against the CUDA build's 527 MB plus its CUDA libraries). Nothing falls back. With torch chosen the binary is never looked for or run. `cuda` where PyTorch finds no CUDA is refused naming why (a CPU-only PyTorch, or a CUDA that finds no GPU) and does not run on the CPU. `mps` (Apple's GPU) is refused (no lane measures it; on a Mac the binary runs on the GPU already). A backend or device named wrong is refused. A device that fails as the network is put on it or as a frame runs (out of its memory, its driver or CUDA failing) is a RIFE that fails, in PyTorch's own words: the loop fails with its record, and the install's check frame fails. PyTorch not installed, or no model where the backend looks (`SPRITE_GEN_RIFE_MODEL`, else the install root), is RIFE not installed, as the binary missing is. Under `--repair auto` the loop is cut as filmed with the warning, and its record names the torch install line. On CUDA each frame runs with cuDNN deterministic, its benchmark off and convolutions in IEEE float32 (no TF32). The same frame twice is the same bytes on one GPU, driver and PyTorch. What made a frame is the RIFE's identity: backend, model bytes loaded, network code, PyTorch, on CUDA the GPU, driver, CUDA, cuDNN and those settings; on the CPU its instruction set. Because of this identity, `--between-cache` never hands one backend's or GPU's frame to another. Measured on synthetic walkers: Seven walker pairs (clean, outline lost, ghost, torn, a cross-fade to look at) get the same verdicts from the binary on an Apple GPU, the binary on Mesa llvmpipe and the torch backend on a CPU. The torch frame lies 0.005 to 0.052 of 255 on average from the binary's Apple GPU frame. The binary's own llvmpipe frame lies 0.007 to 0.25. A 960 x 960 frame of a synthetic walker with `-x -z`: On an Apple M4 Max CPU (12 threads), 3.3 to 3.6 s; the binary on that Mac's GPU takes 1.27 to 1.37 s. In a Modal container of two CPUs (the app's job size), 22.6 to 28.5 s on two threads; the binary on Mesa llvmpipe in the same container takes 127.7 to 131.5 s. On an NVIDIA T4 rented from Modal (CUDA), 1.36 to 1.41 s. The torch backend's frames are not the binary's bytes, which is one reason the binary stays the default. [loop-repair](docs/loop-repair.md) section 1
- **New: a host with no GPU sends the torch backend's frames to a GPU elsewhere (`SPRITE_GEN_RIFE_EXECUTOR`).** The host names a send of its own, `SPRITE_GEN_RIFE_EXECUTOR=module:attr`: a function from a request's bytes to its answer's. It reaches `sprite_gen.video.rife_torch.serve` on the GPU's machine, the same sprite-gen with PyTorch and the model installed there. The engine knows nothing of what carries the bytes, and the host needs no PyTorch. A frame is one call (its two runs made there), and a read is one call (its two runs and sixteen passes, the passes reduced there to how far they disagree). A RIFE asks the executor what it is once, as it is made (the probe, which takes the cold start), so a `video-loop` makes at most 53 calls. The RIFE holds that answer for its life, and every request carries it. An executor that is not it any more (another GPU, driver or image) makes nothing, and the RIFE fails naming what differs (`gpu: Tesla T4 -> NVIDIA L4`). An answer from another identity is not handed on. So one RIFE never hands on frames of two GPUs, and a frame taken from `--between-cache` is the frame it would make; the next RIFE asks again and keys its frames anew. Refused: an executor named with the binary's backend or with `SPRITE_GEN_RIFE_DEVICE` (the device is the executor's), a send that cannot be imported, and an executor whose `rife.py` or protocol is another (run the same sprite-gen on both sides). A send that raises, an answer that does not read (not the format, damaged or cut short on the way, or not the frame's size) and the executor's own failure are each a RIFE that fails, in its words; nothing is asked again or run elsewhere. The host names one GPU type for the executor (no fallback list) and does not deploy it anew while a run goes on. Requests and answers are a compressed NumPy archive, read with `allow_pickle=False`, each member whole so its CRC-32 is checked before its array is read: an answer damaged on the way hands on no other pixels. Reports name `executor` and the executor's `device` and `gpu`; the overlapped wrap counts `runs` and `executor_calls`. Measured on an NVIDIA T4 rented from Modal, from a host of two CPUs without PyTorch: a 960 x 960 `-x -z` frame of a synthetic walker takes 1.49 to 1.52 s, 0.1 to 0.2 s more than in the T4's own process, a read as long, and the frames and readings are the same bytes as there. [loop-repair](docs/loop-repair.md) section 1
- **Lane: `scripts/linux_lane.sh` tests PyTorch 2.13.0 CPU after the suite.** After the suite, PyTorch 2.13.0 CPU is installed into the same environment. It runs `sprite-gen rife install --backend torch`'s check frame. It runs every test marked `torch_rife` with `SPRITE_GEN_RIFE_DEVICE=cpu`, with none skipped. `--rife-only` runs the same. `--rife-unmeasured` lets `torch_rife` tests skip as it lets `real_rife` ones. CUDA is not measured by this lane.
- **Lane: `scripts/t4_lane.py` tests the torch backend on an NVIDIA T4, rented from Modal.** `modal run scripts/t4_lane.py` builds the checked-out commit's tree into an image as the Linux lane installs a clean clone: Python 3.14, PyTorch 2.13.0 from PyPI (its Linux wheel is the CUDA 13 build), the tree with `.[dev]`, and the model by `sprite-gen rife install --backend torch`, where the engine looks for no binary. On one T4 it makes the install's check frame on CUDA and runs every test marked `torch_rife` and not `real_rife` on CUDA, with none skipped. `--whole-suite` first runs the whole suite in that environment, PyTorch in it, as the Linux lane's RIFE-less run does. A commit passes the lanes where the Linux lane passed at it and one T4 lane run at it exited 0. [release](docs/release.md)
- **Tests**: `tests/video/test_rife_torch.py` (the eight ways undo themselves and are the single passes' eight turns; flows of a textured patch run alone each of the sixteen ways and turned back point alike, where a wrong sign, a transpose's x/y, or a backward swap puts one elsewhere; `-z` and `-x` give the same frame the other way in time and flipped; the install check frame's disc lands half way; the model loads strict and other bytes are refused by SHA-256; the same frame twice is the same bytes and the identity names what makes it; seven recorded synthetic walker pairs in `tests/fixtures/rife-walkers` are judged as the binary judged them with no binary, the frame within twice the binary's own GPU-to-GPU gap, and alike to this machine's binary where both run; a device that fails as the network is put on it or as a frame runs (out of its memory, its driver, CUDA not starting, a CUDA call deferred to its start) is a RIFE that fails in PyTorch's words, a TypeError there is raised as it is, a loop whose device fails as the network is put on it fails with its record, and the install's check frame fails; without PyTorch: torch chosen and not installed is RIFE not installed and the binary is never run, a loop's record names it, a backend or device named wrong is refused before anything is looked for, cuda where there is none is refused, a model not the pinned one is refused; with a runner stood in: reports name rife-torch and count runs, the loop and the alignment name the torch backend, and a frame kept by one runner is not taken for another); `tests/video/test_rife_executor.py` (a frame is one call and a read one call, and the RIFE probes once as it is made, their bytes the same device's in this process with this host's augmentation; every request names the engine code and the identity held; the host runs without PyTorch; the identity is the executor's as the RIFE was made; a T4 frame kept is the RIFE's own after the executor turns L4, which it then refuses to make anew, and the next RIFE misses and keeps the L4 frame under its own key; an executor whose GPU, driver, network code, weights, PyTorch, cuDNN or determinism changed makes nothing, the field named; an answer from another identity is neither handed on nor kept; another container of the same identity runs on, and the next RIFE takes every frame with its probe alone; a T4 and L4 pool is refused at the first L4 request; a frame made while this host's augmentation changed is not kept; other engine code or another protocol is refused before anything loads; a request that does not read, is pickled or was damaged on the way is answered so, in the reading's words; a send, an answer or an executor that fails is a RIFE that fails, in its words; an answer damaged in a member's local header, a compressed block, an array's header or its own bytes, or its end record, or cut short, is a RIFE that fails in the words of what reading it raised, which is its cause, and undamaged is the frame made here; each byte of the probe's, a frame's and a read's answer damaged in turn fails the RIFE or reads as it was made, nothing else raised and no other pixels handed on; a reading that cannot read what `pack` writes raises as it is, a fault of the code; an answer that is not bytes does not read; an executor named with the binary, with a device, or not there is refused and the binary never runs; a loop names the executor, and a loop whose executor's answer was damaged on the way fails with its record (`failed`, in the decompressor's words, the gait fallback's frames removed); marked `torch_rife`: `rife_torch.serve` makes the frame and the read of the backend in this process, loaded once); `tests/video/test_rife_install.py` (a zip whose model is not the pinned one installs nothing for both backends; `--backend torch` installs on a machine the binary does not run on and checks its frame through torch); `tests/release/test_lane_rife_collection.py` (a test RIFE decides carries `real_rife` or `torch_rife`); `tests/release/test_t4_lane.py` (the T4 lane installs the PyTorch and the Python the Linux lane does); new pytest mark `torch_rife` (skipped where PyTorch, the model or the named device is missing).

## v3.2.0 - Cycle alignment hole and doubled faults, and loop repair for size held cuts

**Upgrading from 3.1.0: `video-cycle-align` now gives way to the nearer source frame on made frames with enclosed limb holes under `--between auto`, changing only alignment outputs with such holes while made frames without them and all outputs from `video-loop`, `video-loop-compare`, and `video-loop-repair` keep their bytes.**

- **New: `video-cycle-align` now reads a new `hole` fault on frames RIFE makes between source frames and gives way to the nearer source frame.** On every frame RIFE makes between two source frames, `video-cycle-align` reads a new fault, `hole` ("a hole through a limb"). Under `--between auto` (the default) a made frame with this fault gives way to the nearer source frame, like the other faults (`smear`, `outline`, `ghost`); under `--between rife` the frame is kept and named in the warnings. `video-set --align-cycles auto` runs the same alignment. RIFE runs a frame's colour and its coverage as two separate runs. Where a step opens a gap between the legs in one source frame and not the other, the coverage run can carry that gap into a leg the colour run keeps whole: the made frame shows a transparent band through the leg with the leg's fill on both sides. Nothing is added (no `smear`), there is no part coverage (no `ghost`), and the leg's own fill is the only line round the band. The fault `outline` needs both outline readings: the dark line (`outline_loss`) counts a fill darker than 70/255 (dark tights, a boot) as outline, and the rank reading (`outline_shift`) reads the band's edge as lost only where it is 50/255 lighter than the source frames' lines at the same rank, which light lines elsewhere on the art (fur, hair drawn without a dark line) can hide; so such a frame passes every fault and is delivered. The reading `rife.hole(made, a, b, t)` measures pixels under solid (alpha under 0.5) that the outside of the picture cannot reach without crossing its solid body (enclosed holes). A pixel of a made frame's hole is the source frames' own when either source frame is under solid within 4 px (`rife.HOLE_REACH`, a square neighbourhood) of where that pixel lay at that frame's time, read in place (the background, still, and a gap kept, or opened by a step where the frame that shows it open has it) and carried with the whole figure along the motion the two source frames show for the whole figure. Where a made frame sits at fraction `t` of the way from source frame a to source frame b, a pixel of it lay in a at `t` of the motion back and lies in b at `1 - t` of it on (rounded so the two are the whole motion apart). The motion is measured only from the two source frames, never from the made frame and never from RIFE's own flow: over the box round all three frames' solid bodies, each pixel is read in a where it lay and in b where it lies for a candidate motion; the motion whose mean absolute difference of premultiplied colour and coverage over the box is least is the motion there, ties going to the smaller motion. It is followed up to 20 % of the body's height each way (`rife.HOLE_TRAVEL`, 0.2), coarse to fine: the whole range on a level pooled in square blocks of a power of two, the smallest for which the range is at most 24 px each way (`rife.HOLE_COARSE`, 24), then within 2 px of twice the coarser motion on each finer level, down to the pixel. A hole those two readings leave is the source frames' own, whatever carried it (a part moving over a body that stays, plain or patterned, turning, growing, sheared or swinging), where it is one hole of each source frame. A hole is an 8-connected piece of a frame's enclosed pixels: the made frame's count where they keep a pixel opened by a 5 px square, a source frame's where they keep one opened by a 3 px square (`rife.HOLE_SOURCE_THICK`, 3). Each is read for its area, its centroid, its shape (the logarithms of its pixels' two principal second moments, each plus 1/12, over its area, which turning and growing keep) and the colour round it (the mean premultiplied colour of the frame's solid pixels 5 to 8 px out). The two source frames' holes: areas within 4 times of each other (`rife.HOLE_PAIR_AREA`, 4.0), shapes within 2 times on each moment (`rife.HOLE_PAIR_SHAPE`, 2.0), colours within 0.12 on each channel (`rife.HOLE_COLOUR`, 0.12), centroids at most sqrt(2) times the reach and 4 px apart. The made frame's hole: area within a quarter of their range (`rife.HOLE_AREA`, 0.25), shape within 1.3 times of theirs (`rife.HOLE_SHAPE`, 1.3), colour within 0.12 of each, and centroid within 4 px and 0.1 of their distance (`rife.HOLE_PATH`, 0.1) of the point at `t` on the line between theirs. Pairs are taken one to one: no hole of any frame is in two pairs taken, so each source frame's hole explains one of the holes paired. A hole of the made frame that the first two readings give the source frames takes no pair, being theirs already, and the pairing reads only the holes those readings leave: of the sets of pairs that can be taken together, the set taken pairs the most holes, and of those, has the least summed cost (the same frames always giving the same set), so alike holes moving together each keep their own two. Pairs sharing a hole form a group, read apart: an exact search first asks whether one set pairs every hole of the group, cutting branches where the holes left cannot each keep a hole of each source frame of their own; where it finds one, every hole of the group is paired, and where there is none, a second exact search finds the set pairing the most. Each search takes at most 5000 steps per group (`rife.HOLE_PAIR_STEPS`, 5000): where the first runs past them, the second runs; a group whose second search runs past them pairs none of its holes, and the frame gives way to the nearer source frame. The made frame is never used to measure a motion, and RIFE's flow is never read. The motion is read only on frames that keep a hole after the in-place reading, and the pairing only on frames that keep one after the whole figure's motion; a frame with no hole in place is read with no further cost. The regions at least 5 px thick (`rife.HOLE_THICK`, opened by a 5 px square, so a pinhole or an antialiased crevice a pixel or two wide is not counted) are taken after each step, as a fraction of the made frame's solid pixels, or 0 where the frame has no solid body. The fault `hole` is any reading over 0 (`interpolation_quality.HOLE_WARN`, 0.0). A band cut through a leg's edge, reaching the outside, is not an enclosed hole and is left to the outline readings as before.
- Report fields: each row of a loop's `smear` list in the `video-cycle-align` report (and in `cycle_align` in `strip.json`) gains `hole`. A frame given way for it carries `faults: ["hole"]` and `method: "nearest"`, and gets a line in `warnings`; the line says the frame opened a transparent hole inside its outline over the given share of its body, at least 5 px thick, where neither source frame beside it has one, a gap through a limb, and that the nearer source frame was taken there. The `--between` help says the same.
- Python callers: new `rife.hole(made, a, b, t)`, `rife.HOLE_TRAVEL` (0.2), `rife.HOLE_COARSE` (24), `rife.HOLE_REACH` (4), `rife.HOLE_THICK` (5), `rife.HOLE_SOURCE_THICK` (3), `rife.HOLE_PAIR_AREA` (4.0), `rife.HOLE_PAIR_SHAPE` (2.0), `rife.HOLE_AREA` (0.25), `rife.HOLE_SHAPE` (1.3), `rife.HOLE_PATH` (0.1), `rife.HOLE_COLOUR` (0.12), and `rife.HOLE_PAIR_STEPS` (5000); new `interpolation_quality.HOLE_WARN` (0.0); `interpolation_quality.faults(measure)` returns `"hole"` where the measure carries a `hole` reading over `HOLE_WARN`, and measures without a `hole` key are judged as before.
- Output changes: an alignment whose made frame has an enclosed hole 5 px thick or more that neither source frame beside it has within 4 px, in place, carried by the whole figure's motion, or paired with one hole of each source frame as above, now delivers the nearer source frame at that cell instead of the made frame, and counts it in `replaced` and `nearest_at` rather than `made_by_rife` and `made_at`. Made frames without such a hole, and frames whose holes the source frames have, in place, carried or paired, are delivered as in 3.1.0 with the same bytes; the report gains `hole` on every made frame. Synthetic numbers (the figure of the tests below): the frame between with a band 8 px wide and 40 px tall through its far leg reads `outline_loss` 0, `outline_shift` 11.3 % (so no `outline` fault, which needs both over 5 %), `ghost` 0, and `hole` 2.69 %; in 3.1.0 it is kept, now the nearer source frame is taken. The frame drawn between, the same with the arm's gap moved 3 px, and the same with a pinhole 3 px wide through the leg read `hole` 0 and are kept. A frame whose hole both source frames have, carried by a part (moving over a body that stays, plain or patterned, turning, growing, sheared or swinging within reach), is delivered as in 3.1.0 with the same bytes. Figure 5: a body that stays, patterned, with a part on it and a hole through the part's middle, the part moved from one source frame to the next, each frame between drawn exactly at its place. (i) The checkpoint validator's frame: a body 200 by 180 px with stripes 3 px wide every 6 px (a lighter colour by 40 levels), on a 260 by 220 px canvas, a part 24 px square with a hole 10 px square, moved 32 px across: the in-place reading leaves the hole whole, 100 pixels or 0.28 % of the body, and the whole figure's motion is 0; it is one hole of each source frame, alike and on its way, so it is paired: `hole` 0, and both made cells are kept as made, byte for byte. (ii) A body 240 by 260 px on a 300 by 300 px canvas in five patterns (stripes 3, 2 and 6 px wide every 6, 4 and 12 px running up and down; stripes 3 px wide every 6 px running across; checks 4 px square) at contrasts 15, 40 and 120, parts 24, 40 and 60 px square with holes 10, 17 and 25 px square: moved 32 and 48 px across and diagonally at t 0.5 (the moves listed in the test), these frames, and the same three parts moved 16, 32 and 48 px across and diagonally at t 0.25 and 0.75 over the 3 px stripes at contrast 40, every one read `hole` 0. Resampled to eight frames (t 0.25, 0.5 and 0.75 each way), parts of 24, 40 and 60 px moved 32 px diagonally and the 24 px part moved 48 px over stripes at contrast 120 keep every made frame, byte for byte. Figure 6: a torso 120 by 260 px that stays, patterned in blocks 4 px square each lighter by its own whole number up to 200 levels, with an arm beside its right edge: the upper arm and the forearm lie along that edge and are joined by the arm's outer side, so the gap between arm and torso, 16 by 12 px, has the torso's edge on its left and the arm on its other three sides; the arm reaches 48 px above and 52 px below the gap and moves 40 px down from one source frame to the next. The gap lies where the arm covers it in the other source frame, so the in-place reading leaves it, and the whole figure's motion is 0; it is one gap of each source frame, alike and on the arm's way, so it is paired: `hole` 0 at t 0.25, 0.5 and 0.75, and resampled to eight every made frame is kept, byte for byte. A frame with a gap only one source frame opens, on a part moving over a body that stays, plain or patterned, which neither the in-place reading nor the whole figure's motion carries and which has no hole in the other source frame to pair with, gives way to the nearer source frame where 3.1.0 kept it. The boundary, pinned by a test: a part 30 px square moving 32 px across a body that stays, a hole 14 px square open through it in one source frame and closed in the other, 8 px square in the frame between (half open): over a plain body and over the 3 px stripes alike it reads `hole` 0.10 % (its 64 pixels) and both made cells are given way to the nearer source frame, with `faults: ["hole"]`. Figure 7: the plate of the tests (a body 240 by 260 px that stays, striped or plain, with a plate on it and a gap through the plate, 11 by 31 px, each frame between drawn exactly at its place). The third checkpoint validator's frame, the plate turning 60 degrees as it moves 48 px over the stripes, reads `hole` 0 and is delivered as in 3.1.0, both made cells byte for byte, resampled to four and to eight; so are the plate turned 90 degrees, grown 2 times and sheared by 0.6 as it moves 48 px, and swung 45 degrees, over the stripes, and 125 such frames read `hole` 0. A frame between with a hole unlike both source frames' (the gap drawn 16 by 40 px: `hole` 1.04 %; drawn 18 by 18 px: 0.52 %; the plate growing 2.2 times as it moves 48 px, the source frames' gaps more than 4 times apart in area: 0.55 %), off its way (the plate drawn 12 px across it: 0.55 %; drawn where it is at t 0.8 in a frame made at t 0.5: 0.30 %), or out of reach (the plate swung 60 degrees, its centre moving 100 px, where 77.5 px is let through on this body: 0.55 %; the plate moving 80 px: 0.55 %) gives way to the nearer source frame with `hole` among its faults. Figure 8: the plate 120 by 40 px with a row of four holes 8 px square, 24 px apart, moving 32 px down: the frame between drawn exactly, and with the row a pixel taller, read `hole` 0 and are delivered as in 3.1.0; with one hole more (midway between two, between the last two, past the end, or one of the row moved to a midway) it reads 0.10 % and gives way to the nearer source frame. Figure 9: on the same striped body, a plate with a row of two, three or four holes 8 px square, 12 px apart, moving on a curved way, int(40 t + 20 t (1 - t)) px across and int(32 t) px down at time t, the plate moving with the holes or staying while they move through it (with two holes it is the fourth checkpoint validator's frame): the frame between drawn exactly reads `hole` 0 and is delivered as in 3.1.0, both made cells byte for byte, for two, three and four holes, the plate moving or staying; with one hole more before the row, or with the row's last hole shut in the second source frame, it reads 0.10 % and gives way to the nearer source frame. Figure 10: on the same striped body, a plate 40 by 90 px with a column of three holes 8 px square, 24 px apart, moving 32 px down, the middle hole shut in the second source frame: the frame between drawn exactly reads `hole` 0 and is delivered as in 3.1.0: its middle hole, one more than the second source frame has, lies against holes of both source frames and the in-place reading takes it for theirs; the last hole pairs with its own two. Figure 11: on the same striped body, a plate 105 px square with a hole 8 px square that the first source frame and the frame between have in place and the second source frame shuts, beside a hole moving 52 px across and 48 px down, drawn at its exact midpoint 10 px from the first (the fifth checkpoint validator's frame): the frame between reads `hole` 0 and is delivered as in 3.1.0, both made cells byte for byte, and so with the hole in place kept by the second source frame instead. Figure 12: a body 200 by 260 px outlined in black on a 400 by 400 px canvas, moving whole, with holes through it; what the reading does not tell: the body moving 32 px down with a row of four holes 8 px square, 24 px apart, and one hole more midway between the middle two reads `hole` 0 and is delivered as in 3.1.0 (so is figure 8's row standing still, drawn a pixel taller, with one hole more midway); moving 48 px across and 48 px down with a gap 5 by 30 px and one gap more 10 px beside it in the frame between, it reads `hole` 0 and gives way for `outline`, as in 3.1.0; with the gap more 11 px beside, and a gap 8 px wide with its twin 13 px beside it, they read `hole` 0.29 % and 0.45 % and give way as in 3.1.0, now with `hole` among their faults besides `outline`. The jump repair of `video-loop`, its eased retime and overlapped wrap, `video-loop-compare`, and `video-loop-repair` do not read `hole`; their measures and outputs are as in 3.1.0. A `--between-cache` directory written by 3.1.0 is not taken from by this version: the code of `rife.py` is part of what a kept frame is keyed by, so those frames are made again.
- **Tests**: new `tests/video/test_inbetween_hole.py`, synthetic only: figure 1 (a 160 by 200 px figure of rectangles outlined in black, legs filled darker than 70/255, the near leg drawn over the far one, an arm joined to the body above and below with an 8 px gap between them in every frame; one source frame with its legs side by side, the other with a gap between them) checks that the band through the far leg reads as a hole over exactly its own 320 pixels as a share of the solid body, and the drawn frame, the arm's gap, that gap moved 3 px, a 3 px pinhole and an empty frame read 0; that the readings before this one pass the band (`outline_loss` 0, `outline_shift` over 5 %, `dark_excess` below 0, `ghost` 0, `partial_excess` 0, no fault) and that with `hole` the fault is `["hole"]`; that resampling the two frames to four under `--between auto` takes the nearer source frame at both made cells (cells 1 and 3) with the fault named in the warning words, while `--between rife` keeps them named; and that the clean frame and the frame with the moved arm gap are delivered as the very frame made. Figure 2 (a body 120 by 180 px of one colour with a gap 8 by 40 px through it, on a 240 by 220 px canvas, moved whole from one source frame to the next by 0, 4, 8, 12, 24 and 32 px, across and diagonally) checks that at every move it is resampled to four with both made cells delivered as the very frame made, no fault, and reads `hole` 0, each source frame moved half the way giving the frame between byte for byte. Figure 3 (a body 90 px wide over two legs 24 px wide, each standing in a boot that reaches under the other leg, so a gap a step opens between the legs is closed by the boots below; in one source frame the legs touch, in the other they are parted), still and moving 30 px between source frames, is kept the same way and reads 0; and moving with an 8 by 40 px band through its body, it reads exactly its 320 pixels as a share of the solid body, in either order of the source frames, with both made cells given way to the nearer source frame and `hole` among their faults. Figure 4 (a body 200 by 180 px of one colour that stays, with a part on it 40 px square and a hole 14 px square through the part, the part moving 32 px from one source frame to the next) is kept the same way and reads 0. Figure 5 (a body that stays, patterned, with a part on it and a hole through the part's middle, the part moved from one source frame to the next, each frame between drawn exactly at its place: the checkpoint validator's frame with a body 200 by 180 px with stripes 3 px wide every 6 px, a lighter colour by 40 levels, on a 260 by 220 px canvas, a part 24 px square with a hole 10 px square, moved 32 px across; and a body 240 by 260 px on a 300 by 300 px canvas in five patterns, stripes 3, 2 and 6 px wide every 6, 4 and 12 px running up and down, stripes 3 px wide every 6 px running across, and checks 4 px square, at contrasts 15, 40 and 120, parts 24, 40 and 60 px square with holes 10, 17 and 25 px square): figure 5's validator frame resampled to four, and its parts of 24, 40 and 60 px moved 32 px diagonally and the 24 px part moved 48 px over stripes at contrast 120 resampled to eight, keep every made frame as made; the wide grid of figure 5 (every pattern and contrast at each listed move at t 0.5, and the three parts moved 16, 32 and 48 px at t 0.25 and 0.75) reads `hole` 0 and is marked slow, so it runs with `pytest --full`. Figure 6 (a torso 120 by 260 px that stays, patterned in blocks 4 px square each lighter by its own whole number up to 200 levels, with an arm beside its right edge: the upper arm and the forearm lie along that edge and are joined by the arm's outer side, so the gap between arm and torso, 16 by 12 px, has the torso's edge on its left and the arm on its other three sides; the arm reaches 48 px above and 52 px below the gap and moves 40 px down from one source frame to the next) reads `hole` 0 at t 0.25, 0.5 and 0.75 and keeps every made frame resampled to eight. The boundary test (a part 30 px square moving 32 px across a body that stays, a hole 14 px square open through it in one source frame and closed in the other, 8 px square in the frame between) checks that the half-open gap on the moving part, over a plain body and over the stripes, reads its 64 pixels as a share of the body and gives both made cells way to the nearer source frame with `faults: ["hole"]`. Figure 7: a body 240 by 260 px on a 300 by 300 px canvas that stays, in stripes 3 px wide every 6 px (lighter by 40 levels) or plain, with a plate on it (|u| < 24 and |v| < 28 px in its own axes) and a gap through the plate (|u| < 6, |v| < 16), each frame between drawn exactly at its place in one motion: the plate's centre moving across as the plate turns, grows or is sheared about it, or the plate swinging from a point 100 px above its centre. One test checks that the third checkpoint validator's frame (the plate turning 60 degrees as it moves 48 px over the stripes), resampled to four, keeps both made frames as made, and resampled to eight (t 0.25, 0.5 and 0.75 each way) every one; that the plate turned 90 degrees, grown 2 times and sheared by 0.6 as it moves 48 px, and swung 45 degrees, resampled to four over the stripes, keep both made frames; and that over the plain body each of these reads `hole` 0. A test marked slow (it runs with `pytest --full`) checks that 125 frames read `hole` 0: the plate turned 0 to 90 degrees, grown 1.1 to 2 times or sheared by 0.15 to 0.6 as it moves 0, 24 or 48 px, or swung 10 to 45 degrees, over both bodies at t 0.5, and turned as it moves over the stripes at t 0.25. A boundary test checks that, on the plate over either body, a frame between with its gap drawn 16 by 40 px or 18 by 18 px, with the plate drawn 12 px across its way or where it is at t 0.8 in a frame made at t 0.5, with the plate growing 2.2 times as it moves 48 px (the source frames' gaps more than 4 times apart in area), with the plate moving 80 px, and with the plate swung 60 degrees, gives both made cells way to the nearer source frame with `hole` among their faults. Figure 8: the same body with a plate 120 by 40 px on it and a row of four holes 8 px square, 24 px apart, through the plate. Its test checks that, the plate moving 32 px down over either body, the frame between drawn exactly and the same with the row a pixel taller keep both made frames as made; that with one hole more midway between two of the row's, between the last two, past the row's end, or with one of the row's moved 12 px to a midway, both made cells give way to the nearer source frame with `hole` among their faults; and that, the plate standing still, the row drawn a pixel taller keeps both made frames. Figure 9: the striped body (240 by 260 px that stays, stripes 3 px wide every 6 px) with a plate on it and a row of two, three or four holes 8 px square, 12 px apart, through the plate, moving on a curved way from one source frame to the next, int(40 t + 20 t (1 - t)) px across and int(32 t) px down at time t; the plate moves with the holes or stays while they move through it. With two holes it is the fourth checkpoint validator's frame. One test checks that, resampled to four, the frame between drawn exactly keeps both made frames as made, for two, three and four holes, the plate moving or staying. Figure 10: the same striped body with a plate 40 by 90 px on it and a column of three holes 8 px square, 24 px apart, through the plate, the plate moving 32 px down from one source frame to the next. A boundary test checks that, with two and four holes, the plate moving or staying, a frame between with one hole more before the row, and a frame between whose second source frame has the row's last hole shut, give both made cells way to the nearer source frame with `hole` among their faults; and that with `rife.HOLE_PAIR_STEPS` set to one step, both searches (the search for a set pairing every hole, and the search for the set pairing the most) run past it and the frame between of two holes gives both made cells way as well. Figure 11: the striped body with a plate 105 px square on it, through it a hole 8 px square that the first source frame and the frame between have in place and the second source frame shuts, beside a hole moving 52 px across and 48 px down, drawn at its exact midpoint 10 px from the first; the fifth checkpoint validator's frame. Its test checks that, resampled to four, both made frames are kept as made, and so with the hole in place kept by the second source frame instead. Figure 12: a body 200 by 260 px outlined in black on a 400 by 400 px canvas, moving whole, with holes through it. Its test pins what the reading does not tell: it checks that the body moving 32 px down with a row of four holes 8 px square, 24 px apart, and one hole more midway between the middle two keeps both made frames as made, and so do figure 8's row standing still, drawn a pixel taller, with one hole more midway, and figure 10's frame with the middle hole shut in the second source frame; that, the body moving 48 px across and 48 px down with a gap 5 by 30 px, one gap more 10 px beside it in the frame between reads `hole` 0 from either source frame to the other; and that the gap more 11 px beside, and a gap 8 px wide with its twin 13 px beside it, give both made cells way with `hole` among their faults. The test on triples alone: twenty alike holes, each with its own two holes and up to five more pairs near its own at seeded random costs. It checks that all twenty are paired, and that none is where the search for a set pairing every hole runs past the steps (the search for the set pairing the most runs past them on this group).

**Upgrading from 3.1.0: `video-cycle-align` now gives way to the nearer source frame on made frames with double outlines under `--between auto`, changing only alignment outputs with such outlines while made frames without them and all outputs from `video-loop`, `video-loop-compare`, and `video-loop-repair` keep their bytes.**

- **New: `video-cycle-align` now reads a new `doubled` fault on frames RIFE makes between source frames and gives way to the nearer source frame.** On every frame RIFE makes between two source frames, `video-cycle-align` reads a new fault, `doubled` ("a double outline"). Under `--between auto` (the default) a made frame with this fault gives way to the nearer source frame; under `--between rife` the frame is kept and named. `video-set --align-cycles auto` runs the same alignment. Where RIFE's flow does not follow a line (the top of a boot one source frame tilts and the other plants), it can fade the line out in place instead of moving it, and the made frame shows that source frame's line, lighter, in its own place, beside the line the frame drew: a double outline. Its coverage is whole (no `ghost`), nothing darker is added (no `smear`), the line is inside the body and the edge keeps its line (no `outline`), and there is no hole; the cross-fade reading (`crossfade`) only names frames to look at. So such a frame passed every fault and was delivered. The reading `rife.doubled(made, a, b)`: a frame's line is its solid pixels darker by more than 50/255 than its own luma closed by a square 2.5 % of the made frame's body height wide (`rife.DOUBLED_WIDE`, 0.025; a stroke narrower than that is a line, a dark fill wider is not). A pixel of one source frame's line is left at part strength where the three frames are solid, the other source frame draws no line within 4 px (`rife.DOUBLED_SHARED`, 4) and is lighter there by more than 50/255, and the made frame is darker than the other by more than 50/255 (still seen) but by no more than 0.8 of the line's contrast (`rife.DOUBLED_PART`, 0.8), there and at its darkest pixel within 2 px (no line at full strength beside it). Such pixels count in regions 3 px thick (`rife.DOUBLED_THICK`, 3); widened by 3 px, those that touch make one run; the reading is the longest run's length (sqrt(12) times the root of its pixels' larger second moment) over the made frame's body height. The fault `doubled` is a reading over 0.03 (`interpolation_quality.DOUBLED_WARN`). Kept as the source frames' own: a line both draw within 4 px of each other, at any strength; a line carried at more than 0.8 of its contrast (the source frame's own drawing); the soft edge of a line the flow moved (the full line lies within 2 px); a line left less than 50/255 darker than the other source frame there (not seen); a dark patch wider than the closing; a run of 3 % of the body or less. A line only one source frame draws (a crease that comes or goes), left at part strength, is a double outline too.
- Report fields: each row of a loop's `smear` list in the `video-cycle-align` report (and in `cycle_align` in `strip.json`) gains `doubled`. A frame given way for it carries `"doubled"` in `faults` and `method: "nearest"`, and gets a line in `warnings` with these words: "left a line one source frame beside it draws at part strength in its own place, over a run 10.21 % of its body high, a double outline, so the nearer source frame was taken there (--between auto)". The `--between` help names it.
- Python callers: new `rife.doubled(made, a, b)`, `rife.DOUBLED_WIDE` (0.025), `rife.DOUBLED_SHARED` (4), `rife.DOUBLED_PART` (0.8), `rife.DOUBLED_THICK` (3); new `interpolation_quality.DOUBLED_WARN` (0.03); `interpolation_quality.faults(measure)` returns `"doubled"` where the measure carries a `doubled` reading over `DOUBLED_WARN`, and measures without a `doubled` key are judged as before.
- Output changes: an alignment whose made frame shows a double outline over 3 % of its body height now delivers the nearer source frame at that cell instead, counted in `replaced` and `nearest_at` rather than `made_by_rife` and `made_at`. Other made frames are delivered as before with the same bytes; the report gains `doubled` on every made frame. A frame RIFE cross-fades so that both drawings' lines stay at part strength inside the silhouette over a long enough run now gives way too, still named to look at for its cross-fade (`look`); such frames were kept before because the cross-fade reading is no fault (it also reads over its line on a clean drawing between far-apart drawings, which reads no double outline and is kept). Synthetic numbers (the figure of the tests): a coat over two legs in boots outlined 3 px in near-black, the near boot lifted 18 px in one source frame and planted in the other; the frame drawn between with the first source frame's near-boot lines laid over it at 0.45 of their strength reads `outline_loss` 0, `outline_shift` 0, `ghost` 0, `hole` 0, `crossfade` 0 and `doubled` 0.102; in 3.1.0 it is kept, now the nearer source frame is taken. The frame drawn between, blurred, or smeared along the step, a hand moving 60 px over the coat, boots drawn with a second line, a hem drawn with two lines and softened, and a light crease carried at its own strength read `doubled` 0 and are kept. The jump repair of `video-loop`, its eased retime and overlapped wrap, `video-loop-compare`, and `video-loop-repair` do not read `doubled`; their measures and outputs are as before. A `--between-cache` directory written before this version is not taken from: the code of `rife.py` is part of what a kept frame is keyed by, so those frames are made again.
- **Tests**: new `tests/video/test_inbetween_doubled.py`, synthetic only: the figure above; checks that the line left at 0.45 passes every earlier reading and reads `doubled` 0.102, as do both source frames' lines left at half and the colour blended inside the drawing's coverage; that resampling two frames to four under `--between auto` takes the nearer source frame at both made cells with the fault in the warning's words, while `--between rife` keeps them named; that a crease only one source frame draws, left at 0.3 to 0.6, gives way, left at 0.25 reads 0, and the frames that draw it or not keep their bytes; that nine clean, soft or source-drawn frames read 0 and are delivered as the very frame made; that the walker of `tests/video/test_crossfade.py` keeps its drawing between far-apart legs (16 to 3 degrees) in grey and black outlines, reading 0, while its cross-fade gives way, and its 10 to 5 degree cross-fade reads 0.0195 and is kept; and where each bound turns (seen at 0.30 of the line's contrast, not at 0.28; part strength up to 0.80, not 0.82; a boot moved 9 px, not 8; a stroke 3 px wide, not a patch 10 px square; a run over 7 columns, 0.0343, not 6, 0.0294), and that a hand smeared back over a source frame's line reads as that line left (0.063). `tests/video/test_crossfade.py`: the alignment test of the cross-fading stand-in now expects its frames across the legs' long steps to give way under `auto` as double outlines, still named to look at, and to be kept and named to look at under `--between rife`; its 10 to 5 degree cross-fade under `auto` is kept with `doubled` under the line.

**Upgrading from 3.1.0: `video-loop-repair` can now restore damaged cells on held fixed cuts by reading source frames through recorded frame holds.** Fixed cuts held with `--size-hold auto` now record per-frame holds in `size_hold.cut_frames`. A 3.1.0 report of a held fixed cut lacks this record and stays `source-processing-unsupported`; cut it again with this version to enable repair. A report of a cut that was not held restores as before.

- **New: Held fixed cuts can now be restored from source frames.** Source projection applies each cut frame's recorded move and `padding_ltrb` before scrub, small-component cleanup, optional integer body ramp, one shared integer crop, and standard resampling. Unmodified cells must reproduce exactly, and cut frames must match `source_projection` hashes; a changed move on a replaced cell is refused as `source-processing-receipt-mismatch`. Reports without `cut_frames` stay `unknown` and `source-processing-unsupported`. Malformed `cut_frames` exit nonzero without writing a candidate. Cuts with `--anchor motion`, `motion-auto`, cycle alignment, wrap blends, or follow warps remain unprojected.
- Report fields (`<name>.loop.report.json`): `size_hold` records `cut_frames` on held fixed cuts, giving an ordered list of exact float entries with `scale`, `foot_point_px` (`[x, y]`), and `lift_px` (or `null`).
- Repair records: `projection` in repair reports and candidate `restoration` receipts gain `size_hold` (`padding_ltrb`, `cut_frames`) on held cuts, binding proposal IDs to recorded moves.
- Python callers: new `gait_fallback.frame_hold(k, measured, lift=None)` returns a frame's move dict, and `gait_fallback.hold_frame(frame, move, pad)` applies it to one frame. `gait_fallback.undo_scale` uses both and preserves previous byte outputs.
- Output changes: on a synthetic walk away (15.0 % smaller over 73 frames, feet 20 px higher) cut on frames 24 to 47 with `--anchor body`, projection reproduces 24 of 24 cells. Restoring smeared cell 9 (25 pixels) yields verdict `improved` and recovers the cell byte for byte: 119 px tall with feet on row 129 (held loop cells run 119 to 120 px tall, feet on rows 129 to 130; cut with `--size-hold off`, cell 9 is 111 px tall on row 120, and the loop spans 107 to 113 px tall, feet on rows 116 to 122). In 3.1.0 this request ended `unknown`, `source-processing-unsupported` with no candidate. For six synthetic cuts (174 files), `video-loop` matches 3.1.0 except `producer` and `size_hold.cut_frames`. For five non-held cuts (110 files), `video-loop-repair` matches 3.1.0 except `measurement_engine` and candidate report digests.
- **Tests**: `tests/video/test_held_cut_restoration.py` checks that the synthetic walk away cut on frames 24 to 47 with `--anchor body` restores smeared cell 9 byte for byte with verdict `improved`, matching first-cell height and row while the filmed source frame is under 0.95 height and 8 px or more higher; checks exact cell reproduction; checks that reports lacking `cut_frames` stay `source-processing-unsupported`; checks that four malformed records exit nonzero with no candidate; checks that a 1 % scale alteration triggers `source-processing-receipt-mismatch`; and checks that `frame_hold` round-trips through JSON to match `undo_scale` with and without feet held.

## v3.1.0 - Walk and run loop gates for held animation and seam pops, size hold for fixed cuts, talking face layer and default chroma decontamination

**Upgrading from 3.0.0: Fixed walk or run cuts and clips whose feet climb or sink now hold size and feet before cutting, changing bytes when size changes by 1 % or more.** Application scan numbers change for held clips, and held fixed cuts are not restored by `video-loop-repair`. Loops cut with `--size-hold off`, clips changing size by under 1 %, held `motion-auto` cuts on one row, and non-walk cuts keep their bytes. The report records `size_hold` on fixed walk or run cuts, `size_hold.feet` when size is held, `size_hold.anchor_regions` under `--anchor motion`, and `size_hold.at_cut` on fixed cuts; `producer` names the new version.

- **New: Fixed cuts hold size before the cut is read.** Walk or run cuts using `--cycle fixed` with any anchor (`none`, `body`, `feet`, `motion`) now hold size over the whole clip before reading the cut. A fixed cut is held only where the cut itself shows the change, comparing each frame of the cut with the frame one cycle of the cut's length later, or, where the clip ends before that frame, with the frame one cycle earlier: the median of those ratios must show at least half (`AT_CUT_SHARE`, 0.5) of the clip's predicted change over that length, and feet move only where the cut shows them move the same way. Where the cut does not show the change, it is cut as filmed, and the report says why (`size_hold.why`, `size_hold.feet.why`). The report's `size_hold` gains `at_cut` on a fixed cut the clip reads a change on (`pairs`, `share`, `change`, `feet_px`, `expected`, `feet_expected_px`, `size`, `feet`). Seams, foot crossings (`cycle.step_count`), the gate, and application scans are read on held frames. Under `--anchor motion`, target regions are read where held (`size_hold.anchor_regions`). Passing `--size-hold off` keeps filmed frames pixel for pixel, recording `size_hold: {"applied": false, "why": "--size-hold off"}`. Automatic searches with `--anchor body`, `feet`, or `none`, `--cycle pinned`, and idle cuts are not held.
- **Changed: The size hold moves climbing or sinking feet to the first frame's row.** Where size changes by 1 % or more and feet climb or sink by 1 % of first-frame height or more (`FOOT_HOLD_MIN`), frames shift so feet stay on the first frame's row. Foot drift is read one cycle on (`gait_fallback.foot_drift`), preventing settling poses from reading as climbing feet. This stops wrap jumps from shifting into position where motion search (`motion_anchor.SEARCH_RADIUS`, 28 rows) or body anchors cannot correct them. Level clips keep their bytes. `size_hold` records `feet` (`min`: 0.01, `method`: `one-cycle-on`, `change_px`, `held`).
- **Note: What --size-hold off leaves in place.** With `--size-hold off`, `motion-auto` keeps its gait fallback: if the first search finds no cycle, size changes of 3 % or more (`SCALE_DRIFT_MIN`) are scaled back one cycle on with feet where filmed (`gait_fallback.scale_undone`). Cuts with `off` keep their previous bytes.
- **Changed: Held fixed cuts are not restored from source frames.** `video-loop-repair` reads cuts through source projection, which treats `size_hold.applied` as `source-processing-unsupported`. Recuts or scans of held fixed clips are not restored from source frames.
- **Output changes** Fixed walk or run cuts, scans, and `motion-auto` cuts of clips with climbing or sinking feet change bytes when size changes by 1 % or more. Across ten synthetic walkers cut four ways with `auto` and `off`, 62 of 80 keep their bytes. Changed cases are the fixed body cut and scan of eight walks changing size by 1 % or more (shrinking 7 % and 2 %, growing 20 % and 2.5 %, slow walk shrinking 7 %, two walks away, settling walk growing 2.5 %), plus `motion-auto` of the two walks away. On a walk away (15 % smaller, feet 20 px higher), a fixed body cut refused at seam ratio 6.83 passes held at 1.25 (size alone failed at 10.03); shrinking 2 % moves from 3.80 to 1.46; a walk whose feet climb 96 px refused under `motion-auto` at 13.03 passes held at 1.08. Scan ratios drop on the 7 % shrinking walk from 1.16 to 0.52, and on the walk away from 1.66 to 0.42. Shrinking 7 % cut by hand drops from 7.97 to 2.04 (still refused), and a slow walk shrinking 7 % from 18.31 to 4.48. The walk away under `motion-auto` moves from 0.79 to 1.41 because feet are held on row before cutting rather than closed by motion ramps. On test fixtures, a run filmed from a standing start that raises and waves its arm and crouches reads 6.9 % smaller over the clip while its cut shows 0; a walk of 26 frames cut 20 long reads 2.5 % larger over the clip while its cut shows 0; walkers that grow or shrink show 0.94 to 1.07 of predicted change at the cut; and every synthetic case gives the same bytes with this rule as without it.
- **Python callers** `gait_fallback.foot_drift(frames, measured)` returns `min`, `method`, `change_px`, `held`, and `lift` (frame shifts in px, None when not held). `gait_fallback.FOOT_HOLD_MIN` (0.01) is added. `gait_fallback.undo_padding` and `undo_scale` take `lift=None`. `gait_fallback.held_box(box, k, measured, pad, lift=None)` maps filmed frame `k` boxes to held coordinates. `gait_fallback.at_cut(frames, measured, feet, *, start, length)` is added and returns that record; `gait_fallback.AT_CUT_SHARE` (0.5) is added.
- **Tests** In `tests/video/test_gait_fallback.py`: fixed cuts of the walk away and 2 % shrinking walker pass held under 2.0 seam ratio, wrap changes matching internal steps (size-only hold fails on feet); `motion-auto` on the 96 px climbing walk passes held on row with motion anchor applied; `--size-hold off` gives filmed frames pixel for pixel and records why; stable walkers keep identical bytes under `auto` and `off` and record not held; idle fixed cuts omit `size_hold`; foot drift reads 21 px for the 20 px walk away, 0 for level walks, and 0 for settling poses (fitted lines read 2.7 px); held walks away need no top room and `held_box` aligns head boxes; `--anchor motion` regions match held positions; forward walks mark feet not held while walks away close at feet; a hand cut of the run with earlier motion and of the short walk is not held, `size_hold.at_cut` shows 0 over all pairs, the report says why, and strip and frames match `--size-hold off`; held fixed cuts show the change at the cut (`size_hold.at_cut.size`, and `feet` for the walk away); a synthetic walk away from the camera (15 % smaller by the end, feet 20 px higher), cut by hand on the last 24-frame cycle of a 72-frame clip with 0, 1 or 5 of the cut's own first frames repeated after it, and the same walk cut on its first cycle with 0, 1 or 5 of the cut's last frames placed before it, is held each time on 24 pairs, one for each frame of the cut (the repeated frames show no change against the cut), and the loop passes the seam gate. In `tests/video/test_loop_silent_pass.py`: the walk away jitter test passes `--size-hold off` so feet climb crop rows as before.

**Upgrading from 3.0.0: Bakes retain the same bytes for figures whose anatomy is unchanged. The `pixel` refusal message now names the sides and pixel counts. A figure that had `face-absent` may now have a face, with a lower rigid boundary only when that face ends below the neck. `video-sway-inspect` records use schema 3, and a cell whose every offset is 0 is now copied as it was, including colour under clear pixels with `--gain 0`; the other changes add options, and without them the strip, records and inspection stay as before.** Smooth bakes that leave the in-breath share out keep their bytes. A smooth `breathe.json` config and a breath sway's `drive` record now carry `inhale`, Python callers of `smooth_wave` and `smooth_shoulder_wave` must pass the share, and `breathe.INHALE` is gone. A smooth `breathe.json` config and a breath sway's `drive` record now also carry `start_cell` (0 when left out) and `loop_cells` (null when left out), and a record without them reads as the loop itself from its first cell.

The Breathe layer still supports `pixel` and `smooth`.

- **Changed: Pixel refusal.** `pixel` still refuses stretched content past the top, left, or right of a cell instead of cropping it.
  The message names each side and its opaque pixel count. A pixel past a corner counts on both sides.
  When an overflowing side is the drawing's own border, the message also gives the number of pixels covering that edge.
  It advises `--mode smooth` or sidecar `mode: "smooth"` for an illustration cut by its frame.
  It advises a wider cell margin or lower `depth` for a pixel sprite.
  It warns that padding, baking, and cropping back silently loses pushed off content.
  The curator preview uses the same refusal wording.
  The border reading follows the 2% rule in [video pipeline](docs/video-pipeline.md) section 3.
  See [breathing](docs/breathing.md) for behavior at frame edges.
- **New: Second eye pair search.** It runs only when the existing search finds no pair.
  Its minimum eye area is 0.2% of opaque pixels above the neck.
  Its eye width to height limit is 3.0, and its minimum eye center distance from the body axis is 0.02 of content width.
  Pair placement, separation, size similarity, and row overlap conditions remain the same.
  A face found by this search clears `face-absent`. It lowers the boundary only if the face ends below the neck.
- **Faster: Breathe bakes.** Array operations speed up pixel outline thinning, appendage weights, and row mapping.
  `smooth` solves displacement only for pixels that move.
  Anatomy and settings checks run once per row, and `sprite-gen breathe` shares one anatomy measurement between its report and bake.
  On synthetic 960 x 960 cells with Apple M1, bake time fell from 0.20 s to 0.10 s for `smooth`.
  For an outlined figure with arms in `pixel`, it fell from 0.98 s to 0.05 s.
  These measurements exclude PNG reading and writing.
  `bake_breathe_sequence(images, cfg, anat=None)` and `anatomy_report(images, cfg, anat=None)` accept measured anatomy.
  `protect(anat)` accepts one column or an array of columns.
- **New: Sway on a cycle's frames.** `video-sway --frames` moves full-size frames and writes them with `sway.json` to a new folder.
  A whole `cycle/` can supply its frame time; other damped-mass runs require `--fps`.
- **New: Sway on the breath.** `--breathe` drives the move from a checked smooth `breathe.json`, with `--lag` setting how late the part follows.
  The first cell, rests and close have zero offset.
- **New: A region moved whole.** A fifth `--region` number, `flat`, keeps the centre at full weight before the weight falls to the rim.
- **New: Across apart from down.** `--gain-across` sets the across gain separately from `--gain`, which sets the gain down.
- **Changed: Zero-offset cells.** A cell where no region moves is copied byte for byte, including colour under clear pixels.
- **Changed: Inspection seams.** `video-sway-inspect` drops a pair's seam points covered by a third moving region.
  Its record uses `schema_version` 3.
- **New: Breathe beat chosen to miss the hands.** In `smooth` mode, `--avoid-region` chooses a breath beat whose peaks keep the chest above the hands' own rise and records the choice in `breathe.json`.
- **New: The in-breath share.** The smooth sidecar key `inhale` is the share of one breath the in-breath takes; the in-breath peaks at that phase and the rest of the breath is the out-breath.
  Default 0.40, range 0.2 to 0.8 with both ends allowed; out-of-range values are refused, not clamped, and a pixel row refuses the key.
  With the key left out or at 0.40 every bake is byte-identical to before.
  With `breath_peaks`, the rest between peaks p and q is at p + (1 - inhale) x (q - p).
  `sprite-gen breathe --inhale S` sets it, and the normalized smooth config in `breathe.json` always carries it.
  `--avoid-region` scores every candidate on the row's own `inhale` curve, the curve the bake uses.
  Sway `--breathe` takes the chest curve, the late curve and the phase check at the record's share, so `breath_peaks` phases written at another share are accepted when they match.
  A record without the key reads as 0.40 and sways exactly as before, and a hand-written record for a breath made elsewhere can state its share.
  The sway record's `drive` now also carries `inhale`.
  The curator sidecar round trip keeps `inhale` as written and its range table mirrors 0.2 to 0.8.
- **New: A stretch of a loop.** A loop set has an idle loop and short pieces that leave it or join it part way through a breath, and such a piece can now be breathed and swayed on the loop's own breath instead of being padded into a sequence and cut out by hand.
  The smooth sidecar key `loop_cells` is the number of cells of the loop the row carries the breath of, an integer of 2 or more, and `start_cell` is the loop's cell the row starts at, an integer from 0 to loop_cells minus 1, default 0.
  Cell i of the row has the phase of the loop's cell (start_cell + i) mod loop_cells, the very same floating point value, the beat (`breaths`, or `breath_peaks` whose cells are cells of that loop) is the loop's, and the row's first cell is then not necessarily at rest.
  With `loop_cells` left out or null the row is its own loop, with both keys left out phases and bakes are byte identical to before, and the normalized smooth config in `breathe.json` always carries `start_cell: 0` and `loop_cells: null`.
  `sprite-gen breathe --loop-cells N --start-cell K` sets them; `--avoid-region` is refused with either, since the beat is chosen on a whole loop's frames and a stretch carries on its loop's beat.
  `sprite-gen breathe --reference FRAME.png`, in any mode, measures the row's anatomy (head line, chest, how far it rises and its place in the frame) on that frame instead of the row's first frame, normally the loop set's first cell, and `breathe.json` records `reference` with the file's `path` and `sha256`.
  Sway `--breathe` reads `start_cell` and `loop_cells` from the record, recomputes the phases with them and offsets a stretch's cell i exactly as the loop's cell (start_cell + i) mod loop_cells, from the stretch's first cell on, with nothing wound up before it.
  The fold rule and `--stretch-floor` read the loop's whole breath, so a stretch moves by the same gain as its loop and the seams between them do not jump; the record's `reach_px`, `fold` block and `least_cell` are the loop's, and `least_cell` is a cell of the loop.
  `sprite-gen video-sway --reference FRAME.png`, only with `--frames`, names the frame the regions were read on; each frame's body motion is read with it laid first, `body_px`, `body_worn_px` and `body_bob_px` come from or span it, and `sway.json` records `reference` with `path` and `sha256`.
  The sway record's `drive` now also carries `start_cell` and `loop_cells`, and a record without the keys reads as the loop itself from its first cell and sways exactly as before.
  Refused, never clamped or wrapped: either key on a pixel row, a negative or non integer `start_cell`, a `loop_cells` under 2 or non integer, `start_cell` at or above `loop_cells`, `start_cell` at or above the row's cell count with `loop_cells` left out (at the bake), `breath_peaks` whose last peak is within one cell of the end of `loop_cells`, a `--reference` of another size on either command, and `--reference` with `--loop-dir`.
  The curator sidecar round trip keeps `start_cell` and `loop_cells` as written and its range check mirrors the Python refusals.
- **Changed: Breath curve functions.** `breathe.smooth_wave(t, inhale)` and `breathe.smooth_shoulder_wave(t, lag, inhale)` now require the share.
  The module constant `breathe.INHALE` is removed; the default lives in `curation.BREATHE_SMOOTH_SHAPE["inhale"]`, and `breathe.smooth_inhale(cfg)` returns a row's share.
  `breath_beat.choose(images, lift, inhale, avoid, anchor, frame_ms)` and `breath_beat.nudged(n, k, inhale)` take it as well.
- **Tests:** `tests/effects/test_breathe.py` checks border counts, advice, smooth edge preservation, and the second face search.
  `tests/effects/test_breathe_js_mirror.py` checks curator refusal wording and byte identity with the pixel bake.
  `tests/effects/test_breathe_smooth.py` checks pixel and smooth byte goldens and shared anatomy and strain measurements, and that the in-breath takes the row's share: at share 0.42 the cells at phases 0.21 and 0.42 are byte-identical to the default row's cells at 0.20 and 0.40, `breath_peaks` rests at the share, out-of-range and pixel values are refused, and the CLI flag is honoured.
  `tests/effects/test_breathe_smooth_curator.py` checks that the sidecar round trip keeps `inhale`.
  `tests/effects/test_breathe_publish.py` checks that the stand-in bake accepts the new argument.
  `tests/video/test_sway_frames.py`, `tests/video/test_sway_breath.py`, `tests/video/test_sway_flat_across.py`, and `tests/video/test_sway_seams.py` check the Sway paths, drives, weights, gains and seams.
  `tests/video/test_sway_breath.py` also checks that the sway follows the share the record says for `breaths` and `breath_peaks`, that a record without the key sways as the default, and that the default breath sways the same bytes as before the change.
  `tests/effects/test_breathe_smooth.py` also checks that a stretch's cell i has the loop cell (start_cell + i) mod loop_cells phase for `breaths` and `breath_peaks` (inside the loop, past its close, the whole loop, and the loop turned), that a stretch has exactly the phases the pad hand step gave, that a row without the keys is its own loop, the stretch refusals, that the CLI bakes what the sidecar bake bakes, and that `--reference` measures the anatomy on that frame, bakes with it and records it.
  `tests/effects/test_breathe_smooth_curator.py` also checks that the round trip keeps `start_cell` and `loop_cells` and that rows the bake refuses are kept and reported.
  `tests/video/test_sway_breath.py` also checks, on a synthetic bust, that a 17 cell way out of a 48 cell loop of two breaths, starting at the loop's cell 13 with its body 0 to 3 px across, is offset as the loop's cells are, at the gain the loop is lowered to by the fold rule, a gain at which the 17 cells alone would not fold; that engine commands give the pad hand step's phases, offsets, fold, gain, reach, carry and cells byte for byte; that without `--reference` the carry is off by where the body lies in the first frame; that old records without the keys sway as before; and the `--reference` size and `--loop-dir` refusals.
  `tests/effects/test_breath_beat.py` checks beat choice and refusals, and that each beat candidate is read on the row's share.
  `tests/video/test_follow_stretch_floor.py` and `tests/video/test_follow_inspect.py` cover the changed inspection schema and frames options.

- **Tests**: A plain `pytest` runs a fast bundle: tests marked `slow` are deselected, the header points to `pytest --full`, and the summary counts deselections. `pytest --full` runs every test. The release lane, `scripts/linux_lane.sh`, passes `--full` to both `pytest` calls, including `-m real_rife`, so its gate still runs every test. `tests/release/test_fast_bundle.py` checks both calls and probes selection with and without `--full`. The `slow` rule in `pyproject.toml` is 1 s per case or 5 s across a function's cases on an 8-core M1 running the whole suite; marking a function marks every case. Five tests remain unmarked as the only fast callers of `video-stop`, `workflow`, `defaults`, `video-sway`, and `export-aseprite`. Three fixture-first tests remain unmarked because other fast tests build their fixtures. In `tests/frames/test_ui_kit.py`, the fast bundle keeps 16 of 512 scattered-drawing grid seeds, 8 of 256 full-size-frame band grid seeds, 9 of 256 missing-element sets, 2 of 17 counterexample sheets, and one placement and start in each publish sweep. `tests/curate/test_anchor_selection.py` extracts each run once per module instead of repeating 1.5 to 2.6 s of setup per test; 25 of 27 affected tests are now fast. Removed `tests/video/test_loop_overlap.py::test_a_tail_on_its_own_beat_is_closed_at_the_wrap_with_the_clips_frames_a_cycle_apart[on]`; its wordless case makes the same call with the default "on". Removed `tests/video/test_walk_steps.py::test_cycle_align_takes_a_loop_cut_two_steps_as_one_cycle` and `tests/video/test_one_step_cut.py::test_cycle_align_takes_the_loop_cut_again_on_its_count`; `tests/video/test_loop_prop_seam_held.py::test_cycle_align_aligns_a_two_step_loop_as_the_one_cycle_it_is` covers alignment from recorded `steps: 2`. Merged `tests/video/test_loop_overlap.py::test_the_cli_passes_overlap_wrap_off_and_on_is_its_default` into `tests/video/test_loop_overlap.py::test_with_the_overlapped_wrap_off_nothing_is_read_for_the_wrap_and_the_gate_refuses_the_cut_as_filmed`, retaining both sets of assertions on one CLI cut with `--overlap-wrap off`. In a new virtual environment, the former suite ran 7,881 tests (7,880 passed, 1 skipped) in 21 minutes 54 seconds; `pytest --full` now runs 7,881, including two new release tests, and the fast bundle runs 6,555 in about 6 minutes. Package code, commands, and outputs are unchanged.

**Upgrading from 3.0.0: the `sprite-gen logo` text checks read a refused image once more before refusing it, and on codex they read on a fixed model and effort.** A mood or logo reading of `mismatch` or `no-text` is read again, up to `logo.MAX_READS` readings, which is 2. One reading that spells the asked text passes. An image is refused only when every reading differs. A logo now costs four to six calls: two images and two text checks, each read once or twice. Beyond a check's second reading nothing is sent again on its own. On codex the text checks no longer follow the login's `~/.codex/config.toml`. They read on `gpt-6-sol` at reasoning effort `high`, and the report names the model and effort of every reading. **`sprite-gen logo` refuses a chroma key that is one of the title's own colours, and a cut that is see-through inside its letters.** A letter colour in the key's hue is, by its pixels, a mix with the key, so the matte cuts it see-through. `--chroma-key auto` now skips a key whose hue covers 5 % or more of the title's box in the mood image, or 1 % when the brief names a colour in that hue. When both keys are the title's colours, the run is refused at the new step `key` before the lettering image is bought. After the matte, see-through pixels walled in by the lettering, farther from it than the logo's own glow reaches, refuse the logo at `matte` on 1 % of the visible lettering or more. A run that went through before with a key in the title's hue, or with a see-through cut inside its letters, is now refused at `key` or at `matte` instead of passing with `status: ok`. Runs whose title carries neither key's hue pick the same key as before.

- **A misreading is read once more before it refuses an image.** Before, each check read the image once, and one misreading refused it. A model reading stylised lettering can read the same picture two ways, so a correctly drawn title could be refused on one reading. Both checks use the one rule, `logo.read_text`. A call that fails is not asked again. With no reading before it, the check is `unverified` as before. With a misreading before it, the misreading stands and the image is refused. A refusal message names every reading, for example `the mood image reads ['IO'] and then ['I0'], not ['ION']`. docs/logo.md records a measurement on a synthetic pixel title drawn one stroke short (the Hangul syllable 쿠 drawn as 구, the middle bar of ㅋ left out). The codex reader at gpt-6-sol/high read it as 구 in 10 readings of 10 and never as 쿠, so neither one reading nor a check of two readings (0 of 5) passed the wrong picture. A second reading can only pass a wrong picture if the reader writes a stroke that is not drawn.
- `logo.report.json`: `text_check.mood` and `text_check.logo` keep the deciding reading's `verdict` and `read` and gain `max_reads` and `readings`, each reading in order with its `reading` number, `verdict` and `read` lines, or `reason` when it could not be read. `calls` lists a check's call once per reading, each with `reading` (1 or 2) and `effort` (`null` where the provider names none).
- Codex reader: the text checks run `codex exec --model gpt-6-sol -c model_reasoning_effort="high"`, named on each call, so a check's verdict does not change with the machine it runs on. Before, each machine read on its own config and the report recorded `model: null`. The values are `facing_vision.CODEX_READ_MODEL` and `facing_vision.CODEX_READ_EFFORT`. `--model` still names the images' model only. The facing check still runs on the login's config, unchanged. The xAI and OpenAI text checks read on their own fixed models, as before.
- Python callers: `logo.read_text` returns `(check, calls)`, a list of call records, instead of `(check, call)`. `facing_vision.codex_inspect` takes optional `model` and `effort` keyword arguments. Without them its command line is as before.
- **With `--chroma-key auto`, a key in the title's hue is not chosen.** `logo_matte.choose_key` rules out a key whose hue is 5 % or more of the title's box (`logo_matte.CLASH_HUED_PCT`), or 1 % (`logo_matte.NAMED_CLASH_HUED_PCT`) when `--style` or `--scene` names a colour in its hue (`logo.KEY_COLOUR_WORDS`: green, mint, lime, emerald, jade, chartreuse for green; magenta, fuchsia, pink, purple, violet, plum, orchid for magenta). A word alone never rules a key out; the pixels decide. The keys left are scored as before. When both keys are the title's colours there is no third: the run is refused at `key` (`refused_at: key`) before the lettering image is bought. The message gives each key's share and the brief's words for it and says to restyle the title without one of the two hues or to name the key to lose with `--chroma-key` (`--mood <mood.png>` skips the mood image). An explicit `--chroma-key` is kept; when it is one of the title's colours, a warning says so. Before, such a cut passed with `status: ok`, no warning, `key_hue_left` 0 and a matching text check.
- **A cut that is see-through inside its letters is refused at `matte`.** `logo_matte.see_through_inside` measures see-through pixels (coverage under 240 of 255) walled in by opaque lettering and farther from it than the logo's own glow reaches beside it. Walled in means no way out to the picture's edge except across opaque pixels: the way out starts from the transparent pixels on the edge of the trimmed logo and runs through transparent pixels and see-through ones; a see-through gap of 2 px or narrower does not count as a way, a transparent gap of any width does. A hole joined to the outside, even through one pixel, is outside; a transparent hole walled in by the letters (an O's counter, or a letter colour cut all the way to nothing) is no way out, and a see-through patch round such a hole is still counted inside. Transparent pixels are never counted, only see-through ones. The same way out decides the glow's reach (`glow_reach_px`): the 99th percentile of how far the see-through pixels that have a way out to the picture's edge lie from the opaque lettering, and at least 2 px. On 1 % of the visible lettering or more (`logo_matte.INSIDE_MAX_PCT`) the logo is refused before its text is read, the cut is kept as `logo.rejected.png` and the preview is written. The message says to generate the lettering again on the other key or to restyle the title without that colour. Only the logo matte's own cut (`method: logo-unmix`) is refused; a lettering image returned with its own alpha is measured, not refused. A glow fades out beside the letters and pools in a counter no farther than it reaches there, so glows are not counted.
- `logo.report.json`: `steps.key.scores.<key>` gains `named`, `clash_at_pct` and `clashes`, and `steps.key.key` is `null` when refused at `key`. `logo` gains `see_through_inside` with `px`, `pct_of_visible`, `glow_reach_px`, `max_pct` and `wide`. `refused_at` can now be `key`.
- Python callers: `logo_matte.choose_key(mood, box=None, named=None)` takes `named`, the brief's colour words per key from `logo.named_colours(style, scene)`, and returns `key: None` when both keys are the title's colours. `logo_matte.see_through_inside(image)` is new.
- **Tests**: `tests/gen/test_logo.py` checks that one matching reading is the whole check, that a misreading read again as asked passes at either check, that two misreadings refuse the mood image and refuse the logo and keep it aside with a refusal naming both, that a check reads no more than `MAX_READS` (set to 3, a third reading is taken), that a failed call is not asked again, that a misreading stands when the second call failed, and that the report names the reader model and effort of every reading. The existing refusal tests now script two misreadings. `tests/gen/test_vision_read.py` checks that codex reads on the named model and effort, passed before the image list, that docs/logo.md names both values, and that the facing check's command carries neither. `tests/frames/test_logo_matte.py` checks that a title in both keys' hues (synthetic patchwork letters: pink and plum in magenta's hue, mint in green's) gets neither key, that a colour in a key's hue rules the key out, that a colour the brief names rules it out at the smaller share while the same share unnamed and the word with no such pixels do not, that on green the mint patches are cut see-through and found inside (9.5 % of the visible lettering) while the other key keeps them and finds nothing, that six glow fixtures (a white glow, an outlined cyan glow, a cyan glow pooled in an O's counter on green and on magenta, an orange glow on green, pixel lettering) find nothing inside, that a mint patch walled in by a navy letter on green is still found inside, with the glow's reach at 2 px, when one or four squares of the key in it are cut to nothing, that a hole glued to the letter's edge is inside while sealed and outside once one pixel joins it to the key round the letter, and that big hollow O and A letters find nothing inside, plain or with a glow pooled round a counter whose middle stays clear (three glows). `tests/gen/test_logo.py` checks that a title in both keys' hues is refused at `key` before the lettering is bought, that a named key in the title's hue is kept and the warning says so, that a see-through cut is refused at `matte` and kept aside with no text check, with or without a 9x9 square of the key in the middle of its patch, and that a glowing title is not see-through inside. `tests/logo_lettering.py` gains the `quilt` fixture: square fabric patches in outlined pixel letters, one with a counter, on a key. It also gains `walled_patch` (a navy letter with a mint patch on pure green, with squares or a pixel of the key painted in) and `hollow_letters` (the big O and A shapes), which `lettering` draws through its new `shapes` argument.

**Upgrading from 3.0.0:** Existing runs and commands do not change. This release adds two verbs and a JavaScript player. The wheel now also carries `sprite_gen/video/*.mjs`.

- `sprite-gen video-face` builds a face pack from a reference, a keyed talking clip, and keyed body motions.
- `sprite-gen face-bake` runs the pack's player through node to bake a voice onto body frames, with optional MP4 and beside video output.
- Face request kind `sprite-gen-face-request`, version 1, records face geometry, clip placement, a rest loop, and optional own-mouth spans or timelines.
- Face pack kind `sprite-gen-face-pack`, version 1, carries sprites, body cells, anchors, keeps, rules, a report, a copied player, and SHA-256 digests.
- The lower-face layer covers the mouth, jaw line, cheeks, and chin. The body's own dark grey and black that reach in from the layer's boundary, such as a headset boom or a choker, the body's own lines in the layer's feather, and a hand that reaches over the face from outside the layer stay above it.
- Each body frame receives a place, size, and tilt anchor. The pack records anchor residuals and corrects outlying readings against neighbouring frames. A body frame whose own mouth is closed has its place read again on a ring of skin round the mouth, because a head that turns moves its hair, headset, and cheeks further than its mouth, and a frame whose mouth is open takes the move of the read frames round it. The pack records each frame's move and the frames read on the ring.
