- **New: `pixel-draw --motion TEXT`: the frames, in their order, are one motion.** The prompt says so in your words, and G6 takes the rule of one motion, written once in the header of `pixel_gates.py`: a frame off the ground row still fails, but a height that changes (an arm raised over the head, a blade swung past it) is asked whether the body changed size or only its pose, by the reading `video-loop --body-height` already makes of a cut (`video.gait_fallback.size_change` on height, mass and breadth) — a pose is an info line, a body drawn at another size warns — and a frame drawn twice is a hold, an info line. `gates.G6` then has `rule: "motion"`, `size_change`, `resized`, `posed` and `holds`. Same name and meaning as `video-prompt --motion`; spec key `motion`; also on `--grid` ([pixel-draw](docs/pixel-draw.md#gates)).
- **Behavior change: a `pixel-draw --walk-cycle` is one motion.** Its two passing frames drawn alike, or a height that changes by more than a pixel with the pose, no longer fail G6: they are info lines, and a body drawn at another size warns. A walk cycle still fails off the ground row, and still warns when its legs barely change sides.
- **New: `pixel-draw --duration-ms N` times `anim.gif`** (and an effect's `delay_ms`), 150 ms by default as before, at least 10; spec key `duration_ms`, also on `--grid`. Same name and meaning as `compose-cycle --duration-ms`.
- **Docs: `pixel-draw`'s note that only effort xhigh spent every token thinking and wrote nothing is corrected** — a set of four 48 px frames at effort high did too (2026-10-14).
- **Tests** — `tests/gen/test_pixel_draw_frames.py` (a set of six drawn in two calls, the second holding the first's palette with a colour it added refused and repaired, shown the frames before it and carrying its frames' poses, read back whole on `--resume`; a later call shown the first frame and the four just before its own; a call drawn again on its own one effort lower; a call that fails ending its candidate; each call its own batch; spec frames refused; `--style` in the drawing and the pick of every kind with the structure kept; the default drawing and pick prompts frozen byte for byte from before; `--motion` refused for one frame and for an effect; a synthetic 48 px set of 46 frames of one motion — an arm raised for ten frames and held for four — passing `--grid` with `--motion`, and failing without it; `--duration-ms` in `anim.gif` and an effect's strip; a pick sheet of 46 frames for two candidates drawn at ×3 within Claude's unscaled size), `tests/gen/test_pixel_gates.py` (G6 of one motion lets a raised arm and a hold through, fails a frame off the ground and warns a body drawn larger; a walk cycle is one motion) and `tests/gen/test_pixel_grid.py` (a palette held, with a colour added and one changed; parts joined; previews in rows of eight).
- **Fix: a `pixel-draw` answer that writes its block more than once is read from the last block that reads whole.** An answer sometimes writes a first try — the palette and `END` with no frame — then "Corrected:" and the whole block (seen 2026-10-15 on a 16 px icon); G1 read the first block, found the frame missing and drew the candidate again. Now every block is read and the last one that reads whole is the answer (when none does, the last whose rows the row repair can fix), recorded in G1's `recovered` as `block_many` with which block was read. An answer of one block is read as before.
- **Tests** — `tests/gen/test_pixel_grid.py` (several blocks read from the last whole one, or the last with rows to repair; one block as before) and an answer of two blocks drawn once in `tests/gen/test_pixel_draw.py`.
- **New: alpha haze cleanup for transparent sheets (`frames.alpha_floor.clear_haze`).** Pixels at or above a floor, and faint pixels within a few pixels of them (an object's anti-aliased edge), are kept byte for byte; every other faint pixel becomes fully transparent. The report measures the haze share before and after and counts the solid and edge pixels that changed (`0`). Used by `ui-kit` (on by default) and `slice-sheet --alpha-floor N` (off by default; writes `<prefix>alpha-cleanup.json`). `slice-sheet --chroma-key none` cuts a sheet that is already transparent on its own alpha ([sheet-slicing](docs/sheet-slicing.md#transparent-sheets)).
- **New verb: `ui-kit` splits a game UI kit sheet into nine-slice elements.** `gen --layout-guide ui-kit` attaches a fixed square layout (panel, button, pressed button, card frame, HP and XP bar frames, timer badge) and asks for each element in its box. `ui-kit --sheet uikit.png --out-dir ui/` clears the haze, assigns parts to boxes by centroid, and writes each element's PNG and `ui-kit.json` (or `--report PATH`, which must end in `.json`; each element's `file` is its PNG's path from the report's directory, so a report written outside `--out-dir` still finds its elements): its rectangle, its stretch (`xy`, `x` for the bars, `none` for the badge), nine-slice insets measured so that every corner ornament and end cap lies in a fixed slice, and the content padding (a bar frame's track, a panel's text area). An ornament in the middle of an edge, an empty box or a stray part is reported or refused ([ui-kit](docs/ui-kit.md)). `--layout-guide` alone is still the one-slot figure guide.
- **New: `game-pack` request and manifest version 2 with `images`, `tiles` and `ui`.** Single images (props) are atlas frames with an anchor and an optional footprint (`"base"` measures where the image meets the ground); tiles are their own PNG and WebP files with their stored and on-screen sizes; `ui.kit` reads a `ui-kit` report into nine-slice entries (frame, size, stretch, insets, padding) and `ui.icons` adds fixed icons. Every number is in pixels of the stored image. A version 1 request is packed byte for byte as before, and a version 1 request naming the new sections is refused ([game-pack](docs/game-pack.md#version-2-images-tiles-and-ui)).
- **New: `slice-sheet --scale sheet` keeps the sizes a sheet was drawn at, and `--fit contain` keeps every cut whole.** The default gives each cell's main subject the target height on its own, so the props of one sheet came out the same height, and lets the canvas cut whatever that scale puts past its edge, so a subject wider than the canvas lost its sides. `--scale sheet` takes one factor for the whole sheet (the tallest main subject reaches `--target-height`), so a prop drawn twice as tall stays twice as tall. `--fit contain` lowers the factor until the whole cut fits the canvas, with the main subject still centred on its base; with `--scale sheet` the sheet takes the smallest factor any cell fits at. Every cell of a sheet keeps one canvas size, so a game pack that gives the cells one `display` height keeps their sizes too ([sheet-slicing](docs/sheet-slicing.md#props-and-icons-sheet-scale-and-contain-fit)).
- **New: `slice-sheet` writes `<prefix>slice-sheet.json`.** It names the scale and fit, the sheet's factor and where it came from, and per cell the factor, the main subject's size on the sheet and on the canvas, the cut's box on the canvas, the `anchor` (the centre of the main subject's base, ready for a game pack's `images[].anchor`) and `clipped_px`, the pixels the canvas cut ([report](docs/sheet-slicing.md#report)). A run also prints a line for each cell the canvas cut. The PNGs of a default run are unchanged byte for byte; a sheet with an empty cell now fails before any cell is written, where it used to write the cells before it.
- **New: a 2:3 UI kit sheet holds the whole kit: bar fills, an item slot, a counter plate, on and off dots and a joystick.** `gen --layout-guide ui-kit --aspect-ratio 2:3` draws the square layout's seven frames at the same places and, in a band below them, `hp_bar_fill` and `xp_bar_fill` (`x`), `slot` and `badge` (`xy`), `pip_on`, `pip_off`, `joystick_base` and `joystick_knob` (`none`, so round parts keep their proportions; the guide draws them as circles, and a cut with filled corners, a round part drawn on a square plate, makes the report `needs-review`). `ui-kit` picks the layout from the sheet's shape, so a square sheet is cut, measured and packed exactly as before. Padding is measured only for elements that hold content; fills, dots and joystick parts get `0`. Each fill's report entry has `inside`: its bar frame, that frame's content rectangle and the factor that makes the fill as tall as it, and the report is `needs-review` when the fill's ends would overflow it. `game-pack` (still version 2) stores each fill at exactly its frame's content rectangle, ends kept and middle columns repeated or dropped, with `inside` in its entry, and refuses a fill whose frame is missing or whose ends do not fit ([ui-kit](docs/ui-kit.md#fills), [game-pack](docs/game-pack.md#version-2-manifest)).
- **Behavior change: a 2:3 UI kit sheet that leaves a band element out still makes a kit, without it.** The seven frames (`panel`, `button`, `button_pressed`, `card`, `hp_bar_frame`, `xp_bar_frame`, `timer_badge`) are required: an empty frame box still fails `ui-kit` with `<element>: its box holds no element; generate the sheet again`, now naming every empty frame box at once (`panel, card: their boxes hold no element; …`). The eight band elements (the fills, the slot, the badge, the dots and the joystick parts) are optional: an empty band box leaves that element out. It gets no PNG and no `elements` entry, the report names it under `missing` (every 2:3 report has `missing`, `[]` when nothing is missing), and the command's line ends with `; missing, left out of the kit: …`. `status` stays about the elements that are there. `game-pack` is unchanged: it packs the elements a report has, so a missing element has no `ui.nine_slice` entry and a game falls back for exactly that part. A square sheet's report, elements and packs are unchanged byte for byte, and its report has no `missing` ([ui-kit](docs/ui-kit.md#missing-elements)).
- **Behavior change: `ui-kit` writes a kit whole into a new directory and refuses one that holds anything.** `--out-dir` must not exist yet or be an empty directory. One that holds an earlier kit or any other file is refused with its name and what it holds, and left as it was, where a rerun used to rewrite the kit in place one file at a time and a failure partway left two kits mixed under one report. The kit is written into a hidden directory beside `--out-dir` and renamed into place in one step, so a run that fails or is killed at any step leaves either nothing or the whole kit, and of two runs into one new `--out-dir` the second is refused. A `--report` outside `--out-dir` must be a new path: it takes its name after the rename, in a step that fails when another run took the name first. One step publishes the kit, the rename or, with the report outside, the report's own step: a run that fails or is stopped with Ctrl-C before it takes everything it made back out, and one stopped after it leaves the whole kit and its report, so a report never names a PNG that is not there. A killed run can leave its hidden directory (`.<out-dir>.ui-kit-*`) or report temp beside them, and, with the report outside, the whole kit without its report. A run that is interrupted again while it is removing what it made (for example, a second Ctrl-C during that clean-up) cannot finish the clean-up either: it can leave the same things a killed run leaves, and never leaves part of a kit under `--out-dir`'s own name: `--out-dir` holds nothing or the whole kit ([ui-kit](docs/ui-kit.md#split-the-sheet)).
- **Fix: a UI kit sheet drawn a few percent shorter than its guide is cut instead of refused.** An image model may draw the layout a few percent shorter than the guide; the lower an element, the farther it sits above its box, until its centroid lies in the widened box above its own. That box held two elements and the element's own box none, so `ui-kit` refused the sheet with `<element>: its box holds no element` although every element was drawn. When a box is left empty, `ui-kit` now fits the layout to the drawing (a scale and an offset along x and y, least squares from the box centres to what the boxes hold, repeated until the assignment comes out the same) and assigns the parts again. The fit is kept when it leaves fewer required boxes empty, or as many and fewer boxes in all. A kept fit is in the report as `layout_fit`, and each `box` is then the fitted box. A sheet whose every box holds its element is cut exactly as before ([ui-kit](docs/ui-kit.md#a-drawing-that-drifted-from-the-layout)).
- **Behavior change: a key background painted as a transparency checkerboard is refused before keying.** Asked for a magenta or green key, a model sometimes paints the grey and white checkerboard editors show behind transparent pixels. The matte read the checkerboard as the key and keyed nearly the whole image away, so a UI kit sheet had nothing left to cut. Every chroma key in `gen` now checks the raw first (`chroma.painted_checkerboard`: two greys alternating in squares of one size along a horizontal edge and a vertical edge), and fails with its own reason, `chroma: the background is a painted transparency checkerboard (… px squares along the … edges), not the magenta key the image was asked for; keying it would erase the image — generate it again`, publishing nothing. A ref run whose raw is a drawn checkerboard was keyed before; it is refused now. Key backgrounds, plain or striped grey backgrounds, and a checkered subject inside a key background are not refused ([gen](docs/gen.md#transparent-output--strategy-per-provider)).
- **Tests** — `tests/frames/test_ui_kit.py` (the frames required and the band optional; an empty band box, several, or the whole band leaves those elements out of the report, the PNGs and the pack; an empty frame box, alone, several at once, or on a squeezed sheet (each frame in turn), refuses the sheet by that frame's name; a kit is written into a new or empty directory, and one that holds a kit, any other file or a hidden one, or a file at its path, is refused and left byte for byte as it was; one failure, interrupt or kill injected just before or just after each filesystem step of the publish, with the report in and outside `--out-dir` and `--out-dir` absent or empty (and removed first, as on Windows), leaves nothing before the step that publishes the kit and the whole kit from it on; an interrupt of any kind (SIGINT, SIGTERM through a handler that exits, any other exception) at each step from the rename on, with no function replaced, the same; a sweep in which an interrupt of each kind (SIGINT, SIGTERM through a handler that exits, any other exception) is raised once per run at each line, and at each instruction, of the publish, with the report in and outside `--out-dir` and `--out-dir` absent, empty, or empty and removed first, and every run ends with that interrupt and leaves either the start, with nothing hidden, or the whole kit; a run whose `--out-dir` or outside report another run reaches first is refused and removes what it made; a 2:3 sheet drawn 7 % shorter than its guide, which the layout's own boxes refuse, is fitted and every element is cut whole inside its fitted box; a square report has no `missing`), `tests/gen/test_painted_checkerboard.py` (checkerboards of 8, 12 and 32 px squares at any phase and greys, with elements across two edges, are found; key, plain grey, gradient and striped backgrounds and a chessboard inside a key background are not; `key_transparent` and a `--layout-guide ui-kit` run refuse a checkerboard before the matte runs and publish nothing), and the drawn-checkerboard ref run in `tests/gen/test_gen.py`, which now refuses.
- **Tests** — `tests/background/test_tile_xy.py` (an exact doubly periodic crop, a join that stays visible on one axis names that axis, the cyclic seam), `tests/frames/test_alpha_floor.py` (a haze ring cleared, thin strokes and their anti-aliased edges kept), `tests/frames/test_ui_kit.py` (a synthetic sheet on the layout: insets to the pixel over corner ornaments, a long flourish, a bar's end gem and cap; badge padding inside its ring; a packed kit at four scales keeps ornament fringes out of the stretched middle; a report in, beside, below or above `--out-dir` names the PNGs from its own directory and packs the same bytes), `tests/frames/test_slice_sheet_transparent.py`, `tests/compose/test_game_pack_v2.py` (the manifest field by field, refusals, and a version 1 manifest and page pixels frozen from 2.44.0), `tests/compose/test_game_pack_audio.py` (a version 1 pack with sound keeps its file list, manifest bytes and page pixels from 2.44.0; a version 2 pack with sound adds only its tile files) the `--layout-guide ui-kit` cases in `tests/gen/test_layout_guide.py` (square and 2:3, any other ratio refused), the 2:3 sheet in `tests/frames/test_ui_kit.py` (every element cut from a synthetic sheet; fills fit their frame's content area in the report and, packed at three scales, to the pixel with their ends unchanged; dots and joystick parts never stretch and one drawn on a square plate is flagged; slot and plate insets to the pixel; a square sheet's report, element pixels and packs frozen from before the 2:3 sheet) and the fill cases in `tests/compose/test_game_pack_v2.py`, and `tests/frames/test_slice_sheet_scale.py` (synthetic sheets: drawn heights kept within a pixel with `--scale sheet`, after the alpha cleanup too; a 2:1 subject whole on a square canvas with `--fit contain` while the default still cuts it and says so; one factor every cut fits; sliced cells packed as game-pack `images` keep one size and stand on the reported anchor; refusals).

- Bump audio processing recipe to version 4, measure BS.1770-4 loudness over normalized PCM frames and gated blocks, support a peak-limited loudness goal, normalize loudnorm-unanalysable short clips via limiter passes instead of refusing them, encode AAC using `-aac_coder fast`, record `normalization.pcm_lufs` and `pcm_true_peak_dbtp` required to load version 4 bundles, keep recipe version 3 bundles loadable in game packs by their own rule (target loudness, decoded files re-measured with loudnorm), reject recipe version 1-2 bundles, and reject recipe version 1-3 output directories in `sprite-gen sound` while reusing cached provider audio.
- Bump audio processing recipe to version 3, require `ffprobe` on `PATH`, export M4A with `-movie_timescale 48000`, and validate AAC stream duration with `ffprobe`.
- Add bounded one-frame decoded length tolerance, preserving loop duration, trimming one-shots, and rejecting shorter buffers and undersized overlaps at export.
- Configure loop playback in `createAudioSource` using an internally cached L+1 guard frame buffer while preserving `prepareAudioBuffer` frame contracts.

- Upgraded sound generation and game-pack audio integration to version 2: removed file-level crossfades and boundary tapers in favor of complete normalized exports with integer frame `playback` coordinates, removed legacy loop seconds and taper metadata, and bundled standalone runtime helper `sprite-gen-audio-v2.mjs` for Web Audio loop preparation.

- Added `sprite-gen sound` CLI and game-pack audio integration for ElevenLabs SFX/BGM generation, featuring SQLite request deduplication, cosine loop crossfading, multi-pass FFmpeg loudnorm/alimiter normalization, dual Opus/AAC encoding, and Web Audio loop boundary metadata.

- Added `--cycle once` nonloop motion support with automatic selection for `death`, `fall`, `land`, `cast`, `hurt_hold`, and `transform`, rejection on cyclic states, no seam gate or wrap correction, and a terminal stability gate searching the whole clip for the first stable terminal window ($K = \max(4, \lceil\text{fps} \times 0.2\rceil)$) after action departure (ending at window end, discarding later recovery or shaking), with conservative candidate end pose geometric screening (alpha >= 8 body boxes; height <= 80% of initial for `death`/`fall`, changed geometry for the other named states) and retained cut edge contact validation (ignoring discarded tail).
- Added nonloop-only flags `video-prompt --end-frame final.png` (refuses `--no-last-frame`, prints instructions without image validation) and `video-set --end-frame ITEM=PATH` (checks final canvas dimensions and corner key match initial canvas), with clip cache tracking end-image SHA256 where changed end pins reject until user explicitly `--force` regenerates.
- Added endpoint-preserving strip downsampling with candidate events (`events:[{"frame": n, "name":"motion_peak"}]` using zero-based output cell indices mapped to keyed source frames via `source_cut.samples`) and `once` preview handling, with nonloop cycles insisting on existing `--anchor none` and `--fit state` while rejecting non-none anchor and tight fit.

- **Behavior change: a flag given together with a file that holds the same key wins, and one stderr line says so — for `prepare --request` / `--request-json` and `pixel-draw --spec` alike.** `pixel-draw` refused the pair; `prepare` let the flag win for most keys in silence, and let the request win for three: `--style`, `--cell-size` and `--safe-margin` were dropped when the request carried a style, a cell size or a margin. Now every such key takes the flag and the run says `[prepare] --style, --cell-size given both as a flag and in --request-json: the flag wins` (`gen.base.flags_over_spec`, the one answer, [run contract](docs/run-contract.md#1-pipeline-stages)). A cell flag replaces the measure however the request spelled it (`size`, `width`, `cell_width`). `prepare`'s note no longer lists a request's `subject` among the keys it drops — it was always carried.
- **Behavior change: an answered provider request is sent again only when the provider documents the answer as a request it did not take — one rule for every direct call.** `gen`'s OpenAI and Grok image POSTs, the facing check and the clip POST of the video verbs were never sent again, and `pixel-draw`'s Anthropic transport sent 429 and every 5xx again after 10 s and 30 s. Now each asks `gen.retry`: OpenAI 429 (but its billing codes — a spent credit balance, a spend or usage limit, `insufficient_quota` — which ask you to act) and 503 overloaded; xAI 429; Anthropic 429 (but its spend cap), 500 and 529 — read off each provider's error and rate-limit pages on 2026-10-13 ([a refused request sent again](docs/gen.md#a-refused-request-sent-again)). Not sent again any more: Anthropic 502, 503 and 504 (`timed out while processing`). The wait is the answer's `retry-after` when it is at most 120 s (a longer one is not cut short: the request is not sent again), else the doubling the video polls already waited by, 8 s then 16 s; twice at most, each on stderr as `[gen] openai refused the request (429 rate_limit_exceeded); nothing was made — asking again in 8s (1 of 2)`. A refusal still there ends the call as before, its line saying `sent 3 times, no provider fallback` in place of `no retry or provider fallback`. A request with no answer it could read is still never sent again. `video-set` runs each clip once: its own rerun of a clip whose log said `HTTP 429` (15 s, 30 s) is gone, as it made every POST a second retry loop on top of the first. The video polls and downloads also ask again on `529`.
- **Fix: a refusal whose body broke on the way is no answer on every transport.** `xai.http_json` already said so; `openai-gen` let the broken read escape as a traceback, and `pixel-draw`'s Anthropic transport read it as a refusal with an empty body (and sent a 429 or 5xx again). Both now end as a request that got no answer — the server may have taken it — through the one reader of a refusal's body, `xai.error_body`. An OpenAI answer cut short (`IncompleteRead`) is that line too, not a traceback.
- **Fix: a hex colour is six hex digits, the `#` optional — read and written in one place (`util.hexcolor`).** `recolor`'s map, `slice-sheet --chroma-key`, `handed-check --marker` / `--strap` and the curation view's pixel edits took a sign or an underscore as a digit (`#-1-1-1` read as (-1, -1, -1); a non-hex six-letter edit stopped the request); each now refuses it in its own words, and the curation view skips it as it skips a value of the wrong length. `prepare --chroma-key` also takes `RRGGBB` without the `#`, as the other flags did. Written colours are unchanged: upper case, and lower case in `recolor`'s report and palette draft.
- **Fix: a Python call of `gen.run`, the video verbs' `run`, `pixel-draw`'s `run` or `inspect-motion`'s `run` without a required flag is a TypeError naming it**, as the other verbs' `run(**kwargs)` always said; these four read their keywords by hand and went on with `None`. One reader for all, `util.cli_kwargs.namespace_from_kwargs`, in place of twelve copies and four hand-made readings.
- **Internal: `pixel-draw`'s G8 sizes a frame by the image path's opaque box, `frames.extract.solid_alpha_bbox`**, in place of its own count of opaque rows and columns — the same box, read in one place; `gates.G8` is unchanged.
- **New: `anthropic_api.billed_tokens(usage)` — a Claude call's tokens by the price each is billed at**, the split `cost_usd` priced inline, now one public function `cost_usd` asks: input, output, cache reads, and cache writes by their lifetime (`usage.cache_creation`'s five-minute and one-hour counts; a reply that does not split them wrote for five minutes), keyed as `PRICE_PER_MTOK` prices them, each count as the reply gave it. A caller that prices the calls at rates of its own reads the same split instead of keeping a copy. `cost_usd` is unchanged.
- **Tests** — `tests/gen/test_retry.py` (the rule per provider and status, every direct call asking it, a broken refusal body is no answer on all three transports), `tests/gen/test_flags_over_spec.py`, `tests/test_cli_kwargs.py`, `tests/test_hexcolor.py` (each verb reads through the one function; a second copy turns them red), and the provider, video, video-set and G8 tests moved to the new answers.
- **New (experimental): `sprite-gen pixel-draw` — Claude Opus draws pixel art of 8–48 px directly, as a pixel grid.** The model writes a palette and one line of keys per pixel row (a character grid, or JSON with `--format json`); code checks it and renders it. `--subject` (or `--spec`), `--size` 8–48 (32), `--kind sprite|icon|tile`, `--frames` (up to four, drawn in one call; `--walk-cycle` for contact, passing, contact, passing), `--palette free[:N]|pico8|#hex,…|file`, `--candidates` (2), `--effort medium|high|xhigh` (high), `--batch` (Message Batches, half price), `--max-tokens` (32000 for one frame, 64000 for more), `--pick vision|human`. G1 reads every answer strictly; a row one key too long or short, a key outside the palette or rows missing at the end go back to the model on their own with a column ruler and are spliced in (two rounds), and an answer still broken — or broken beyond its rows — is drawn once more; a single frame written without its `FRAME` line is recovered and recorded. A sprite or icon is drawn to the engine's safe area — `prepare`'s margin, 9.4% of the side, floored, taken from `prepare` rather than copied — as a target for the body's average size, not a wall: at 32 px the body is about the size of the central 26×26 (16 → 14, 24 → 20, 48 → 40), a pose or a detail that needs the room (a raised weapon, a long blade, an effect, a wide stance) may reach past it into the margin, up to the canvas edge, and a sprite's feet stand on the safe area's bottom row, the row `prepare`'s fit grounds a frame on, in every frame; the prompt says it in rows and columns counted from 1. G2 palette, G3 alpha and G6 frames (one ground line, heights within a pixel, no two frames the same — a sprite's or icon's measured inside the safe area, so a staff or a spark that reaches into the margin in one frame is not a taller figure) fail a candidate; G4 tile seam, G5 stray pixels and a walk whose legs barely change sides warn. G3, G4 and G5 ask judgments sprite-gen already makes rather than keep their own, each lifted out of its owner unchanged: G3 is whether a frame's background is really transparent by the verdict `gen` reads a generated raw by (`chroma.alpha_verdict`, out of `classify_raw_alpha`: at least 5 % of the pixels and half the one-pixel border alpha 0 for a sprite or icon, with something drawn and the four corners clear; no alpha-0 pixel for a tile); G4 is `background-tile`'s join measure (`inspect_tile`), asked of a tile along x and along y, and warns with its reasons where it says `needs-review`; G5 counts the lone pixels the YCbCr matte cleanup erases (`frames.extract.isolated_dots`, out of `_cleanup_alpha_ycc`: no opaque pixel among the eight neighbours), so each pixel of a one-pixel diagonal stroke, which touches the next at a corner, is no longer a stray pixel. G8 safe area never fails a candidate: it takes the judgment the pixel snap already makes — one function, `safe_area_fit` in `frames/extract.py`, lifted out of the snap with its lines unchanged — on each frame's opaque box: past the physical cap (the canvas's width, its height less the margin under the floor row, 32×29 at 32 px) warns, a reroll candidate; past the safe area but within the cap is an info line, `info` in the report, which asks for nothing; and a sprite whose feet are off the floor row warns. Of two or more candidates that pass, one vision call at effort low picks (G7). An answer that spent every token thinking is drawn once more one effort lower; a refusal is reported as it came (`refused by the provider's content policy (stop_reason=refusal category=…)`); nothing falls back to another model. The run folder holds `grid.txt` (the truth: edit it and re-render with `--grid`), `palette.json`, `frame-<name>.png` written by an encoder whose bytes depend on the pixels alone, `preview-x<s>.png`, `tiled-3x3.png` for a tile, `anim.gif` for several frames, every call under `calls/` and `report.json` with each call's model, effort, usage (thinking tokens included), price, seconds and request id, the gate results and the repair history. A batch is recorded the moment it is submitted, and `--resume` collects it — and reads every answered call back — instead of buying it twice. It runs on `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, billed per call and said so on stderr; the transport is urllib, so the dependency surface stays Pillow and NumPy — [docs/pixel-draw.md](docs/pixel-draw.md).
- **Fix: a poll xAI answers 429 or 5xx asks the same request again — the clip is bought once.** `video`, `video-extend` and `video-edit` ended the run on any non-2xx poll, after xAI had accepted the request and may bill it, and the line read `(HTTP 429)` — what `video-set`, and any caller that backs off on a refused submission, answers by submitting the clip again: a second clip paid for, the first never published. A poll answered `429`, `500`, `502`, `503` or `504` is now asked again on the same `request_id`, after a wait that doubles from 8 s up to 60 s while the polls are refused and goes back to 4 s on an answer; a stderr note says each, the status in plain digits. The poll timeout (`SPRITE_GEN_VIDEO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`) still bounds the whole poll, a pending poll's waits included (and, below, the downloads). A refused POST is still never sent again, and a `400` or `404` poll still ends the run with its status (a `401` or `403` poll is unfinished, below).
- **Output change: a generation the poll timeout cut short is unfinished, named by its `request_id`.** Polls still pending or still refused at the timeout end the run with a line that names `request_id=<id>`, says the generation may still finish and be billed, and carries no `(HTTP <n>)`, a refusal line's status — a caller that reads `(HTTP 429)` there sends the request again, unbilled only when it was refused. The pending line keeps its front (`still 'pending' after N polls — poll timeout;`). With `--report` the run leaves a `sprite-gen-video-unfinished` report there (`verb`, `auth_source`, `model`, `request_id`, `out`, `polls`, `polls_refused`, `last_poll`, `elapsed_seconds`) and prints nothing on stdout; Python callers catch `GenerationUnfinished`, a `SystemExit`. The success report gains `polls_refused`, how many of its `polls` xAI answered 429 or 5xx (docs/video.md, An unfinished generation).
- **`tests/gen/test_video.py`** scripts the polls: each of the five statuses refused twice, then pending, then done, is asked on the one request with waits 8, 16, 4 and published with `polls_refused` 2; polls refused until a 300 s timeout wait 8, 16, 32, 60 × 4, ask no poll whose wait would reach the timeout and end unfinished after eight with no `(HTTP n)` and no `HTTP 429` on stderr; a pending timeout names the request; a 400 or 404 poll ends at once; a refused POST is sent once; the CLI leaves the unfinished report and an empty stdout. The 500-poll case of the failure table is a 404 now, the status that still ends a run.
- **New verb: `video-resume` asks again for a generation a run left unfinished — by its `request_id`, with no new POST.** A run cut short named the request to ask for again, but no verb could ask for it: a caller had to submit the clip again, a second purchase, or give the clip up. `sprite-gen video-resume --request-id <id> --out clip.mp4 [--report r.json]` polls `GET /v1/videos/{id}` as the generating verbs do — the same retries, backoff and poll timeout — then downloads, verifies and publishes the clip. Nothing is submitted, so nothing is bought twice and the `XAI_API_KEY` route prints no charge notice. Its report is a `sprite-gen-video-report` with `mode: "resume"`, the same `request_id`, `inputs: {}` and `duration_requested: null`; cut short again it is unfinished on the same id, `verb: "video-resume"`, `model: null`; an id xAI does not know ends with its `(HTTP 404)`. The id is refused before any call unless it is letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-`. A resumed clip is checked as an mp4 only — `video-extend`'s length rule needs the input clip (docs/video.md, `sprite-gen video-resume`).
- **Fix: a poll that gets no answer, or whose credential xAI rejects, leaves the request's id.** After xAI had accepted the request, a poll whose connection failed or timed out ended the run with the transport's line and no `request_id`, and a poll answered `401` ended it with its status. A poll that gets no answer is a GET, which buys nothing, so it is now asked again with the refused polls' backoff (a refused poll and a lost one are two failures in a row) and a stderr note, `poll N of generation <id> got no answer (<reason>); asking again in 8s`; lost until the poll timeout, the run is unfinished. A poll answered `401` or `403` — the credential stopped working after the POST, typically an expired login — ends the run unfinished at once, naming the credential's fix (refresh the grok login, or check `XAI_API_KEY`) before the request to ask for. A POST that gets no answer ends as before: no `request_id` came back. `xai.http_json` raises `xai.TransportError` for a request that got no answer, still a `SystemExit` with the same line, the socket's words in `reason`.
- **Output change: the unfinished line names the command that asks again; reports count the polls that got no answer.** The line reads ``ask for request_id=<id> again with `sprite-gen video-resume --request-id <id> --out <out>` instead of submitting a new request``, `request_id=<id>` kept as it was. The success and unfinished reports gain `polls_unanswered`, how many of `polls` got no answer, each asked again; the unfinished report's `last_poll` gains `error`, what the socket said, when its last poll got none (`http` then `null`).
- **`tests/gen/test_video.py`** asks again by id and loses polls: `xai.http_json` raises `xai.TransportError` with the old line for a DNS failure, a timeout and a reset; lost, pending, refused, lost, done is asked on the one request with waits 8, 4, 8, 16 and published with `polls_unanswered` 2; lost until a 30 s timeout waits 8 and 16 and ends unfinished with `last_poll.error`; a lost POST is sent once and is not unfinished; a 401 or 403 poll, on the login or on the key, ends unfinished at once naming its fix; `video-resume` polls with no POST and announces no charge, ends unfinished on the same id when refused, lost or pending past the timeout or when its credential is rejected, ends with `(HTTP 404)` for an unknown id, refuses eight ids that cannot be one before any call, and its CLI writes the resume report, the unfinished report and an empty stdout; the unified CLI routes it under `gen`.
- **Fix: a poll whose answer is cut short, garbled or not JSON is asked again on the same request — the run no longer ends with no `request_id`.** A poll answer that broke after it began — a body that closed before its `Content-Length` (`http.client.IncompleteRead`), a status line that is not one (`BadStatusLine`), a refusal's body cut the same way — escaped `video`, `video-extend`, `video-edit` and `video-resume` as a raw exception, with no `request_id`, no unfinished report and no resume command, so a caller could only submit the clip again and buy it twice; a `200` poll whose body is not JSON ended the run on `xai: response was not valid JSON`, also with no id. Both are now a poll that got no answer it could read, as a lost one is: asked again on the same `request_id` with the same backoff and stderr note (`got no answer (IncompleteRead(10 bytes read, 190 more expected))`, `got no answer (response was not valid JSON)`), counted in `polls_unanswered`, and unfinished at the poll timeout with that reason in `last_poll.error`. A POST so answered still ends at once and is never sent again — no `request_id` could be read: on the transport's line for a broken answer (a raw exception before), on the not-JSON line it always had. `xai.http_json` raises `xai.TransportError` for every answer it cannot read whole, each with the line it always had; `reason` is the `http.client` exception's repr for a broken answer, `response was not valid JSON` for the other.
- **`tests/gen/test_video.py`** answers over a real socket from a local server: `xai.http_json` raises `xai.TransportError` for a body closed 10 bytes into its 200, a 503 body cut the same way, a garbage status line (the transport's line) and a whole non-JSON `200` (the not-JSON line); cut, garbled, not JSON, cut refusal, pending, done is asked on the one request with waits 8, 16, 32, 60, 4 and published with `polls_unanswered` 4; a resumed poll cut short is asked again and published; cut until a 30 s timeout, the CLI leaves the unfinished report with `last_poll.error` the `IncompleteRead` and an empty stdout; a cut POST is sent once and is not unfinished.
- **Fix: a clip that is done and whose download fails leaves the run unfinished on its `request_id` — never a second purchase.** Once the poll said done the clip is made and may be billed, yet a failed download ended `video`, `video-extend`, `video-edit` and `video-resume` with no `request_id`, no unfinished report and no resume command: a refused connection on `video: cannot download the clip: …`, a body cut short of its `Content-Length` (`http.client.IncompleteRead`) or a read that timed out as a raw exception, a refused download on `video: download failed with HTTP <n>`, and bytes that are not an mp4 on `… refusing to publish` — so a caller could only submit the clip again and buy it twice. A download that gets no answer it can read, or that the clip's host refuses with `429`, `500`, `502`, `503` or `504`, is now downloaded again after 8 s and then 16 s, three downloads in all, with a stderr note before each (`download N of generation <id> got no answer (<reason>); downloading again in 8s`, `… was refused (503); …`); one still failing, one refused with another status (a `401` or `403` still asked once more with the bearer token first), or bytes that are not an mp4 end the run unfinished on that id: ``… is done, but its download got no answer (last: <reason>) after 3 attempts; the clip is made and may be billed — ask for request_id=<id> again with `sprite-gen video-resume --request-id <id> --out <out>` …``, no `(HTTP <n>)` on it. `video-resume` polls afresh, so it downloads from the URL the new poll names. `video.http_download` raises `xai.TransportError` (its line still `video: cannot download the clip: <reason>`) for a download that got no answer and `video.DownloadRefused`, a `SystemExit` carrying `status`, for a refused one; `xai.NO_ANSWER` and `xai.no_answer` are the transport's classifier, now shared by both.
- **Output change: bytes that came whole and are not an mp4 are unfinished, not a refusal.** xAI's one content-policy signal is `respect_moderation: false` on the done poll, read before any download; anything else at the clip's URL — an expired link's page, a proxy's error page served `200` — is a fault in delivering a clip that is made, and the `request_id` is the only way back to it. `video-resume` costs a poll and asks for a fresh URL; a refusal left only a new submission. The same URL brings the same bytes, so the run does not download it again itself (docs/video.md, An unfinished generation). `video.verify_mp4` is gone.
- **Output change: reports count the clip's downloads.** The success report gains `downloads`, how many downloads the clip took, the last one whole (1 unless one failed). An unfinished report of a clip that could not be downloaded gains `downloads` and `last_download` — `http` the status the clip's host refused it with, else `null`; `error` what the transport said, or `not an mp4 (no ftyp box)` with the `bytes` that came — and its `last_poll` is the done poll; an unfinished report of a poll is unchanged.
- **`tests/gen/test_video.py`** downloads the clip over a real socket from the local server: a clip body closed 10 bytes into its 76, a read held open past a 1 s timeout and a connection to a port nothing holds are each downloaded three times with waits 8 and 16, and the CLI leaves the unfinished report naming the request with `downloads` 3, `last_download.error` the transport's words and an empty stdout; a cut download, then a `503`, then the whole clip publishes with `downloads` 3; a `503` three times and a `404` once end unfinished with `last_download.http`, no `(HTTP n)`; 34 whole bytes of HTML end unfinished at once with `last_download.bytes`; and a run left unfinished by its downloads is published by `video-resume` on the id its report names, from the fresh URL of a new poll and with no POST. The fake transport's non-mp4 refusal test is that HTML test now. A port held bound and not listening is no refused port on macOS (its SYN is dropped and the connect times out), so the test takes a free port and lets it go.
- **Fix: the poll timeout bounds the clip's downloads and every read — a caller that stops the run at a hard limit gets the `request_id` back before it.** The downloads after a done poll ran outside the poll timeout — up to three, each with its own 120 s read timeout, 8 s and 16 s between — and a poll in flight when the timeout came could read on for its own 120 s. A run could so outlive a limit its caller set a little over the timeout and be killed before it wrote its unfinished report: no `request_id`, and a clip that may be billed could only be bought again. The poll timeout (`SPRITE_GEN_VIDEO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`) is now the one time limit of an accepted request, counted from the POST's send. Every poll and download reads only what is left of it — one that stalls, or whose bytes come too slowly for a read's timeout ever to fire, is cut when it comes (`timed out`) — a poll or download whose wait would reach it is not asked, and the run is unfinished on the `request_id` then: `… is done, but its download got no answer (last: timed out) until the poll timeout (210s, 1 attempt); …`, `… was refused (last answer 503) until the poll timeout (20s, 2 attempts); …`. The POST is not cut by it, its answer being the one way to the id; one answered after the whole timeout is unfinished before any poll (`… was accepted after the poll timeout (600s) had run out, before any poll; …`, `polls` 0). `xai.http_json` and `video.http_download` take `deadline=`, a `time.monotonic()` instant, and read the body a chunk at a time by it (`xai.read_whole`, `xai.time_left`); without one they read as before (docs/video.md, step 3).
- **Output change: the poll timeout ends the run when the next poll's wait would reach it, not with a last poll at it.** That poll would have no time left to be answered. A run cut short by the timeout ends up to one wait before it (4 s while pending, up to 60 s while refused), with one poll fewer: a 300 s timeout of refused polls ends after 8 polls at 296 s, where it asked a ninth at 300 s.
- **Fix: a done poll that names no URL to download is unfinished on its `request_id`.** It ended on `<verb>: generation <id> is done but reported no video url`, with no unfinished report, and a `url` that is not a string or not a web address escaped as a raw exception: a clip xAI says is made, and may bill, left only a second purchase. A done poll with no `http(s)://` URL — none, empty, `null`, not a string, no `video` object — now ends ``… is done, but its poll named no video url to download; the clip is made and may be billed — ask for request_id=<id> again with `sprite-gen video-resume …` …``, the report's `last_poll` the done poll and no `downloads`; `video-resume` polls it afresh. `respect_moderation: false` is still the content-policy refusal it was.
- **`tests/gen/test_video.py`** runs the time limit on the real clock against the local server, with a 1.5 s timeout: a clip download that stalls past it (its read timeout longer than what is left) or comes a byte every 20 ms is downloaded once and cut at the timeout, and the CLI leaves the unfinished report by then (`last_download.error` `timed out`, `elapsed_seconds` under 2.5); a poll that stalls or trickles is cut the same way. A `503` download whose next wait would reach a 20 s timeout is not downloaded again; a POST that took 12 of 20 s leaves the polls 8 s, and one that took 25 s is unfinished before any poll. A done poll with no url, an empty, null or numeric one, one with no scheme or no `video` object is unfinished on its id for `video` and `video-resume`. `xai.http_json` reads a cut, garbled or not-JSON answer as no answer with a deadline as without one. The poll-timeout cases end a poll earlier: refused polls until 300 s wait 8, 16, 32, 60 × 4 and end after 8 polls at 296 s; lost or cut polls until 30 s wait 8 and 16 and end after 3; pending polls until 10 s end after 3. The failure table's done poll with an empty `video` expects the unfinished line.
- **Output change: an `openai` image report carries the response's rate-limit headers — `extra.rate_limit`.** The provider dropped the response headers, so what the account may spend per minute and has left could not be read off real traffic. `extra.rate_limit` now holds every `x-ratelimit-*` header the response carried, names lower-cased and values as sent, and `{}` when none came (docs/gen.md). `openai_provider.http_image` returns the response headers as a third value.
- **`tests/gen/test_openai_provider.py`** answers with headers: the report holds exactly the `x-ratelimit-*` ones among others of any case, and `{}` for a response without any.

## v2.44.0 - A filmed ghost in a loop delivered as cut is given way, or named a take to film again; restoration reads the ghost; the RIFE lane runs every real-RIFE test

**Upgrading from 2.43.0: the restoration contract moves with the version.** Source restoration is now metric `source-restoration-v5` under policy `key-protected-source-copy-one-step-cap-jump-ghost-guard-v1`, and writes repair report `schema_version` 4 and comparison `schema_version` 6 (`sprite_gen.video.restoration`: `METRIC`, `POLICY`, `REPAIR_SCHEMA`, `COMPARISON_SCHEMA`); a consumer that checks these values raises all four with its pin. Nothing `source-restoration-v4` (2.40.0 – 2.43.0) wrote is read as v5: a v4 repair report given to `video-loop-compare` is an input error, and a request whose baseline carries a v4 receipt in its loop report is shared `unknown`, `restoration-receipt-policy-unsupported`. A loop restored under v4 therefore goes on from its origin, not its v4 baseline: the origin itself is still a valid first request (the same five files as origin and baseline). Proposal IDs change with the versions. `video-loop-compare` without the origin and the source (`same-sample-defects-v1`) is unchanged.

- **Fix: the Linux lane's `--rife-only` runs every test of the real RIFE, not one file.** It ran `tests/video/test_rife.py` alone, while `--rife-unmeasured` skips every test that needs the real `rife-ncnn-vulkan` — so a real-RIFE test in another file (2.43.0's in `tests/video/test_crossfade.py` and `tests/video/test_ghost_screen.py`) was measured by neither run. Such a test now carries one mark, `real_rife` (registered in `pyproject.toml`): `tests/conftest.py` skips the marked tests where `rife.locate()` finds no RIFE, and `scripts/linux_lane.sh --rife-only` runs `pytest -m real_rife` over the whole suite, none skipped. A new real-RIFE test is run by `--rife-only` once it is marked. `docs/release.md` says what each run measures.
- **The lane's RIFE-less run fails a skip that `--rife-only` will not run.** `--rife-unmeasured` runs the suite as `pytest --rife-unmeasured`, where `tests/conftest.py` reads every skip — by a skip mark, in a fixture or the test body, or of a whole file at import: one whose test is not marked `real_rife` fails by name, unless `NOT_RIFE_SKIPS` in the same file names it as skipping for an input the lane does not give (the `SPRITE_GEN_CHROMA_REAL_STILLS` stills). Such a skip would be measured by neither run.
- **`tests/release/test_lane_rife_collection.py`** collects the suite twice, once with a stand-in RIFE where `rife.locate()` looks (`SPRITE_GEN_RIFE`, `PATH`, the install root) and once with none: a test the two collections skip or collect differently and that is not marked `real_rife` fails it by name — a test collected only where RIFE is reachable never reaches the RIFE-less run. It also runs a marked test and each unmarked way to skip without RIFE through `pytest --rife-unmeasured`: the marked one skips, every other fails by name.
- **Correction to 2.43.0's cross-fade measure:** "the windows step by half their size, so one lost pixel is counted in up to four of them" holds where the window is an even number of pixels wide. The step is half the width rounded down, so an odd-width window counts one pixel in up to nine. The `rife.crossfade` docstring and `docs/loop-repair.md` section 4 now say "up to four of them (nine where the window is an odd number of pixels wide)". The measure is unchanged.
- **Output change: a filmed ghost with no clean frame beside it is a take to film again — `retake` reason `filmed-ghost`.** Since 2.43.0 `video-cycle-align` delivers a filmed ghost only where the source frames on both sides of its time are ghosts too, and said so in a warning line alone: the loop's `retake` stayed `null`, so a pipeline that films a direction again on `retake` delivered the ghost. Such a loop is now named: its row's `retake` (and the report's, and `video-set`'s) reads `reason: "filmed-ghost"` with `ghost` — the cells `kept`, their frames of `cycle.source/` (`sources`), the highest reading (`ghost_max`) and the screen's `limit` — and `limits.ghost_kept_min` (one cell, `align.RETAKE_GHOST_KEPT_MIN`), with a line "film this direction again (filmed-ghost) — …". `retake` stays one object: `reason` is the first that applies (`held-drawings`, then `filmed-ghost`), the new `reasons` lists every one (`["held-drawings"]` for a held loop as before), and the held numbers stand only where that reason applies. Each `ghost_at` cell that keeps its ghost says why (`kept`: `no-clean-frame`, under any `--between`, or `between-rife` where `--between rife` kept it beside a clean frame — the aligner's choice, not counted). The set is aligned as before (`applied: true`, exit 0). Docs: `docs/loop-repair.md` section 4 (filmed ghost), `docs/video-pipeline.md`.
- **Fix: a made frame given way at a cell that keeps its ghost no longer says the nearer source frame settled it.** Its line said "so the nearer source frame was taken there" where that frame was itself a filmed ghost, delivered for want of a clean one; it now says "the nearer source frame, a filmed ghost, was kept there, the frames beside it being filmed ghosts too". A ghost `--between rife` kept with no clean frame beside it is named "film this direction again", no longer told to align with `auto` for a clean frame that is not there.
- **Output change: a walk delivered as cut gives its filmed ghost way — `video-loop`'s jump repair stage.** A walk filmed in one direction never meets `video-cycle-align`, which takes a clean frame beside a filmed ghost, so its strip, GIF and WebP delivered the ghost as cut: 2.43.0 named it in a `warning:` line and changed no pixel, and only a band wide enough to read as a jump was replaced, by the jump repair, by chance. The ghost screen now reads the cut as filmed, before the jump repair (`ghost_screen` names a ghost the repair replaced, where it was read after it before), and each ghost it names is a round of that repair, first and outside its three-call budget (`why: "ghost"`; the jump rounds now say `why: "jump"`): RIFE's frame between the two frames beside it where both are clean and the frame has no fault (`taken: "rife"`), else the clean frame beside it — the one after, else the one before, the alignment's own choice at a time on a ghost (`interpolation_quality.clean_beside`, `taken: {"source": k}`) — else, both being filmed ghosts too, the ghost kept (`taken: null`). A ghost round also records `neighbours`, `outcome` (`accepted`, `given-way`, `kept`) and `no_proposal` where no frame was made. A cell given way is in `jump_repair.replaced`, so the jumps are read after it and never made beside it, and the source projection reads it as a repair hint. The seam gate reads a cell given way to the frame beside it as filmed (`seam_as_filmed`, frames of the cut): that frame shown twice in a row is a step of nothing and a step of two, and at the wrap — a ghost at the cut's first frame given way to its second, or at its last given way to its first — the step of two would refuse a walk that closes ("the cycle does not close") and the step of nothing would pass a cut that does not. A loop that only gave its ghosts way to the frames beside them is gated on its source frames (`seam_measurement: source-frames`, as filmed); one with a frame made — RIFE's, for a jump or a ghost — on its rendered cells, the cell given way read on the strip the cut makes with that frame as filmed. `--anchor body`'s `seam_ratio_after_anchor` reads the wrap the same way. The report and its summary carry `ghost_given_way` (`frame`, `ghost`, `taken`) and `ghost_kept` (`frame`, `ghost`), and the strip metadata each where it is not empty — `cycle/` is the alignment's `cycle.source/`, whose screen no longer sees a ghost given way, so an alignment carries the record over. A `warning:` line names each ("… a filmed ghost; given way to RIFE's frame between frames 9 and 11", "given way to frame 11 beside it, shown twice in a row", "kept … — film this direction again"). Without RIFE, `--repair auto` still gives each ghost way to the clean frame beside it and says a jump frame was not repaired only where a jump was left (`unmade`: the step, target and score the jump search stopped at; `repair.repair_jumps` with no interpolator no longer raises on a jump); `--repair on` refuses a ghost between two clean frames as it refuses a jump. `--repair off` and the states other than walk and run are unchanged. A loop with no filmed ghost is cut as before: the same cells and strip metadata, its report adding the two records empty. [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md) section 2.
- **Fix: a GIF or WebP that holds a frame shown twice in a row passes its check.** Both formats hold two identical frames in a row as one frame of twice the delay, and `video-loop` checked its GIF and WebP for one frame per cell: a cut with two identical frames in a row — what giving a ghost way to the frame beside it leaves — failed (`walk.gif failed verification: 19 frames, expected 20`). `video-loop` and `video-follow` now check for one frame per run of identical cells, as `video-cycle-align` already did (`loop.shown_runs`), and the restoration's playback check (`playback.verify`) reads such a run as one frame held for the sum of its delays (in hundredths of a second in the GIF, as it stores it); its `strip_indices` still lists every scheduled cell.
- **Output change: source restoration reads the ghost — the ghost guard.** A cell `video-loop` gave a filmed ghost way is a cell the jump repair replaced, and its source frame is the ghost: the source's pixels clear any fault the cell carries, the smear and outline faults measure a frame only against its neighbours, and partial coverage was read only beyond the source reference's own, which is the ghost — so `source-restoration-v4` could propose the cell and accept the ghost back. Now no cell whose source frame the cut's ghost screen names (`interpolation_quality.ghost_screen` on the cropped source frames, at their own size, as the cut read them) is a target; every final cell's faults carry its own ghost reading where the screen reads the loop; and the comparison's new `axes.ghost` reads each cell's own `rife.ghost` on final pixels (`baseline`, `candidate`, `reference`, `threshold` 0.003, `filmed` — the screen), any rise of the excess over the limit `regressed` with `ghost:regressed`. A loop the screen does not read is not judged (`reason: "loop-drawn-part-covered"`). [docs/loop-comparison.md](docs/loop-comparison.md#the-ghost-guard).
- **Contract versions: repair report schema 4, comparison schema 6, metric `source-restoration-v5`, policy `key-protected-source-copy-one-step-cap-jump-ghost-guard-v1`.** A restored cell's pixels are made as in v4. Evidence and receipts of `source-restoration-v4` (2.40.0 – 2.43.0) are refused, never read as v5: a v4 repair report given to `video-loop-compare` is an input error, and a request whose baseline carries a v4 receipt in its loop report is shared `unknown`, `restoration-receipt-policy-unsupported`. The origin itself is still a valid first request. Proposal IDs change with the versions. `video-loop-compare` without the origin and the source (`same-sample-defects-v1`) is unchanged.
- **`tests/video/test_ghost_given_way.py`** draws the walk of `tests/video/test_cycle_align.py` with a band of 0.3 coverage beside the foot: a small one, four times the ghost limit and no jump, delivered as cut before and now given way to RIFE's frame between its neighbours (a stand-in that draws the walk's true frame) or, without RIFE, to the frame after it, the GIF and WebP holding it as one frame; two identical frames in a row passing the check; three ghosts in a row, the middle one kept and named; a wide band that also reads as a jump, named by the screen read before the repair; `--repair off` and `--repair on` without RIFE; an alignment reading the cut given way and carrying its record; with the default seam gate, the walk cut where its wrap is an ordinary step, its ghost at the cut's first frame given way without RIFE or with RIFE's frame a ghost too (refused before, 2.17 times the mean step), at its last given way to its first (read 0.0 before), both beside a frame made, ghosts at its first and last frames at once given way inward (refused before, 2.40), and a cut that does not close whose last frame gives way to its first (delivered before), under `--anchor none` and `--anchor body`; under `--anchor body`, the walk's default, the strip's `seam_ratio_after_anchor` reading a ghost given way at the cut's first frame, its last or both as `--repair off` reads it (read on `cycle/` before: 0.0 at the last); the side walker of `tests/video/test_one_step_cut.py` cut with the application's walk flags (`--anchor motion-auto --size-hold auto --body-height 160`), through a stand-in and through the real RIFE (marked `real_rife`); and the cut's source projection proving every other cell and listing the cell given way, a restoration request reading its playback and proposing nothing there. `tests/video/test_source_restoration.py` adds a source frame that is a filmed ghost the smear and outline faults pass, whose cell v4 proposed first: it is no target, and the ghost restored there regresses.
- **`tests/video/test_ghost_retake.py`** reproduces on the synthetic legs of `tests/video/test_ghost_screen.py` a ghost kept at a whole time (three in a row), one kept at a time made between two (the stand-in RIFE, and the real one making each frame beside a ghost, marked `real_rife`), and a held loop on twos that keeps ghosts too (both reasons, `held-drawings` first); under `--between rife` only the ghost with no clean frame beside it is counted; the fault line names the kept cell as it is.

## v2.43.0 - No loop delivers a ghost frame unmeasured; a cross-fade inside one silhouette is named, not judged

- **Fix: no loop path delivers a ghost frame unmeasured — `video-cycle-align` takes a clean source frame beside it.** A video model filming a walk held on twos or threes sometimes leaves a frame half drawn between two drawings: a band of part coverage where a leg was and is going. The alignment took such a frame as filmed at its own time, never measured, and a frame RIFE made beside it carried the band and passed — `--between auto` judged a made frame only against its two neighbours (`dark_excess`, `outline_loss`), and a neighbour that is itself the ghost leaves nothing in excess. Now every frame delivered is read on its own coverage (`rife.ghost`: part-covered pixels, alpha 0.1–0.9, in regions at least 5 px thick, over the frame's solid pixels; a ghost over 0.3 %, `interpolation_quality.GHOST_WARN`). The source loop is screened first (`ghost_screen` per loop row and in `cycle_align`: `limit`, `thick_px`, the loop's own `level`, `reads`, and each filmed `ghosts` frame of `cycle.source/`); a loop whose median frame is over the limit is drawn part-covered (a glow, a translucent cape) and is not judged. Under `auto` and `nearest` a time on a filmed ghost takes the frame after it (or before it), a made frame that is a ghost gives way to the nearer source frame (fault `ghost`), and where that nearer frame is the ghost the other frame beside the time is taken; `rife` keeps both, named. Each such cell is listed in `ghost_at` (`at`, `source`, `ghost`, `taken`) with a warning line; a ghost with no clean frame beside it is kept and the line says to film the direction again. A loop already the set's length is screened the same way. Made frames in `smear` carry their `ghost` reading.
- **The same screen in the other loop paths.** `video-loop` records the screen of the cut it delivers for a walk or run (`ghost_screen` in its report and summary, a `warning:` line per ghost naming `cycle/frame-NNN.png`): a loop delivered as cut, never aligned, shows its ghost. The jump repair rejects a proposal that is a ghost, its rounds' `original` and `proposal` carrying `ghost`. `video-loop-compare` and `video-loop-repair` keep their versioned `rife.smear` measures and do not read it. Not seen by the screen: a made frame that blends two drawings inside one silhouette with its outline whole — no part coverage to read; the cross-fade reading below names it. A loop the screen does not read (`reads: false`, drawn part-covered) is now said on stderr too: `video-loop` prints a `warning:` line for its cut (and carries `ghost_screen` in its summary), and `video-cycle-align` a line in `warnings` per such loop — before, only the report said so.
- **Named, not judged: a made frame that cross-fades two drawings inside its silhouette.** Where the legs trade places across a gap wider than the flow follows, RIFE can move the coverage and cross-fade the colour — both drawings' boots at part strength inside one whole outline. That frame has no band of part coverage, nothing darker inside the body, and loses its outline only where the art outlines in near-black: the outline rule counts pixels under 70/255 as outline, so the same cross-fade outlined lighter than 70/255 (grey, a light brown) passed every fault — caught or passed by the palette's accident. `rife.crossfade(made, a, b, t)` reads it relative to the source frames' own edges: in windows 8 % of the body's height where the two source frames differ, pixels at their plain blend at the made frame's fraction (in patches 5 px thick) together with the made frame keeping under 60 % of the sources' strong edges there (local range over 7 px, wider than RIFE's blur) — the strong-edge pixels lost there, over the frame's solid pixels (the windows step by half their size, so one lost pixel is counted in up to four of them). Over 0.015 (`interpolation_quality.CROSSFADE_LOOK`, `looks`) a made frame is named to look at: `cycle_align.smear` rows carry `crossfade` and `look`, and a kept frame gets a `warnings` line ("may cross-fade two drawings inside its outline … kept"). It is no fault — `auto` keeps the frame and the retake rule does not count it — because checked against frames labelled by eye it does not part them: on synthetic legs over 32 palettes (outline black, brown, grey or none; boots dark, mid, light or none) the colour-blended, coverage-whole frame reads over the line in 209 of 384 (74 of 96 outlined in grey, where the fault rules catch 2; 11 of 96 drawn without outlines) and a clean drawing between two far-apart drawings in 29 of 384. The jump repair takes such a proposal and names it (its round's `look`, the proposal's `crossfade`, a `video-loop` `warning:` line). `video-loop-compare` and `video-loop-repair` keep their versioned measures.
- **`tests/video/test_crossfade.py`** draws the outlined legs with boots three times the size, the outline grey or black: a frame whose colour run cross-faded inside the drawing's own coverage reads over the line in both, the outline rule catching only the black; the drawing between reads under it, and a clean drawing between legs 13 degrees apart in grey over it (why it is no fault); the alignment keeps a cross-faded frame and names it, where the black one still gives way to its outline fault; an alignment through a stand-in whose colour run blends names each kept frame in a warning line; the jump repair takes a cross-faded proposal and names it; through the real RIFE, where installed, a short step reads no cross-fade; and a loop drawn part-covered is named on stderr by `video-loop` and in the alignment's warnings. `tests/video/test_rife.py` pins the real RIFE's whole verdict on the melted crossing (`outline` and `ghost` one way, `outline` the other, no cross-fade either way), where it pinned only the first fault.
- **`tests/video/test_ghost_screen.py`** draws outlined legs with a band of 0.3 coverage between the feet: the measure reads the band and not a softened edge; the alignment takes the clean frame beside a ghost at a whole time and beside a made frame that carries one, keeps it named under `rife` or with no clean frame beside it, screens a loop already that long and leaves a loop drawn part-covered as before; `video-loop` names the ghost in its cut; the jump repair rejects a ghost proposal; and through the real RIFE, where installed, no resampled cell carries the filmed ghost.

## v2.42.0 - A set's directions on one sheet, standing on one ground line; a loop whose strip as cut is missing is refused by name

- **New `sprite-gen video-set-sheet`: the directions of one set on one sheet, standing on one ground line.** Each direction is cut on its own and a strip is the union of its own cycle's bodies, so laid side by side the directions stand at different heights and cell sizes. `video-set-sheet --loop-dir <dir> --view <view> …` (the views as `video-cycle-align` takes them; a strip turned over to face the other way is a direction like any other) writes `<name>.sheet.png` and `<name>.sheet.json`: a row per direction in the compass order S, SW, W, NW, N, NE, E, SE whatever order the loops are given in, a frame a column; one cell that holds every pixel of every cell of every direction, centred on the pivot each strip declares (`anchor`) or else its bottom centre, so every cell's pivot is its bottom centre; one ground line, `baseline_y`, the lowest body pixel (alpha 8 and over, as `video-loop` reads a body) of any cell — a fainter pixel, a soft shadow, is kept in the cell below the line and is no body — (a direction whose strip leaves room under its feet stands that much above it, as cut: `body_bottom_y`); and every row as cut, pixel for pixel — nothing is scaled direction by direction, one size across a set is `video-loop --body-height`'s. `--center <still>` stands a transparent still (the set's base) with its body on the same line at the rows' standing height (the median of their `body_h`, against the still's own as `video-loop` reads one), centred, in `<name>.center.png`. Loops of different lengths, in frames or frame time, are laid all the same, the shorter rows' last cells empty, with `same_length: false` and a warning. The record carries `order`, `columns`, `cell_w`, `cell_h`, `baseline_y`, `anchor`, `same_length`, per row its `code`, `view`, `frames`, `delay_ms`, strip `name`, `input`, `strip_sha256`, `body_h` and `body_bottom_y`, and the centre's `png`, `input`, `sha256`, `standing_h`, `body_h`, `scale` and `box`. A direction given twice, a front or back view with a side, a `--view` count other than the loops', a loop directory without one strip and its sidecar, an opaque centre still and a centre beside rows without `body_h` are refused before anything is written. The loop directories are only read. Every other verb writes what 2.41.0 writes. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#7-one-sheet-for-a-set--video-set-sheet).
- **`tests/video/test_set_sheet.py`** lays synthetic strips of different sizes, one standing above its strip's bottom and one with a declared pivot: the rows come out in compass order from any order given, every cell is its strip's cell pixel for pixel with no alpha lost, the lowest body pixel of any cell is the ground line and the floating direction stands its gap above it, a soft shadow under the feet is kept below the line with no alpha lost, the cell is the bodies' union about the pivot and touches it where they do, and a declared pivot lands on the cell's centre. A sheet laid by hand — every strip centred in one wide cell and stood on its bottom, the ground line the lowest pixel of any cell — is the engine's sheet cropped by one offset, cell for cell, its ground line the same line. A centre still drawn twice the rows' size and wider than them, with a faint shadow, stands its body on the line at the rows' median standing height, centred, the cell made to hold it and its shadow. Rows of 12 and 10 frames, or of one length at two frame times, are laid and said to differ; each refusal writes nothing.
- **Fix: `video-cycle-align` refuses a loop whose strip as cut is missing, by name, before any loop of the set is touched.** Since 2.41.0 a loop's first alignment reads its strip as cut (`<name>.strip.png`, or `follow.source.png` where a follow-through moved it) to record `cycle_align.origin`. A loop directory holding `cycle/` and its `strip.json` without that PNG ended in a `FileNotFoundError` traceback, with no report and `cycle.source/` already copied into every loop of the set. It is now refused as a loop cut before `cell_height_cap` was recorded is: `video-cycle-align: <loop dir>: no <name>.strip.png — …`, exit 1, every loop of the set file for file as it was (under `video-set`, that state's alignment fails and its loops stay as cut). It is still not aligned without its origin, which `video-follow --read-on` carries regions by: put the strip back, or cut the loop again. A loop aligned before keeps the origin it recorded and is aligned again without its strip, as before. [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md) section 4.
- **`tests/video/test_cycle_align.py`** takes the strip out of one loop of a synthetic set: the refusal names it, and both loop directories are as they were, with no `cycle.source/` and no report. A loop aligned once is aligned again without its strip, its origin kept.

## v2.41.0 - Regions read on the cut follow the loop aligned from it, cell for cell; a floor on the stretch, by direction and never above the fold rule; the bend and the seam written down

- **New `video-follow --read-on <sha256>`: the strip the regions were read on, and the reading carried from it.** The regions of a follow-through are where somebody looked, in the first cell of the strip they were read on. `video-cycle-align` turns a loop to start on a foot strike, so its first cell is another, and `video-follow` given the same regions read the motion again from that cell 0: the regions landed on another place of the body, and a stretched loop moved them by a motion read anew. `--read-on` names the strip they were read on by its file's sha256 (as cut, before any follow-through). This loop's own strip as cut is `same`: the follow-through is the one without the flag, byte for byte. The cut this loop was aligned from is `transported`: each cell is carried by the cut's reading of the cut's cell it is, the regions stay on the cut's cell 0, and the damped mass is solved on the loop's own order, length and frame time — a turned loop gives the cut's follow-through cell for cell, a loop stretched by the nearer frame the new cycle's offsets on the cut's motion. A cell made between two frames of the cut, or one that is not the cut's cell pixel for pixel (a crop or a scale changed and nothing records how, or a cell drawn again), is refused `uncertain`: a made cell has no reading of its own, and none is taken from the nearer frame by rounding (a cut made before its strip recorded its crop is judged by its cells' pixels alone). Any other strip is refused `no-match`. Without `--read-on` nothing of this is read or written and the record holds no new key; with it the record carries `read_on` (`sha256`, `relation`, and for `transported` its `policy` and `cut_cells`). `video-follow-inspect` takes it too. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#regions-read-on-the-cut-followed-on-the-aligned-loop----read-on).
- **Output change: `video-cycle-align` records the cut it aligns from and where every cell is from.** `cycle_align.cells_from` holds, per cell of the rebuilt strip, `{"source": i}` (frame i of `cycle.source/`, as many times as it is taken) or `{"between": [i, j], "t": t}` (a frame made between two); the report's loop rows carry it too. The first alignment of a loop also records the strip as cut, read before anything is rewritten (`cycle_align.origin`: its file's sha256 — `follow.source.png`'s where a follow-through had moved it —, cell size, scale, crop origin, `sample_indices`, each cell's pixels by sha256, and the body's motion as `video-follow` reads it there), and every later alignment keeps it as it is. The strip, cycle frames, GIF and WebP are unchanged; the aligned meta gains these two keys. A loop aligned before this release has no origin and gets none: its cut is no longer there to read. [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md).
- **New `video-follow --stretch-floor D`: a least area every moved pixel keeps, read by the way the region moves.** The fold rule keeps a region from folding and so lets its steepest pixel take nearly nothing of the source. A move (ox, oy) of a cos² region leaves 1 − (π/2)·hypot(ox/rx, oy/ry) of a pixel's area; each region takes the largest gain, in steps of 0.01, that keeps D in every cell, and never one above the fold rule's — where the direction would allow more (a region moving along its long axis) the fold rule's gain stands, so the floor only lowers. Under it the fold rule's handling stands: a region taken under 1 is held and named, every region held is refused, and `--on-fold refuse` refuses a move under the floor. It is opt-in with no default value: without it the fold rule alone decides, byte for byte, and a floor the move already keeps draws the same strip, GIF and WebP. The record carries `stretch` (`policy`, `floor`, per region `per_gain`, `least_cell`, `rule_gain`, `floor_gain`, `gain`, `held`, `least_area`, `under_measured`), and stderr names what lowered a region, the fold or the floor. On an aligned loop, with `--read-on`, the floor is read on the cut's motion, so a turned loop is lowered, held or refused as the cut is. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#a-least-area-each-move-leaves----stretch-floor-d).
- **Output change: `video-follow-inspect` writes schema 2 — the bend and the seams beside the least area.** A move stretches the picture (`jacobian_min`, D), tilts a line by its steepest slope, |offset|·π/(2·smaller radius) (`bend`, G, with `bend_deg` = atan G; the same number as `fold_ratio`, named for what it does — at 1 a line tilts 45°), and where two regions overlap bends it where the larger move changes hands. `cells[k].seams` and the record's `seams` give, per pair of moving regions that meet, the largest jump of the move's gradient on that curve and its angle (`jump`, `deg`, the point `at`, the `cell`); a held region has none. `regions[i]` adds the largest `bend` and `bend_deg`. `inputs.settings` adds `read_on` and `stretch_floor`, `inputs.policy` the names of the ways a reading is carried and a floor kept, both in `input_id`; and `carry_basis` says what carries a region (`outline-lay`) and that whose pixels it holds is `unverified`. `video-follow`'s own record changes only where a new flag is given, and then also carries `carry_basis`. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#inspecting-a-follow-through--video-follow-inspect).
- **`tests/video/test_follow_read_on.py` and `tests/video/test_follow_stretch_floor.py`** use synthetic strips. A runner turned by `video-cycle-align` and followed with `--read-on` is the cut's follow-through cell for cell, and the same turned loop followed as before moves other pixels; a loop stretched by the nearer frame is carried cell for cell, duplicates and all; made cells, a crop that narrowed when a frame was dropped and a cell drawn again are refused `uncertain` with the loop left as it was; another clip and an unknown or malformed `read_on` are refused; a floor on a turned loop decides as on the cut. Every cell keeps the floor; where the fold rule is stricter its gain stands, where the floor is it lowers; a floor already kept draws the same bytes, and a floor of 0 is the fold rule, not the direction alone. A lowered round region bends a line just under 45°; two overlapping regions record a seam at the point where their weights are equal, its jump the norm of the gradients' difference there. Two strips alike in alpha whose chest patch moves differently are carried alike and say the region is unverified, and an arm added to the outline carries nothing.

## v2.40.0 - A restored cell that brings back the jump the repair took out is not an improvement

- **Output change: source restoration's comparison reads the jump around the restored cell (`axes.jump`).** A cell the restoration rebuilds is one the jump repair replaced, because a step into or out of its source frame jumped.
