The source frame's pixels clear the in-between's melt and can bring that jump back, and the comparison read only the melt. `video-loop-compare` (and the preview in `video-loop-repair`) now scores the final cells of both loops with the jump repair's own step score (`repair.jump_scores`: per step, the change of coverage over the whole body and over the hair behind it, each over its median in the loop, the larger of the two), on the source cells' union box and with the `facing` the loop report's `jump_repair` recorded. A changed cell reads the larger score of the step into it and the step out of it; more than the baseline's is `regressed`, reason `jump:regressed`, as on every other axis. No bound is read (`JUMP_RATIO` included) and no constant is added: the baseline is the measure. A cell the jump repair made is now restored only where its in-between jumped more than the source frame does, as when it lost coverage; where the in-between was the smoother step and only its drawing melted, the delivered in-between stays. The guard reads coverage, not colour. `axes.jump` carries both loops' step scores and their whole-body and hair parts, the facing, the hair box, the box and, per changed cell, its two steps and both readings. A loop report whose jump repair names replaced cells but no facing (`right` or `left`) is shared `unknown`, `jump-repair-facing-unverified`; a loop whose repair replaced nothing needs none and stays a no-op. [docs/loop-comparison.md](docs/loop-comparison.md#the-jump-guard).
- **Contract versions: comparison schema 5, metric `source-restoration-v4`, policy `key-protected-source-copy-one-step-cap-jump-guard-v1`.** The repair report stays schema 3, and a restored cell's pixels are made as in 2.39.0. Evidence and receipts of `source-restoration-v3` (2.39.0) are refused, never read as v4: a v3 repair report given to `video-loop-compare` is an input error, and a request whose baseline carries a v3 receipt in its loop report is shared `unknown`, `restoration-receipt-policy-unsupported`. The origin itself is still a valid first request. Proposal IDs change with the versions.
- **`scripts/dev/source_restoration_demo.py`** still ends with both requests `improved` and the third a no-op, its 25 checks unchanged: each melted in-between there left the feet a pale ghost, a larger jump than the source frame's. Its stand-in loop report now records the jump repair's `facing`, as `video-loop` writes it.
- **`tests/video/test_source_restoration.py`**, facing right and left: a restored cell whose source frame stands six pixels to one side of its neighbours clears the melt and is `regressed` with `jump:regressed` alone, while the other proposal stays `improved`; a restored cell that gives the steps back the source's reads no higher than the in-between; the axis is the jump repair's own score on the source cells' box. A record without a facing, or with another word, is shared `unknown`, and a loop with nothing replaced is still a no-op. Receipts and evidence of v3 are refused.

## v2.39.0 - A damaged cell is restored from its own source frame; a faulty in-between is no longer adopted; a follow-through is shown in every cell

- **Output change: a jump repair adopts RIFE's in-between only when it has no interpolation fault.** In a walk or run loop (`video-loop --repair auto|on`), the frame that breaks a jump used to be replaced by whatever RIFE drew between its two neighbours. RIFE's frame is now a proposal, measured against those neighbours with `rife.smear` and adopted only without a fault: a smear inside the body (`dark_excess` over 0.001), a lost outline (`outline_loss` over 0.05) or an empty frame keeps the filmed frame. These are the bounds cycle alignment already used, now one policy for both (`sprite_gen/video/interpolation_quality.py`). The limit of 3 (`MAX_REPAIRS`) counts interpolation calls, rejected proposals included; a rejected target is not tried again in that run, and a target next to a frame already made is skipped while an independent one remains, where it used to end the search. The report's `jump_repair` adds `attempts`, `blocked` and, per round, `target`, the `original` and `proposal` measurements, `faults` and `outcome` (`accepted` or `rejected`). A rejection keeps the filmed frame and its jump: it is not a verdict on the loop. [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md).
- **Output change: a loop records the source it was cut from.** `video-loop`'s report adds `source` (one sha256 over the ordered keyed PNGs' own sha256s, their count and the fps, read before cleanup, size hold and anchoring), `producer` (a fingerprint of the measuring code), for a walk or run `gait` (cut-local silhouette signals, `status: "unverified"`: they do not identify the character's own feet), and under `--anchor none` or `body` `source_projection` (the cleanup recipe, the body ramp and each frame's pixel digest before the jump repair). The strip's meta adds `sample_indices`, `source_rect` (null under `--anchor feet`) and `source_cut` (the source, the cut's start and length, and the source frame of each cell). The cells, the GIF and the WebP are what 2.38.0 writes but for the jump repair above.
- **New `sprite-gen video-loop-compare`: the loop shown now against a candidate, read from the final files.** It reads each side's final strip and meta (after `video-follow`, if that ran) and its loop report for provenance only, never for a seam score or a pass, and writes a `sprite-gen-video-loop-comparison` (schema 1, `same-sample-defects-v1`): the sha256 and size of every file as given, a digest of the decoded cells, and per axis the two values and whether they compare. It orders only defects measured at the same source samples, timing, crop and scale: smear and outline loss frame by frame against each frame's actual neighbours, without averaging a worse frame away, and the seam over the native canvas; a worse axis is `regressed`. `improved` also needs every sample's coverage byte for byte the same, and colour changes only in frames that failed the interpolation bounds and now pass them. Another cut, an aligned strip without a proven source mapping, missing or inconsistent evidence and changed coverage are `unknown`; equal pixels and timing are `non_regressing`, never `improved`; the gait stays `unverified`. Every verdict, `unknown` included, exits 0; invalid input, disagreeing geometry and I/O failures are errors. An application that replaces a result on its own should take only `improved`. [docs/loop-comparison.md](docs/loop-comparison.md).
- **New source restoration: `video-source-manifest`, `video-frames --source-manifest`, `video-loop-repair`, and `video-loop-compare` given the origin and the source.** A cell the loop report lists as made by the jump repair, which carries a smear or outline fault that the source frame at the same time does not, is rebuilt from that source frame, with no interpolation. In that cell a pixel that differs from the source becomes the source's, unless the source's pixel would raise the key-colour guard there: where that excess is exactly one step that the cell's resample made out of source pixels with none, the source's pixel is written with the keyed channels one step lower and its own coverage; anywhere else the delivered pixel stays, and if that leaves a coverage the source does not have, the proposal ends `unknown` and writes nothing. Nothing is blended, moved or taken from another frame; the cut, the metadata bytes, every other cell and the GIF and WebP schedules stay as delivered. `video-source-manifest` (or `video-frames --source-manifest` with `--reference`) receipts the clip, the canvas, the frames report, the ordered keyed PNGs and the clip's presentation timestamps. `video-loop-repair` takes the current five files (strip, meta, loop report, GIF, WebP), the origin's (the five the first restoration started from; on a first request the same five again) and the source, and writes one candidate into a new `--out-dir` and a repair report, for one of up to three independent cells (`--proposal-index`). Every request rebuilds the current loop from the origin and the source and recomputes the receipt kept in the loop report rather than trusting it. `video-loop-compare` with the same origin, source and repair evidence measures the five final files again: `improved` needs the one changed cell to be exactly that copy with the source's coverage, a measured fault cleared, no other cell, metadata byte or playback interval changed, and no protected axis worse; key excess is counted per pixel, so less of it elsewhere does not hide new spill. A correspondence the engine cannot prove exactly (legacy evidence it cannot explain, a follow-through, cycle alignment, size hold, motion anchoring) is `unknown`, not a guess; source, origin or evidence files that are missing or changed are an error. Only a final `improved` is grounds to adopt a candidate. Repair report schema 3, comparison schema 4, metric `source-restoration-v3`, policy `key-protected-source-copy-one-step-cap-v1`; evidence of the two unreleased drafts (`source-restoration-v1`, `-v2`) is refused, never read as v3. The cell resample is unchanged and caps nothing; it now publishes the source pixels each cell pixel is mixed from (`resample.mix_windows`), which the restoration reads. [docs/loop-comparison.md](docs/loop-comparison.md#restoring-the-active-cut-from-source).
- **A synthetic example, end to end: `scripts/dev/source_restoration_demo.py`.** It draws a small walker (twelve keyed frames at twice the cell's size, and a clip of them over green for real timestamps) and a stand-in for a delivered loop whose cells 4 and 9 are in-betweens that melted: outline and two-colour belt drawn in the body's colour, feet a pale ghost. From there only the verbs above run: `video-source-manifest`, two `video-loop-repair` requests, each compared `improved` by `video-loop-compare`, the second on the first's adopted files with the same origin, and a third that is a byte-exact no-op. It ends with an error unless every other cell, the metadata bytes, the GIF and WebP schedules and the origin's files are unchanged, each restored cell has the source's coverage and its outline fault cleared, no key excess is introduced, and the belt's one-step excess, which the resample makes where its two colours meet, is capped in each restored cell. Its `summary.json` holds no path or time. It is a new synthetic example, not a measurement of any delivered loop; the GIFs below are its two engine GIFs with each pixel drawn 3 × 3.
- **`tests/video/test_loop_compare.py`, `tests/video/test_source_restoration.py`, `tests/video/test_loop_repair.py` and `tests/video/test_strip_resample.py`** use synthetic loops. Identical pixels are never `improved`; an outline restored with the bob kept is `improved` and its inverse `regressed`; a follow-through is measured from the final pixels; another cut with a better report ratio, a flattened pose and flattened colour motion cannot win. Restoration keeps normal motion and timing, offers the next independent proposal within the budget, is a no-op on a clean loop, refuses forged or changed source receipts and tampered final files, keeps key spill out while restoring the rest of the cell, ends a proposal whose protected pixel has another coverage, caps a one-step excess the resample made and keeps a deeper one or one a source pixel carries, and rebuilds every later request from the origin. A blocked worst jump does not hide an independent one; faulty proposals keep the originals and use the call budget; with the real RIFE (`tests/video/test_rife.py`) an in-between that loses the outline is rejected and the filmed middle frame kept. The resample's published windows hold every pixel its colour filter mixes, and a mix can carry more key hue than the colours under it.
- **New `sprite-gen video-follow-inspect`: where a follow-through carries each region in every cell, with nothing moved.** `video-follow` is given an ellipse in the strip's first cell and carries it with the body into every other; inside the carried ellipse every pixel moves, whatever it is a picture of. For the first cell somebody looked. For the others nobody did: an arm that swings across a chest is inside the chest's ellipse there and moves with it, and so does a sleeve of the chest's own colour, which no count of colours finds. Where the ellipse lies in those cells was written nowhere — the record keeps the body's bob and the strip's rounded offsets, from which the carry cannot be worked back for a strip of more than thirteen cells, and `--board` draws two cells. `video-follow-inspect` takes `video-follow`'s arguments (all but `--board`), reads the strip as cut and solves it with the same code, and writes into `--out-dir` `follow-inspect.json` and two boards of every cell. The record has, per cell, how far the regions are carried (`carry_px`, whole pixels) and per region the carried ellipse, its own offset and reach, how near that is to folding it, the least area of the source a pixel of the moved cell takes (`jacobian_min`: 0 where the picture stops and one line of the source is drawn across many — a region lowered by `--on-fold lower` is drawn nearly stopped where it moves most, not folded and stretched), the pixels the ellipse covers and how many are body, and the pixels the move changes; per region its gain and whether it is held; and the follow-through as `video-follow` records it. It names what it was read from: the strip's and the meta's sha256, the regions, the settings, the way the motion is read (`follow.POLICY`) and the engine's version, and `input_id`, one sha256 over those — the same before and after `video-follow` is run on the loop, another after a new cut, an alignment, a changed region or setting. It holds no path and no time: the same loop asked the same way writes the same record byte for byte. `follow-inspect.source.png` has the cells as cut and `follow-inspect.moved.png` the cells as `video-follow` would write them, each region's carried ellipse ringed just outside it; they are two files so that whoever says what is inside an ellipse can read the cells as cut alone, and each has a `pixels_sha256` over its pixels beside its file's. **The engine does not read what is inside an ellipse and does not guess it**: every region is `ownership: "unknown"` in every cell, from its centre to its rim, and `video-follow` still moves every cell. The loop directory is only read — not the strip, not its meta, not the `follow.source.png` that `video-follow` keeps — and an `--out-dir` at or under it is refused; what `video-follow` refuses, the inspection refuses in the same words under its own name. `video-follow` writes what 2.38.0 writes: strip, GIF, WebP, record, printed summary, messages and `--help`, byte for byte. Its body is now `follow.read_request` (the request, read and checked), `follow.solve` (an `Answer`: the motion, each region's gain, every cell's move) and the writing, and the inspection reads the first two; `follow.move_field` and `follow.region_weight` are the move and the weight `move_regions` draws with. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#inspecting-a-follow-through--video-follow-inspect).
- **`tests/video/test_follow_inspect.py`** draws a figure whose head and torso sway by whole pixels, across once a cycle and down twice: the carry of every cell is the sway as drawn, and the ellipse is carried by it. The record's `follow` is what `video-follow` then writes to the strip's meta; every pixel `video-follow` changed is inside the carried ellipse the record gave, counted alike; each cell is on both boards at its box, as cut and as written, with the ring only on the board pixels just outside the ellipse. Three regions take a gain each, and the one held is carried and ringed and moves nothing; regions given in the other order keep their numbers and swap their places. The loop directory is the same before and after, byte and time, as cut and after a follow-through, and a loop inspected first is followed byte for byte as one that was not. The same loop asked twice writes the same three files; after `video-follow` the id is the same; a cycle turned by one cell, one more pixel in one cell, another delay or another key in the cut's record is another id; so is each of gain, freq, zeta, on-fold and every number of a region, and not the boards' scale or columns. What `video-follow` refuses is refused in the same words and writes nothing. The least area is the steepest of the weight along the move, to 0.05, and under 0.15 for a region lowered to its limit. A forearm of the torso's own colour across the chest in five cells leaves the carry and the record's `follow` as the bare figure's, is on the board of the cells as cut inside the ring, moves with the chest on the other board, and is marked in no cell.

| As delivered: cells 4 and 9 are melted in-betweens | After two `video-loop-repair` requests, each compared `improved` |
|---|---|
| ![A synthetic walker as delivered, two cells melted](https://github.com/aldegad/sprite-gen/releases/download/v2.39.0/source-restoration-before-x3.gif) | ![The same walker with both cells restored from their source frames](https://github.com/aldegad/sprite-gen/releases/download/v2.39.0/source-restoration-after-x3.gif) |

## v2.38.0 - A body that is not one person stands square from behind and walks from behind without turning

- **Output change: a body that is not one person stands square from behind, and walks or runs from behind without turning.** With `--body-plan` (a quadruped, a body without legs, a scene of several figures), the back still's view sentence (`STILL_VIEW_TEXT_ANY_BODY["back"]`, read by `still_view_text` and `gen --direction back`) now also says the body is square to the viewer and symmetric left to right: the back of the head centred above the body, neither side showing more than the other, any tail rising from the middle. A walk or run seen from behind says, after what the body stands on, that it stays level, square and straight the whole time, never turning or angling to either side so neither side comes into view, and does not sway from side to side, any tail held raised where it is with only its tip swaying (`BACK_GAIT_TEXT_ANY_BODY`; for a scene, every figure), in the built-in sentence and after a caller's `--motion` alike. A four-legged back still drawn from a three-quarter reference had come out turned, its head to one side and its tail to the other, and the walk filmed from it turned further until a flank showed and swayed; with both sentences the still was drawn straight behind and the walk held square. A person's prompts are unchanged, byte for byte; the front, side and diagonal views and every other state are unchanged.
- **`tests/video/test_body_plan.py`** holds the horse's back still and back walk as written, and checks that the square sentence comes after what the body stands on in a walk or run seen from behind, for a quadruped, a body without legs and a scene, with the built-in sentence and a caller's paragraph, and in no other view or state and in no person's prompt.

## v2.37.0 - A lead-in or a stray speck no longer costs a walk its loop; follow-through reads the body and moves each part on its own gain; a diagonal still turns its body; the same cut on every machine

v2.36.0 was cut but not published on its own: its release was held for a cut that differed by machine, fixed here. This release is the first to carry its changes too — see `v2.36.0` in [CHANGELOG.md](https://github.com/aldegad/sprite-gen/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md).

- **Output change: a diagonal still turns its chest or back and its legs, not only its head** (`STILL_VIEW_TEXT["front_diagonal"]`, `["back_diagonal"]`, read by `still_view_text` and `gen --direction`; an app that draws its own still takes it too). The sentences said the turn, the head and the feet, and a still that turns only its head and shoes, the chest (back) and the legs standing as from the front (straight behind), meets those words. They now say what that angle shows: not a front (back) view with only the head turned, the middle of the chest (back) and of the waist about three quarters of the way across the body toward its far edge, the near shoulder and side seen broad, the far shoulder and arm partly hidden, the legs at the same angle with the far foot set a little higher and partly behind the near leg, and not to follow a reference picture's angle. `STILL_VIEW_TEXT` takes `{other}`, the side opposite `{facing}` (the near side of a front diagonal, the far side of a back one); facing left is the same sentence with the sides swapped. The clip's view sentences (`VIEW_TEXT`, `VIEW_MOTION_TEXT`, `GAIT_HOLD_TEXT`) and the body plan's (`STILL_VIEW_TEXT_ANY_BODY`) are unchanged. The words were chosen by a before-and-after comparison on the still model; its measurements are kept in private evidence and none is recorded here ([video-pipeline](docs/video-pipeline.md#2-clip--sprite-gen-video)).
- **`tests/video/test_video_pipeline.py`** holds both diagonal still sentences, facing right and left: the chest (back) and hips turn with the head, where the middle of the body sits, the near side broad, the far shoulder, arm and foot on the far side, the reference picture not followed, and facing left the same sentence with the sides swapped. **`tests/gen/test_prompt_freeze.py`** lists the two 2.22.0 sentences and the words measured in their place (`MEASURED`): the still prompts that hold the old words are compared with the measured ones, those prompts and no others differ from 2.22.0, and a measured sentence reworded again fails as any other prompt would.

- **Fix: a key-tinted rim joined to the subject at the edge is the subject.** The edge check split its contact pixels by their raw colour alone: the key's hue was leftover background. A body that reaches an edge band brings its antialiased rim there first — hair tips, the crown's outline, the key blended into them — and on a frame where only that rim was in the band, `--allow-subject-edge-contact` refused the clip as leftover background. Each key-tinted contact pixel is now read with the piece of the keyed frame it is in (its opaque pixels, corners joining them, the pieces `drop_specks` reads): it is `residual` only when nothing in its piece is off the key's hue — background on its own — and `subject` when it is joined to the subject. A frame with such a rim in the band passes with `--allow-subject-edge-contact` and is recorded as the subject's contact; refused by default, it says the subject was framed too tight, and a mixed refusal no longer counts the rim's frames as carrying leftover background. Key-coloured background apart from the body is refused as before. A clip with no key-tinted pixel in a band, or none joined to the subject, keys and reports as before, byte for byte. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#3-frames--extract-key-check-the-edges).
- **`tests/video/test_edge_speck_lead_in.py`**: a body that rises until only the key-tinted rim over its crown is in the top band was refused as leftover background with the subject allowed; now it passes as the subject's contact, and by default is refused as framed too tight. A strip of the key's colour against the edge, apart from the body, is still refused as leftover background. `tests/frames/test_chroma_key_relative.py`: Grok's magenta in the band is residual cut off from the rest, and the subject's when joined to it.

- **Output change: `video-canvas --shape wide` gives a walk or run no head-room.** A forced-wide canvas gave every state a jump's 35 % above the still, and above a square still that head-room set the width as well (2800 × 1575 for 1024 × 1024, 1216 columns in front of the still); a walker that small in its frame is one a video model may reframe. A walk or run forced wide now has a row of its own (`canvas.GAIT_WIDE`, read through `STATE_SHAPE_CANVAS`): 28 % in front, 20 % behind, nothing above — 1969 × 1108 for a 1024 × 1024 still. A jump, and every other state forced wide, keeps the forced-wide row (`WIDE_OVERRIDE`) and its canvas byte for byte; `--headroom` still sets the room above. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#1-canvas--the-input-frame-decides-the-output-frame).
- **`tests/video/test_edge_speck_lead_in.py`**: a walk or run forced wide is 1969 × 1108 with nothing above, a jump still 2800 × 1575.

- **Fix: `video-loop` leaves a walk's lead-in out of the search.** A video model may reframe a small subject in a clip's first frames — the body grows or shrinks by a third or more in under half a second, then walks at its new size. Searched with those frames, the motion analysis read the reframing as the clip's motion and lost the walk's repeat, and the gait fallback, scaling back by a line through the clip, read the step as steady growth and shrank the walk. A walk or run's sizes are now read one cycle on (`gait_fallback.cycle_drift`, the size hold's model) and a clip whose first frame's height and size (the square root of the frame's coverage) are both off it by 10 % or more, the same way (`LEAD_IN_MIN`), opens on a lead-in (`gait_fallback.lead_in`) that runs while the height stays that far off — a reframing scales the whole body, a pose such as an item held up from the second frame on changes the height alone; `--cycle auto` and `periodic` — the size hold, the motion analysis, the cycle search and the gait fallback — read the clip after it as a clip of its own, window included. Every frame number the report gives is the clip's own: `cycle.start`, each candidate's `start` and `context_pair_range`, a refusal's windows too, so `--cycle fixed --start` taken from them cuts the frames they name. Every walk and run report gains `lead_in` (`frames`, `height_change`, `first_off`, `first_off_size`, `min`, `method`, `search_from`); a clip too short to read its size one cycle on has none (`why`). A cut the caller names (`fixed`, `pinned`) is the caller's frames. `cycle.coverage` counts the frames the search read. The gait fallback now scales back by the size read one cycle on, as the hold does, so a first pose settling into the walk no longer hides a body that grows from it (`gait_fallback.scale_drift` gains `method: one-cycle-on` in the report); a clip that reaches the fallback with 3 % or more of change is scaled by that model now, and its pixels and the drift its refusal quotes can differ in the last places. A walk or run with no lead-in that the first search cuts is cut as before, its strip, GIF, WebP, cycle frames and `strip.json` byte for byte. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#4-loop--period-first-seam-second-then-the-gait-floor), [docs/loop-review.md](docs/loop-review.md#the-gait-fallback-a-slow-walk-or-a-walk-toward-the-camera).
- **Fix: `--body-height` reads the standing height on the cut's first frame where the cut is filmed at another size than the clip's first.** The clip's first frame is the still's pose as filmed, but a video model may reframe the subject after it, by a lead-in or by less, and then the walk is filmed at another size than that pose: read there, the standing height scaled a walk after a lead-in up into the cell cap, and delivered a walk reframed by 7 % over its first frames 7 % too large. A walk or run cut for a `--body-height` now has its size read against the first frame's (`gait_fallback.size_change`) on three lengths at once — the height, the mass (the square root of the summed coverage) and the breadth (the widest row of the upper half of the body), each the middle of the cut's frames over the first frame's. A pose moves them apart (a walk is shorter than its standing pose and broader; an item held up is taller and no heavier) and a reframing moves all three the same way, so the change is the least of the three when all go one way and none when they part. At 1 % or more (`SIZE_HOLD_MIN`) the standing height is read on the cut's own first frame, and `strip.json` says so (`body_ref: cut-first-frame`, `body_ref_frame`, the clip frame); `video-cycle-align` rebuilds such a strip at the same height. The report gains `cut_size` (`change`, `height`, `mass`, `breadth`, `min`, `method`) on every walk and run cut for a `--body-height`. A cut the size of its first frame reads the first frame as before, and its strip, GIF, WebP, cycle frames and `strip.json` are as before. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#4-loop--period-first-seam-second-then-the-gait-floor).
- **`tests/video/test_edge_speck_lead_in.py`**: a walker filmed at 0.63 of its size and reframed over 9 frames: a lead-in of 5 frames, found no cycle before, now cuts its 24-frame walk after it, every frame number its own; one filmed at 1.3 of its size has a lead-in too, and one that holds an item up from its second frame on has none; too slow a walk after the same lead-in is refused with windows that all start after it; a fixed cut there reads its standing height on its own first frame, and `video-cycle-align` keeps it; a walker reframed from 0.93 of its size, or from 1.07, opens no lead-in, and a fixed cut after it read the first frame's height (7 % too large or small) and now reads its own first frame's; a first pose that settles into the walk, an item held up from the second frame on and an arm swung out are each read as a pose, on the first frame. A walker that settles from its first pose and grows 5 % reads 2.8 % on a line and was refused; read one cycle on it is scaled back and cut.

- **Fix: `video-frames` erases a speck far from the body before the edge check.** A fleck the model drew drifting across the background survives the matte as a few pale pixels; where it crossed an edge band in one frame, the edge check read it as the subject framed too tight and refused the clip, and `video-loop --anchor motion-auto`, which never cleans a frame, kept it in the loop and widened every cell to reach it. Now every keyed frame has its specks erased before anything reads it (`frames.drop_specks`): a piece of the frame — its opaque pixels, corners joining them — under 1 % of the body (`SPECK_MIN_FRACTION`, at least 8 px) and more than a tenth of the body's height from it (`SPECK_APART`). Small pieces near the body are the subject's own and stay: the outline's loose pixels, a shadow drawn under a shoe, a part of a shoe the matte cut loose. Each report row gains `specks`, how many its frame lost, and the report `specks` (`dropped`, `frames` and the rule's numbers); a frame with no speck is not written again, so its bytes are as before. `video-loop`'s own pass on a cut that is not motion-reviewed (`--anchor none|feet|body`) erases every island of solid pixels under the same 1 % as before, pixel for pixel, through the same function (a run-based labelling, about twenty times faster than the pixel-by-pixel search it replaces). [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#3-frames--extract-key-check-the-edges).
- **`tests/video/test_edge_speck_lead_in.py`** keys a body on magenta with five pale pixels in its right edge band in one frame: before this the frames were refused as framed too tight, now the fleck is erased (`specks: 1`) and no frame touches the edge; a shadow under the feet, a loose pixel by the outline and a far block too large to be a speck are kept; `video-loop`'s own reading still erases the loose pieces. A walker on magenta with a fleck drifting far from it, keyed and cut `--anchor motion-auto`, is cut 49 px wide, the walker alone (147 px before).

- **Output change: `video-follow` reads the body's motion off the body, not off whatever is at its top.** The motion was the top row of the silhouette and the middle of the band under it, so whatever came to the top was the body: a tail tip rising over the head, a flopping ear, a ponytail, a sword or a rod held up. Where such a part took the top in some cells and not in others, the read jumped by the part's whole swing from one cell to the next; where it held the top, the bob was the part's own swing. The follow-through then answered the tail, the ears or the weapon, the region was carried off its part, and large parts were refused. Now each cell is laid where its silhouette, worn down by every whole number of pixels from a quarter to half of the first cell's depth, overlaps the first cell's, worn alike, most: the overlaps of every level summed, counted in whole pixels (the FFT's rounding is gone, so the same strip reads the same on every machine), a tie going to the smaller move. What swings on the body and is thinner than it is worn away, the deepest of the body counts most, and a torso that is shallower in one cell (an arm swung away from it) still counts. A part as thick as the body outlasts the wearing — ears as wide as the head — and a cell that has it elsewhere than the first cell does, laid on the first cell alone, lies ears on ears with its body tens of pixels off, so the read jumped there too. The cells are therefore laid twice: laid on the first cell, each is moved back by its lay and the cells are counted — per level and pixel, how many more of them are in that level there than are not, and none where no more are — and each cell is then laid on that count the same way; its lay less the first cell's own is the motion. The head and torso are at one place in every cell and count in full; ears up in some cells and flopped onto the face in others, or a leg forward and back, are at any one place in half of the cells or fewer and count nothing. A thick part that keeps one place in most of the cells is body to this read, which sees outlines only. **Every follow-through moves differently, the strips whose top is the head included — no strip is written byte for byte as before**: the head nods and leads the torso, and the read is now the torso's. The record gains `body_worn_px`, the first and last level the silhouette was worn down to. A cell with nothing in it a quarter as deep as the first cell's body is refused: `video-follow: cell <k> has no body as deep as a quarter of cell 0's (<px> px)`; so is a strip with no part of the body at one place in more than half of its cells: `video-follow: no part of the body is at one place in more than half of the cells`. Reading the motion is two lays of every cell: on an Apple M1, one thread, 0.5 s for 24 cells of 210 × 240 px, 16 s for 24 cells of 630 × 720 px (11 s for one lay), and 110 s for 64 cells of 700 × 700 px around one disc 640 px across (58 s for one lay). [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#6-follow-through--video-follow).
- **`tests/video/test_follow.py`** follows a chest on a walker with a thin tail whose tip swings over the head, and on one holding a sword up and swinging it over the head: the record (bob, `dx_px`, `dy_px`, reach, gain, fold) and the chest's moved pixels are the bare walker's. Before this, the chest was refused on both: the read made the move at gain 1 20.8 and 17.5 px, so the chest could take no more than gain 0.52 and 0.61, where on the bare walker it moves at 2.5. A strip with a cell no deeper than a line is refused untouched. It also follows a chest on a bunny whose lop ears, more than half as thick as its body is wide, swing down and back up once a cycle: the record and the chest's moved pixels are the earless bunny's. Laid on the first cell alone, the body was read as dropping with the ears by up to 30 px in 8 of the 24 cells and as jumping back in the next, and the chest was refused (a move at gain 1 of 16.1 px, no more than gain 0.94). A strip whose cells share no place — a bar, and in each other cell a block at a different place along it — is refused untouched.

- **Fix: `video-follow` replaces the loop's files only once all of them are written.** The strip, the GIF and the WebP were written over the loop's before the record was, so a run that failed on the record — `follow.follow_loop` given numpy numbers, an int64 of which has no JSON form (`TypeError`) — or on an animation's check left a moved strip beside a record that did not say so. Now the strip, GIF, WebP and record are written and checked in a scratch directory beside the loop (`.follow.*`), each under its own name, and moved over the loop's files after the record is written; a run that fails on the way leaves all four as they were (the strip as cut is still kept as `follow.source.png`, as on a refusal). `follow_loop` reads its regions, `gain`, `freq` and `zeta` as the floats the command line gives, so numpy numbers, or plain ints, write the strip and record that the same numbers as floats write, byte for byte (a caller that passed plain ints finds them recorded as floats, `80.0` where it was `80`). `loop.verify_animation` closes the file it checks. The command's output, its strip, GIF, WebP and record, and `--help` are as before, byte for byte. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#6-follow-through--video-follow).
- **`tests/video/test_follow.py`** moves a chest and a smaller head region given as int64, int32, float32, float64 and plain int coordinates, as the rows of an int64 array, and with a float32 `gain`: each writes the strip, GIF, WebP and record the float coordinates write, byte for byte (before this the int64 run failed on the record with the strip already moved). A run made to fail on its record or on its WebP's check, on a loop as cut and on one already followed, leaves the strip, GIF, WebP and record as they were, and nothing beside them but `follow.source.png`.

- **Output change: `video-follow --on-fold lower` gives each region a gain of its own.** One gain for the strip, set by the region with the smallest radius, let the smallest ellipse decide for every part: a small one (an ear) lowered a large one (a chest) to its own gain, and where that gain went under 1 refused the whole strip, so the chest did not move either. Now a region that folds at `--gain` takes the largest gain that does not fold it (in steps of 0.01), a region that does not fold keeps `--gain`, and one that would have to go under 1, the mass as measured, is **held**: it does not move, and stderr names it by its `--region` (`video-follow: --region <cx,cy,rx,ry> folds at --gain <g>, under 1 (the mass as measured): it is held, and does not move`); a region lowered while another keeps its gain is named the same way (`video-follow: --gain 2.5 folds --region <cx,cy,rx,ry> (a move of <px> px); lowered to --gain <g> for it`). The strip is refused only when every region is held, and that message names the largest region (`… folds a region of radius <r> px over itself (the largest of <n> regions: every one folds), and --on-fold lower would have to go under --gain 1 …`); with one region it reads as before. Where regions of different gains overlap, the larger move wins (`follow.move_regions` takes groups of regions, each with its own offset), and the move there is nowhere steeper than either region's own. In the record each `fold.regions` entry gains `gain` (0 when held) and `held`, the strip's `gain` is the largest any region moved by (`dx_px`, `dy_px` and `reach_px` are that gain's), `fold.ratio` is the nearest any region came to folding and `fold.lowered` says whether any region was lowered or held; the printed summary gains `region_gains`. A strip whose regions all take one gain — one region, regions that fold alike, or none that folds — is written as before, strip, GIF, WebP and record byte for byte. `--on-fold refuse`, the default, is unchanged. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#6-follow-through--video-follow).
- **`tests/video/test_follow.py`** moves a chest with a region on the head too small to move at gain 1: before this the strip was refused, now the head region is held and the strip is the chest's alone, byte for byte (and at `--gain 0.9` the chest still moves at 0.9). A head region that folds at 1.75 beside a chest that does not fold takes 1.75 while the chest keeps 2.5, each region's pixels those of the region moved alone at its gain; a small region inside a larger one folds nowhere; a strip whose every region is held is refused untouched; two regions that fold alike are written with the one-gain record and the strip their gain asked for gives; the command names the region it lowered and the one it held.

- **Fix: `video-loop --anchor motion-auto` reads the same motion from the same pixels on every machine.** A patch of its region search that sits on a flat fill matches equally at several offsets; the FFTs that score them round differently between numpy builds (about 1e-14), and that rounding chose the offset. From the same frames a synthetic walk was tracked differently on Linux than on macOS, its distance matrix differed in most entries, and `tests/video/test_loop_prop_seam_held.py` failed on CI only (first choice popping 2.4 there, 11.7 on macOS). Now costs within 1e-9 of the lowest are one match (`auto_motion.MATCH_TIE`) and the offset nearest the centre the search was put on is taken; regions whose qualities tie on that grain are ranked by their box; and the drift lines that move the analysis frames are least-squares lines with exactly rounded sums (`auto_motion._line`, now `sprite_gen.util.lsq.line`), not `np.polyfit`, whose LAPACK solve rounded differently and tipped bilinear samples of those frames. The tracked offsets, the analysis frames and the distance matrix are now bit-identical on macOS arm64 and Linux x86-64; only the last digits of reported costs and scores differ. Footage whose patches never tie is cut as before: the same regions, offsets and cut, its analysis translations moved only in their last bits (about 1e-14), which can tip a bilinear sample and move a score in its sixth decimal. Drawn, flat-filled frames, where ties are the rule, are tracked — and may be cut — otherwise than v2.36.0 tracked them on any one machine.
- **`tests/video/test_auto_motion.py`** perturbs every FFT correlation by up to 1e-12 of its largest value, seed by seed: the tied offset taken on the flat torso, the regions found and every tracked offset are the same under all of them (under v2.36.0 eight seeds took six offsets, 14 px apart, and four seeds found four different region pairs), and the drift line is `np.polyfit`'s to 1e-12 and bit-identical in any order of its points. `test_a_held_part_that_swings_with_the_step_is_cut_one_step_long` draws its pennant on a 53-frame beat (phase 0.75): its first choice pops 12.6, no other candidate's top over 8.5, the cut taken closes below the hand at 0.0; on v2.35.0 with this fix it fails where v2.35.0 reads nothing below the top.
- **Fix: the size hold and the gait fallback of `video-loop --anchor motion-auto` scale the same frames on every machine.** Both scale every frame back about a foot point read off a fitted line, and size the room the frames need from it. The line was `np.polyfit`'s, whose LAPACK solve rounds differently between builds (Accelerate on macOS, OpenBLAS on Linux; on the 10-05 synthetic grid their lines lay a few 1e-13 px apart). Feet that stand on the frame's bottom edge in every frame sit exactly where that rounding decides: on a synthetic walker growing 3 %, `np.polyfit` on Linux put them 4 units in the last place past the edge, and with its coefficients moved by up to 1e-12 of themselves, four seeds in eight gave the frames 2 px of room below and four none — another frame size, and so other frames for the cut search. The lines are now least-squares lines with exactly rounded sums (`sprite_gen.util.lsq`, the line `auto_motion` already drew its analysis drift with, moved there): feet on the edge read exactly the edge, and the foot point, the room and every held pixel are the same under any rounding. A clip the hold leaves as filmed and the fallback does not scale is cut as before.
- **`tests/video/test_gait_fallback.py`** holds a synthetic walker whose feet stand on the frame's bottom edge with `np.polyfit`'s coefficients moved by up to 1e-12 of themselves, seed by seed: the foot point, the room and every held pixel are the same under all of them (under v2.36.0 eight seeds gave eight foot points and two paddings); a walker that neither moves nor grows reads its box's foot point and height to the bit. `tests/test_lsq.py` checks the line against `np.polyfit` and in any order of its points (moved from `test_auto_motion.py`).

## v2.36.0 - A slow walk cut one step long is named, and cut again on a count; a staff that closes only on two steps is cut as one cycle

Not published on its own: it ships in v2.37.0.

- **New record: `cycle.step_screen` on every walk and run `video-loop --anchor motion-auto` cuts.** A walk slower than its window holds (0.5 to 1.6 s, at most half the clip) is cut one step long where its legs are drawn alike: the step repeats in pixels, the loop walks on one leg, its arms jump at the wrap, and a set aligned with it plays one step where its other directions play two. Nothing said so — the two-cycle screen looks only below the cut. Now the cut is screened the other way, over the whole clip (`period.step_screen`): the lag within 3 frames of twice the cut that repeats best (`lag`, `searched`), its distance over the cut's own over the same frames (`ratio`, `pairs`), its dip under the clip's profile (`depth`), how much of a second cycle the clip holds past it (`coverage`), and the step signal's peaks over the cut (`leg_peaks`, a record only). A repeat there (`depth` at least 0.15) better than at the cut (`ratio` at most **0.85**, `period.STEP_RATIO_MAX`) is a `suspect`. **Nothing is cut on it**: a cycle on a frame count that is not whole repeats better at its double too, so only a count of the steps settles it. Images, `strip.json`, warnings and stderr are as before, byte for byte; the report's `cycle` and the printed summary gain `step_screen`. [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md#the-step-screen--a-slow-walk-cut-one-step-long).
- **New option: `video-loop --steps 1|2`** (with `--anchor motion-auto`), the count of steps in the cut it takes without it, from whoever looked at the loop. `1` cuts the walk again in the window [2L − 1, 2L + 1] — not bounded by half the clip, but held inside an explicit `--min-len`/`--max-len` (below) — with the same search, the equivalent cut whose wrap the clip itself plays taken (`choice: "closure"`) and no window twice that; a clip too short to see that window repeat is cut all the same, the count standing for the repeat (`method: "counted-window-v1"`, `unseen`). `2` keeps the cut. Either way `cycle.steps` records the count (`by: "given"`, `given`, and for `1` `first_cut` and `window`), `strip.json` says `steps: 2`, and `cycle.coverage` is (n − L) / L. **New warning**: a cut two steps long whose clip holds under 0.25 of a cycle past it (`loop.COVERAGE_MIN`) — `the cut is two steps long (… frames, one cycle) and the clip holds … of a cycle past it (under 0.25): its repeat is barely seen — the seam says whether it closes; a longer take shows it repeat`. It is cut, not refused; the seam gate judges it.
- **Output change: an explicit `--min-len`/`--max-len` window is the window, on every path that sets a cut's length.** `--anchor motion-auto` cut the caller's ceiling back to half the clip, so asking for a slow walk's cycle (`--min-len 41 --max-len 43` on 73 frames) searched [41, 36] and failed. Only the state's default window is bounded by half the clip now. The window is worked out once (`loop.CutWindow`) and every search reads its own off it — the first search, a window two steps long, the count (`--steps 1`), the gait fallback, the one-shot failover — so none sets a bound of its own: the `--cycle auto` one-shot failover of an action state widened an explicit `--max-len` to nine tenths of the clip (a 16-frame hop asked under `--max-len 14` was cut 36 long) and the one-shot search took no `--min-len` at all; both are held to the caller's bounds now (`detect_one_shot` honours `min_len`; `run_loop` passes `ONE_SHOT_MIN_LEN` unless `--min-len` is given, so a run without one cuts as before), and a one-shot the bounds leave no room for says so (`no complete one-shot return … — in 8-14 frames, inside --min-len 8 --max-len 14`). Whatever chose the cut, `--cycle fixed` and `pinned` included, a cut outside an explicit bound fails: `the cut is … frames long (…), outside --max-len N`. **A count and a window with no length in common fail**: `--steps 1` on a 21-frame step under `--max-len 25` cut 42 frames; now `--steps 1: the cut of 21 frames from 18 is one step, so the cycle is 41-43 frames long, and --max-len 25 leaves none of that: the count and the window disagree — drop one, or give a window that holds the cycle` (exit 1, the failed report's `cycle.steps` with `counted_window`, `window: null` and `refused`). Where they share part of the window, only that part is read (`cycle.steps.window`, `counted_window` beside it). A run with no explicit bound cuts byte for byte as before.
- **Output change: once the top of the silhouette jumps at its first choice, `video-loop --anchor motion-auto` reads the held part below its top too, and may cut two steps long — one cycle.** A staff turns about the hand as the arm swings, once a cycle; one step on it is back in place at the top band moving the other way. Where the legs are drawn alike the search takes that step as the cycle: v2.35.0 chose again on the top band alone, took such a cut — the top closed — and the shaft under the hand swung back at the wrap, with no warning. Now, behind the seam pop only, the side of the body the top band sits on is read row by row on the silhouette's outer edge (`repair.held_side`, `repair.side_edges`, `repair.held_edge` on the frames the search chooses from, `loop.HeldEdge`): per row, the cut's first and last frames against the clip's frames after its last and before its first, in place and in speed, over the row's median step in the loop; the 80th percentile over the rows is the held seam, closing under **1.2** (`repair.HELD_EDGE_REFERENCE`). Among the candidates whose top does not jump, one whose held side closes is taken (the usual 15 % of the best score, then the smallest held seam). Where none closes, windows twice a candidate's length are read — two steps of a confirmed repeat, one cycle, not themselves seen repeating — and one whose held side closes and whose top does not jump is cut: `cycle.steps` is `{"count": 2, "by": "held-edge", "step_lengths": [...]}` (the candidates it is two of, each one step), `cycle.period_local` its own length, `cycle.coverage` recorded, and `strip.json` says `steps: 2`. A cut with fewer than two clip frames either side of it cannot be read: `skipped`, counted under `unread`, never chosen. An explicit `--max-len` stays the cut's ceiling (`two_step_capped`: `max_len`, `lengths_over`), and counted steps read nothing twice as long. `cycle.seam_pop.held_edge` records `side`, `reference`, `measured`, `unread`, `doubled`, `chosen` and `two_step` (`read`, lengths tried, `measured`, `unread`, `top_pops`, `closing`); a candidate row carries `held_edge` (`null` with `held_edge_skipped`). A first choice whose top does not jump reads nothing new. [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md#the-held-side--a-staff-that-closes-at-the-top-and-swings-below-the-hand).
- **New warning: a held part that no window one or two lengths long closes.** The top band's cut stands and `jolt.warnings` and stderr gain `the held part does not close at the wrap on the <side> side of the silhouette, below its top: …x its rows' median step (over 1.2); no window one or two lengths long closes it — it swings on a beat of its own` (or, where `--max-len` kept the longer windows from being read, `no window one length long closes it, and windows two lengths long (…-… frames) are over --max-len N, so they were not read`; where the steps were counted, `no window of the counted length closes it`); a held side that could not be read is said as well. Nothing fails on it.
- **Output change: `video-cycle-align` reads a loop's steps, and stops on a loop that may be one step.** A loop whose `strip.json` says `steps: 2` returns half way at its second step: that suspect is recorded `status: "steps"` (with `steps`) and does not stop the set, and the loop is resampled to the set's length like the rest; `--cycles <loop>=2` on it takes one half out and drops `steps`. A loop whose cut's report (beside the loop, or one directory up for a `video-set` item: `align.cut_report`) has `cycle.step_screen.suspect`, and whose steps nobody counted, is a suspect too (`one_step`: `length`, `lag`, `seconds`, `ratio`, `depth`, `pairs`, `coverage`, `leg_peaks`, `why`, `report`): stopped under `--multi-cycle fail` with its name and `cut it again with sprite-gen video-loop … --steps 1`, aligned and warned about under `warn`; `--cycles <loop>=1` says it holds a whole cycle. Every loop with neither is aligned as before, its row, `strip.json` and cells byte for byte.
- **`video-set`**: an item whose cut's steps are known carries `loop.steps`, one the step screen suspects `loop.step_screen` (`lag`, `ratio`, `depth`, `coverage`), and `table.md` says so in the cycle column (`46 (2 steps)`, `21 (one step?)`); a state stopped on a loop that may be one step says `<item> may be one step (cut it again with video-loop --steps 1 if it is)`. A cut two steps long now carries `ratio` like any candidate, which the item reads.
- **`tests/video/test_explicit_window.py`** holds one table of every path that sets a length — the first search, a window two steps long, the count, the gait fallback, the one-shot failover, a fixed cut — under an explicit `--max-len` or `--min-len`, partial windows included (a held staff read only at 47 of 47-49 frames, a count read at 41-42 of 41-43): each run cuts inside the bounds or fails, and the report shows the path ran. Before this, the count (25 → 42), the one-shot failover (14 and 30 → 36) and the fixed cut (30 → 40) were cut outside them.
- **`tests/video/test_one_step_cut.py`** draws a side walker with a 42-frame cycle, legs alike and arms shaded apart, over 73 frames: v2.35.0 cuts it at 21 and says nothing; now the cut is the same and the step screen names it (`ratio` 0.48), the same drawing on 25 and 23.5 frames is written byte for byte as with no screen (23.5 a suspect that is a cycle), `--steps 1` cuts 15/42 where the arm's wrap is 0 against 7.6 for the step, `--steps 2` keeps the cut, a 50-frame clip is cut 8/42 and warned about its coverage, `--min-len 41 --max-len 43` is the window, and the set alignment stops on the step and aligns its re-cut. **`tests/video/test_loop_prop_seam_held.py`** draws a walker whose legs come back every 24 frames with a staff turning about the hand every 48: every cut one step long whose top closes reads a held seam of 2.0 to 5.2, and the window two steps long taken reads 0 and is aligned at the set's length as one cycle. A staff swinging with the step below a pennant that sways on its own beat stays one step long; a staff whose foot swings every 37 frames is kept on the top band's cut and warned about; a walk without a staff reads nothing below its top; two loops without a staff are aligned with exactly the `strip.json` and `cycle_align` keys v2.35.0 wrote.

## v2.35.0 - A staff that swings on its own beat is not cut where it jumps

- **Output change: `video-loop --anchor motion-auto` chooses its cut again when the top of the silhouette jumps into the first frame.** A staff, a flag or a raised spear held above the head can sway on a beat slower than the steps; a cut one step long then ends with it elsewhere, the body closes and the held part jumps at the wrap. The seam ratio is an area measure and a thin rod hardly moves it, so such a cut passed. The search now weighs, for its first choice, the top band's step into the first frame over the loop's median step (`repair.seam_pop`, on the frames it chooses from, moved by the analysis translation; the median a pixel at least), counted only when the wrap is that band's largest step. Over the bound (**10**, `repair.SEAM_POP_REFERENCE`) every candidate is measured, and among those under it the cut is taken from the scores within the usual 15 % of the best, smallest jump first. The report's `cycle.seam_pop` records `first_choice`, `applied`, `measured`, `unread` and `chosen` (or `why` when every candidate read jumps and the first choice is kept). A cut whose top band cannot be read — a frame with nothing in it — has no jump to rank: it is recorded `skipped` (why) in place of a `pop` (`first_choice.skipped`; a candidate row's `seam_pop` is `null` with `seam_pop_skipped`), counted under `unread`, and never chosen as one that closes; a first choice that cannot be read is kept. A first choice under the bound is kept and nothing past it is measured: a loop without such a part is cut, written and warned about byte for byte as before. [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md#the-seam-pop--a-part-held-above-the-head-swinging-on-its-own-beat).
- **New: every walk and run loop reports the seam pop and warns on it** (`jolt.seam_pop`: per axis `wrap_pct`, `step_median_pct`, `wrap_over_median`, `wrap_is_largest`; `pop`, `reference`, `pops`; `seam_pop` in the printed summary's `jolt`). Over the bound, on the loop as it plays, `jolt.warnings` and stderr gain `the top of the silhouette jumps into the loop's first frame up or down … (over 10x): a part held above the head swings on its own beat and does not close at this cut`. The top line (up or down) had no bound at all; a sideways jump into frame 0 the head's 0.75 % bound already names is not said twice. Nothing fails on it, and a fixed or `--cycle periodic` cut is not chosen again.
- **New: a motion-auto search that finds no cycle reports the windows it measured and refused.** The failed report's `cycle` was `null`, so a caller that delivers anyway (a forced `--cycle fixed`) had no measured window and took the whole clip — which, for a walk, cannot loop. It now carries `status: "refused"`, `window`, `refused_count` and up to 12 `candidates` (`start`, `length`, `score`, `ratio`, `context_repeat_over_step`, `drift_line_residual_analysis_px`, `seam_pop`, and `refused`: why), best score first, those whose top could not be read (`seam_pop: null`, `seam_pop_skipped`) after those whose top closes, and those whose top jumps at the wrap last (`local_cycle.REFUSED_SHOWN`). The error, the exit code and every other gate are unchanged. [docs/loop-review.md](docs/loop-review.md#the-gait-fallback-a-slow-walk-or-a-walk-toward-the-camera).
- **`tests/video/test_loop_prop_seam.py`** draws a walker stepping every 24 frames with a staff above its head swaying every 61: v2.34.0 cuts it where the staff's tip jumps 11 times its largest step inside the loop, and now it is cut where the tip closes; a fixed cut whose staff tip rises and drops back at the wrap warns; a walker whose staff tip is flung out of the top band in one frame is not cut on a window holding that frame, which cannot be read (the branch's first take recorded those as jumping 0 and chose one whose tip jumps 8 times its largest step); a walk whose legs never come back fails with measured windows in its report.

## v2.34.0 - A foot the set alignment names by too small a margin is left for whoever looks

- **Output change: a side or diagonal loop whose shade parts its two strikes by less than 0.025 in luma is no longer named** (`video-cycle-align`, `video-set --align-cycles`; `align.SHADE_MARGIN`, was 0.015). The shade cue (the far leg drawn darker) named a foot whenever the strikes differed by 0.015; on drawn walks checked by eye it named feet wrong at margins under the new bar and right above it. A foot named wrong is not asked about — the loop ships half a cycle off — so a margin under the bar now leaves the foot unnamed: the loop starts on its larger strike with `start_foot: null`, is listed under the report's `unnamed_feet` with its two candidate strike frames, and is told back with `--foot <loop>=left|right` (`video-set --align-foot`) by a person or a vision call. Its frames, strip, GIF and WebP are those of its larger strike as before; only who named the foot changes. Front and back loops (the depth cue, 1 % of the body's height) and every loop whose margin clears the bar come out byte for byte as they were. [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md#4-one-cycle-for-a-direction-set--video-cycle-align-video-set---align-cycles-auto).
- **`foot_why` of a margin too small starts `low-margin`**, with the margin, the cue and the bar (`low-margin: the two strikes differ by 0.0235 in shade, under the 0.025 it takes to name a foot`; `align.LOW_MARGIN`), in place of `the two strikes look alike in shade (…)`, for both cues — in the loop row, `unnamed_feet`, the warning, and `foot_unnamed_why` once told.
- **`tests/video/test_cycle_align_foot_margin.py`** draws the side walker with its far leg 3 levels darker (margin 0.0235: named under 0.015, unnamed now — listed, warned, told back by `--foot`) and 4 levels darker (0.0314: named as before).

## v2.33.0 - A horse's sheet does not swing its arms, and it does not strike with its hands

- **Fix: the sheet rows `prepare` writes gave every body a person's arms, shoulders and hands.** 2.32.0's body plan reached the clip's walk, idle and still but not the rows an image model draws a sheet from: the walk and run rows moved "body, arm, leg, hair, and prop", the front and three-quarter-front walks "alternating leg, arm, shoulder", the diagonal runs traded "foot-contact phases" of "the left and right legs", the wave was "arm pose only: arm down, arm raised, hand tilted" (default action "friendly hand wave gesture"), every row's anchor lock spent its motion on "arm counter-swing" and turned "the body, feet, shoulders", and a diagonal row read its facing by "shoulder overlap, hand/foot placement". A horse's walk sheet came with arms.
- **Fix: an animal's attack was made with its hands.** The clip's attack (`MOTION_TEXT["attack"]`) struck "with what it is already holding (bare hands only if it holds nothing)", and its hold (`HOLD_TEXT["attack"]`, after a caller's `--motion` too) said "one hand stays one hand, both hands stay both hands … A hand the motion does not use stays where it is drawn", whatever the body.
- **New `prepare --body-plan`** (the same `biped` / `quadruped` / `legless` / `"<figure>=<plan>"` as `video-set`; repeatable; over the request's `body_plan`): any body but one biped gets rows without the parts it lacks (`STATE_REQUIREMENTS_ANY_BODY`, `ANCHOR_LOCK_ANY_BODY`, the diagonal facing line; the wave lifts one side as the clip's wave does, and its default action is `DEFAULT_ACTIONS_ANY_BODY["wave"]` where the request names none) and every row ends its state requirements with what it stands on (`body_plan.text`). `sprite-request.json` records `body_plan` (absent without one) and a run prepared again from it keeps it. **`video-set` / `video-prompt --body-plan`** now also give the attack `ATTACK_TEXT_ANY_BODY` ("with what it is already holding, or with its own body if it holds nothing") and `HOLD_TEXT_ANY_BODY` (anything it holds stays held the same way; no hand counted); which part strikes is left to the caller's `--motion`. No body plan, or one biped, keeps every clip prompt byte for byte (2.22.0's) and every sheet row byte for byte (2.32.0's, new `tests/fixtures/prepare-rows-v2.32.0.json.gz` drawn by `tests/gen/prepare_freeze_table.py`). The new sentences are not yet measured on an image or clip model. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#the-sheet-rows--prepare---body-plan).
- **`tests/gen/test_body_plan_sheets.py`** draws every sheet row (each state with a sentence of its own, the default states, a state with none, with and without a base image, a direction-contract run) and every clip prompt of every state (built-in and `--motion`, every view and model, `video-prompt`) for a quadruped, a body without legs and a man leading a horse, and finds no arm, hand, fist, wrist, shoulder, chest, hip, knee, shoe, heel, toe or counted foot in any (no leg or foot at all for a body without legs, apart from the sentence saying it has none); against 2.32.0 it fails on the attack's hands and on `prepare`, which took no body plan.

## v2.32.0 - A horse is not told a person's walk

- **Fix: an animal's walk and idle named a person's arms and feet.** The walk and run sentences have named no limb since 2.0.0 ("`video-set` motion templates no longer assume a biped"), but four sentences added later were measured on people and said to every body: the Lite walk calm ("a gentle arm swing close to the body", 2.18.0), the Lite back-diagonal head hold ("only the legs, arms and the ends of the hair move", 2.18.0), the idle ("both feet planted flat", chest, shoulders and arms, 2.9.0) and the front or back walk's mid-step redraw ("one foot … directly under its own hip … the arms swing", 2.18.0). Said of a horse, they walk it on a person's legs or stand it up on two. Not a regression of the 2.0.0 fix: these paths came after it.
- **Fix: the still a front, back or diagonal clip starts from counted a person's feet.** The view sentence a still is drawn with (`still_view_text`, `gen --direction`; an app that draws its own still takes it too) said "the chest, hips and the toes of both feet" (front), "the shoulders, the hips and the heels of both feet" (back), "the face looking the same way as the chest" (front diagonal) and "the backs of the shoes" (back diagonal). The clip starts from that still, so a horse drawn on two feet stands up before the walk begins.
- **New `--body-plan` on `video-set`, `video-prompt` and `gen`** (`build_prompt(body_plan=...)`, `walk_start_prompt(body_plan=...)`, `still_view_text(body_plan=...)`, `gen.still_prompt(body_plan=...)`, `sprite_gen/video/body_plan.py`; on `gen` it needs `--direction`): `biped` (default), `quadruped` or `legless`, or one `"<figure>=<plan>"` per figure of a scene (`--body-plan "the man=biped" --body-plan "the horse=quadruped"`). Any body but one biped gets those four sentences without the parts it lacks (`IDLE_TEXT_ANY_BODY`, `LITE_WALK_TEXT_LEGGED` / `LITE_WALK_TEXT_LEGLESS`, `LITE_HEAD_TEXT_ANY_BODY`, `WALK_START_TEXT_ANY_BODY`) and, after the motion sentence or the caller's `--motion`, what it stands on: "It stays on all four legs, as in the image, and never rises onto its hind legs."; a scene says it per figure. The still's view sentence becomes `STILL_VIEW_TEXT_ANY_BODY` (where the body and head point, no feet, chest, hips, shoulders or shoes) ending in what it stands on (`body_plan.still_text`): "…, standing on all four legs, not rearing onto its hind legs". A body without legs has no mid-step redraw. `video-set`, `video-prompt --json` and `gen`'s `extra.view` record `body_plan`. No body plan, or one biped, keeps every prompt byte for byte (`tests/gen/test_prompt_freeze.py`). The new sentences are not yet measured on clips. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#a-body-that-is-not-a-person----body-plan).
- **`tests/video/test_body_plan.py`** draws every walk, run, idle and jump prompt (built-in and `--motion`, every model, `video-prompt`, the mid-step redraw) and every still view sentence (`still_view_text` and `gen.still_prompt`, every view and facing) for a quadruped, a body without legs and a person leading a horse, and finds no arm, hand, wrist, shoulder, chest, hip, knee, shoe, heel, toe or counted foot in any (no foot at all for a body without legs); seven of them are pinned as snapshots.

## v2.31.0 - A loop whose foot the set alignment cannot name is told it, one loop at a time

- **New `video-cycle-align --foot <loop>=left|right`** (repeatable; the loop named as for `--cycles`: its `--loop-dir`, its strip's name, its directory's name or a `video-set` item's name) and **`video-set --align-foot <item>=left|right`**: which own foot lands on a loop's first strike, as seen by whoever looked at it — a person, the agent, or a vision call. Where a loop's view cannot tell its feet apart (`start_foot: null`), `--start-foot` was set-wide and there was no way to say "this loop's larger strike is its left foot". Now that loop alone starts as the set's start foot lands — on its first strike, or half a cycle on — and every other loop comes out byte for byte as without it. The answer is about the loop as filmed (its strikes are read from `cycle.source/`), so it holds for a later alignment at the same length; like `--cycles` it is not remembered. A foot given for a loop the view did name is taken too, and where it disagrees the row carries `foot_disagrees` and a warning. A `--foot` that names no loop or two, or is not `left`/`right`, is refused before anything is rewritten; `video-set` refuses an `--align-foot` item that is not a walk or run filmed in two or more directions before filming, and names one given for an item that failed or whose state was not aligned in that state's `feet_unused` and a warning. The way feet are named from the pixels is unchanged.
- **New in the alignment report: `unnamed_feet`**, per loop whose foot nobody named: `dir`, `name`, `view`, `foot_why`, `candidates` (its two strikes: `strike` 0 and 1, `frame` in the rebuilt `cycle/` and the `path` to that frame — the frame to show whoever looks; the first is the frame the loop starts on) and `settle` (the two `--foot` arguments). The report also gains `feet_given`; every loop row and its `strip.json` `cycle_align` gain `start_foot_source` (`"engine"` where the view's cue named the foot, `"given"` from `--foot`, `null` where nobody did) and `strikes` (the two strikes as frames of the rebuilt `cycle/`), and a told row `foot_given` and, where the engine had not named it, `foot_unnamed_why` instead of `foot_why`. `video-set` carries `unnamed_feet` and `feet_given` per state and `start_foot_source` per item; a stopped set's `command` carries the `--foot` given. The warning for an unnamed loop now names the frame to look at and the `--foot` that settles it. Without `--foot`, the cycle, strip, GIF and WebP are unchanged. [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md#4-one-cycle-for-a-direction-set--video-cycle-align-video-set---align-cycles-auto), [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md).

## v2.30.0 - A held loop is judged on the cycle cut out of its clip, not on the whole clip

- **Output change: which loops `video-cycle-align` names for a retake** (`held-drawings`). The hold was read over the whole clip and given to the cut cycle, so a clip held for part of its length and drawn every frame for the rest was judged by the clip: a cycle cut from its every-frame part and stretched could be named (the clip reads as held), and one cut from its held part was passed (the clip reads as drawn every frame). `video-loop` now also reads the hold over the cut, on the clip's own steps as keyed from the cut's first frame into the frame after its last (`cycle_drawings` in the loop report and in `strip.json`: `start`, `length`, `steps`, `hold`, `drawings_per_second`, `contrast`), and the alignment uses it (`drawings.source: "span"`, with the clip's reading under `drawings.clip`). A cut of fewer than 12 steps (`held.SPAN_MIN_STEPS`, one window of the clip's reading) is not read on its own: the clip's reading stands (`source: "clip"`), and the row's `span_why` says so, as it does for a loop cut by 2.29.0. A clip held or drawn every frame throughout reads the same over its cut as over the whole: the cut, the strip, the GIF and the WebP are unchanged, and the loop report and `strip.json` gain `cycle_drawings`. [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md#4-one-cycle-for-a-direction-set--video-cycle-align-video-set---align-cycles-auto).

## v2.29.0 - A clip that shows each drawing for two or three frames is read as such, and the set alignment names a held loop it could not fill as a take to film again

- **New: `video-cycle-align` names a loop to film again** (`retake`, reason `held-drawings`). A loop whose clip holds each drawing for two or three frames ("on twos", "on threes"), that the set's length leaves at under 13 drawings a second (`align.RETAKE_DRAWINGS_MIN`; a clip on twos shows 12), with one or more frames between its drawings not made (`align.RETAKE_UNMADE_MIN`: taken from the nearer source frame under `--between auto` or `nearest`, made with a fault and kept under `rife`), is listed in the report's `retake` (`dir`, `name`, `hold`, `source`, `drawings`, `drawings_per_second_filmed`, `drawings_per_second`, `frames_per_drawing`, `unmade`, `limits`) and in a warning line ("film this direction again (held-drawings) — …"). Where the legs swap places between two drawings no interpolator draws them, so each frame `auto` took from the nearer source frame holds a drawing a frame longer and the loop halts there; only a new take drawn every frame fixes it. The set is still aligned and written, exit 0: the reason is for the caller to act on, never silent. Each loop row and its `strip.json` `cycle_align` gain `drawings` (the hold, read from the clip's record) and `retake` (the record, or `null`); `video-set` carries `retake` per state and per item. A held loop at its own length (nothing made), a held loop whose made frames all kept their outline, one squeezed to 13 drawings a second or more, and a loop drawn every frame with a replaced frame are not named.
- **New: `video-loop` records the clip's drawings** (`drawings` in the loop report and in `strip.json`, `hold`/`drawings_per_second` in the printed summary): whether the clip shows each drawing for one, two or three frames, how many drawings a second, the held steps, and `contrast` per hold as the evidence (`sprite_gen/video/held.py`), read over the whole clip as keyed, before the cut. The alignment reads the hold from `strip.json`: a cut cycle is short, and `--anchor motion-auto` shifts each cut frame so a pair's repeat may no longer read as one; a loop cut before this record is measured on its own cycle. The cut, the strip, the GIF and the WebP are unchanged.
- **Why a rhythm and not a threshold**: a model rarely repeats a drawing as a byte copy — it redraws the second frame of a pair a little — and a rule that calls a step held under a fraction of the median step misses exactly that, because with half the steps held the median falls between the two kinds. The judgement reads, for a hold of 2 or 3, the phase whose every second or third step is the change, in windows of 12 steps: the held steps' median over the change steps' median, under 0.3 (`held.CONTRAST_MAX`). A clip drawn every frame whose steps alternate long and short, and a frame repeated now and then (a 20 fps clip carried at 24), read above it. [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md#4-one-cycle-for-a-direction-set--video-cycle-align-video-set---align-cycles-auto), [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#4-loop--period-first-seam-second-then-the-gait-floor).

## v2.28.0 - A frame RIFE made whose legs melted is caught by the outline it lost, and the set alignment takes the nearer source frame there

- **Output change: which frames `video-cycle-align` keeps from RIFE.** `--between` gains `auto`, now the default (`video-set --align-between` too): every frame between two source frames is still made by RIFE and measured, and one that smeared or lost its outline takes the nearer source frame instead, named in the warnings. A set whose made frames all keep their outline comes out byte for byte as 2.27.0's; where one melted, that frame is now the nearer filmed frame, the loop's `cycle_align` gains `nearest_at` beside `made_at`, and `made_by_rife` counts only the frames kept. `--between rife` gives 2.27.0's frames, with the new warnings; `nearest` is unchanged.
- **Fix: a frame RIFE melted passed without a word.** Where two drawings are too far apart for the flow — legs crossing a long way, as a clip drawn on twos gives between its drawings — RIFE draws the legs as one shape of fill with no outline between or around them. 2.25.0's smear check warns only on dark pixels added, and a melted frame has fewer: it lost its outline. `rife.smear` now also measures `outline_loss`: of the frame's coverage edge (alpha from 0.1, so a pale ghost limb's edge counts), the share with no dark pixel within 2 px, beyond the less outlined neighbour's. Over 5 % (`align.OUTLINE_WARN`) the frame has the fault `outline`; over 0.1 % dark, as before, `smear` (`align.faults`). The dark count's other side does not do this: a clean frame half way between two drawings has fewer dark pixels than the darker one wherever they differ, and on two outlined legs RIFE's clean frames of a short step read `dark_excess` −1.3 %, lower than its melted frame of a long crossing (−1.1 %); `outline_loss` reads under 3 % and 32 % (`tests/video/test_rife.py`). `partial_excess` is still reported and not judged: a melted frame and a clean one read alike on it.
- **Every made frame says what became of it**: `cycle_align.smear` rows gain `method` (`rife`, kept, or `nearest`, the nearer source frame taken instead), `outline_loss` and `faults`; the report gains `replaced`, and `video-set`'s `cycle_align` per state too. A fault is a line in `warnings` under every `--between`: under `auto` it says the nearer source frame was taken there, under `rife` that the frame is kept and where to look. `auto` runs RIFE wherever `rife` does and needs it installed the same way. [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md#4-one-cycle-for-a-direction-set--video-cycle-align-video-set---align-cycles-auto).

## v2.27.0 - A set is not aligned with a loop that may hold two cycles until someone counts them, and only that count takes one cycle out of it

- **Output change: which sets `video-cycle-align` rewrites.** `video-loop`'s output is 2.25.0's and 2.26.0's: every cut keeps its period, length and start, whatever its legs or shading (the report gains `cycle.fundamental`, and `strip.json` gains `state`). `video-cycle-align` now **stops a set** — nothing rewritten, exit status 1 — where one of its loops may hold two or three cycles (a half or a third of it repeats and is no shorter than the gait floor), and 2.26.0 aligned it; the report is written with `applied: false`, `refused: "cycle-suspects"` and the typed `suspects`. A set with no suspect is aligned as before, every loop resampled from all of its filmed frames. `video-set --align-cycles auto` skips such a state with a warning (`applied: false`, `reason: "cycle-suspects"`, `suspects`) where it aligned it, and the RIFE skip now carries `reason: "rife-not-installed"`. The number of cycles in a loop changes only through the new `--cycles`.
- **Fix: a walk filmed through two strides walked twice as fast as its set, without a word.** A clip drawn on twos whose stride is an odd number of frames repeats better two strides on than one, so `video-loop` cut two strides as one loop, and `video-cycle-align`, which gives every loop one length on the assumption that each holds one cycle, squeezed both into it. The engine does not cut such a loop shorter on the pixels' word: half a stride on, the legs are back in place with near and far swapped, and where they are drawn alike that one step and a true stride half a drawing late are the same picture, so every rule that took the half took some one-stride walk or run to its half step.
