Instead the shorter repeat is screened (`sprite_gen/video/period.py`: `screen` measures the half and the third — `periodicity`, `pose` off the motion's path, and for the record the legs' `steps` and `follow` — and one rule, `period.verdict`, decides: a half or a third that repeats is a `suspect`, whatever its pose; nothing says "a cycle" and nothing says "not one"). `video-loop` records it (`cycle.fundamental`: `checked`, `suspects`, `legs`), and so does the local search of `--anchor motion-auto`. `video-cycle-align` screens each loop as a ring (`align.cycle_screen`) and stops a set on a suspect, naming the loop, where it returns (frames and seconds), the evidence and the command that settles it (`command`, the same alignment with `--cycles <loop>=<1|2>` to fill in); `--multi-cycle warn` aligns it as it is with a warning per suspect. The pose is not a reason to refuse: a rule that refused "another pose" missed most two-stride loops whose second stride was redrawn a pixel aside or a few per cent lighter, which in pixels is off the motion's path just as a swapped step is. The cost is the other way: a run's half step repeats too, so a run set stops nearly every time until its loops are counted ([docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md) section 4, "The fundamental period").
- **New `video-cycle-align --cycles <loop>=k`** (repeatable; the loop by its `--loop-dir`, its strip's name, its directory's name or a `video-set` item's name): how many cycles a loop holds, as counted by whoever looked at it (a person, or a vision call asked how often each foot lands). `1` aligns it as it is; `2` or `3` takes one cycle out of the loop as filmed (`cycle.source/`, `align.take_cycle`: round(L/k) frames from the start whose frame one cycle on is the most like it) and aligns that, without cutting the clip again. Loop rows record `cycles_given`, `cycle_screen` and `cycle_taken`; the report `cycles_given`, `suspects` and `multi_cycle`. Also new: `--multi-cycle fail|warn` and `--state` (the gait floor for loops cut before `strip.json` carried `state`).
- **The foot-strike signals moved to `sprite_gen/video/legs.py`** (`align.strike_signals` and the `align` constants still name them), with one more per frame, `gap` (the widest empty run in the foot band). The turn reads them as before.
- **Docs**: [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md) section 4 (two cycles in one loop are stopped and counted; `--cycles`), [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md) section 4 ("The fundamental period": the engine does not cut shorter on the pixels' word, because a gait's half step with the legs alike and a cycle half a drawing late are the same picture) and section 5, [docs/loop-review.md](docs/loop-review.md).

## v2.26.0 - The size hold reads a walk's change of size one cycle on, and a follow-through that would fold its region can take the largest gain that does not

- **Output change: which walk and run loops are held at their size.** `video-loop --anchor motion-auto` with `--size-hold auto` (the default) reads the clip's change of size one cycle on instead of off a line fitted through the clip (`gait_fallback.cycle_drift`). A clip whose reading crosses 1 % one way or the other is cut differently: one that starts from a standing pose and settles into the walk is no longer held (the line read the settle as a shrinking body), and one whose settle pulled the line under 1 % while the body did grow is now held. A clip held either way is scaled back by the change one cycle on, not by the line's, so its frames differ too: a clip whose first frames zoom in reads its walk's own change, not the zoom, and can find a cycle it did not find scaled by the line. A clip under 1 % both ways, and every clip with `--size-hold off`, is cut exactly as before. `video-follow` with its default `--on-fold refuse` writes what 2.24.0 wrote.
- **Fix: a first pose that settles into the walk read as a change of size.** The hold fitted a line to the subject's height over the clip, and a clip that stands tall in its first frames and then walks a few pixels lower read as a body shrinking the whole way: at 1 % or more it was scaled back. The hold now finds the lag at which the pose (cut out about its feet) matches itself best, compares each frame's height — to a fraction of a pixel, the rows' coverage summed — with the frames one, two, … of those lags later, and carries the median change per frame over the clip; the few frames of the first pose do not move the median of the rest. On a synthetic walker that stands 6 px taller for its first frames and walks at one size, the line read −2.2 % and, scaled back by it, the clip found no cycle; it now reads 0 and is cut as filmed. A walker that settles and grows 2.5 % read +0.6 % on the line and was not held, and found no cycle either; it is now held and cut. `size_hold` in the report carries the evidence: `method`, `lag`, `pose_match`, `pairs`, `per_lag` and `drift_fitted_line` (what the line would have read). The gait fallback after a failed search still reads the line at 3 %. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#4-loop--period-first-seam-second-then-the-gait-floor).
- **New `video-follow --on-fold lower`**: a move that would fold a region over itself is no longer only refused. The move is the gain times the move at gain 1, so the gain at which a region folds is known (2 · radius / (π · move at gain 1)); `lower` takes the largest gain under it, in steps of 0.01 and no more than `--gain`, one gain for the strip set by the region with the smallest radius, and names it on stderr. It never goes under 1, the mass as measured: a region too small for that is still refused, and the message names the largest gain that would not fold. The record says what was asked and what was used (`gain_requested`, `gain`, `on_fold`, and `fold`: lowered or not, the move asked for, the move at gain 1, per region the smaller radius, how near the used move is to folding it, and the gain at which it folds). The default stays `--on-fold refuse`: the same strip, GIF, WebP and refusal message as 2.24.0, byte for byte; the record only gains those keys. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#6-follow-through--video-follow).
- **Docs: a clip from a video MCP on your agent goes through the set stage and the size hold.** That route stopped at `video-loop`, so what `video-set` does for a set was left out. It now cuts a walk or run with `--anchor motion-auto` (the anchor that holds a clip with no end frame at its first frame's size; `video-loop`'s default for a walk, `body`, does not), takes `--fps` from the frames report, and ends with `video-cycle-align --view …` once a walk or run is cut in two or more directions (`SKILL.md` routes, [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#a-clip-from-a-video-mcp-on-your-agent--zcre) with `Set` and `Size` rows in its ZCRE table, [docs/user-workflow.md](docs/user-workflow.md), the READMEs). `video-cycle-align` is in the pipeline's diagram and verb table. `SKILL.md` forbids replacing any engine stage — extraction, the loop cut, set alignment, loop repair — with a script of your own: the engine's own options first, the defect reported, a new clip only for what no stage can reach. [docs/qa-motion.md](docs/qa-motion.md) says its "regenerate that row" is for image rows; a video loop is repaired and aligned by the engine first. No code or output changes.

## v2.25.0 - Every view of an aligned walk starts as the same foot lands, read off the legs, and RIFE leaves no black between crossing legs

- **Output change: every frame RIFE makes, and where every aligned loop starts.** Frames made by RIFE (`video-cycle-align`, `video-set --align-cycles auto`, `video-loop` jump repair) are drawn differently wherever the legs cross or the flow is unsure; frames taken as filmed are untouched. Every loop `video-cycle-align` turns may start on a different frame: on its legs instead of its top edge, and, with views, on one foot for the whole set. A loop already aligned starts where the new rule says the next time it is aligned (it is read from `cycle.source/`).
- **Fix: a black smear between crossing legs in a frame RIFE made.** RIFE runs its flow twice, once for the colour and once for the coverage, and where two legs pass each other the runs disagree: the coverage says body where the colour still carries what lay around the body. 2.24 premultiplied the colour over black, so the disagreement came out black. The colour image now carries the body's own colour around the body (`rife.bleed`: the colour from 0.8 % of the body's height inside its solid edge, past a drawn outline, pushed out over the transparent area), so a disagreement reads as the body's fill; inside the body the colour is the frame's own, outline included. On two outlined legs walking through each other the made frame at t = 0.5 had 6 % of its body dark beyond either neighbour; now none (`tests/video/test_rife.py`). A limb RIFE cannot follow is still soft and pale, never black. [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md#1-rife--what-runs-where-and-what-it-costs).
- **A smear is measured on every made frame, never passed over quietly.** `cycle_align.smear` lists per made frame `dark_excess` (dark pixels inside the body beyond the darker neighbour's count — a moved hat or watch keeps its count) and `partial_excess` (part-covered pixels beyond the more ragged neighbour's); over 0.1 % dark, the frame is named in the alignment report's `warnings`, on stderr and in `video-set`'s `warnings` (`rife.smear`, `align.SMEAR_WARN`).
- **New `video-cycle-align --between nearest`** (`video-set --align-between nearest`): a time between two source frames takes the nearer source frame, so nothing is made and no RIFE is needed; the motion keeps the filmed frames up to half a frame off their time. `rife` stays the default. A loop stretched this way shows a frame twice; its GIF and WebP are checked for one frame per run of identical cells, since both hold a repeated frame as one of twice the delay.
- **Fix: the loop started where an ear tip was lowest.** The turn took the frame whose alpha box top was lowest, and a long ear, a hat's point or an antenna owns that edge and flops on its own rhythm, so each view started at another point of its step. The start is now read off the legs (`align.foot_strike`, `turned_on` in the loop row): side and diagonal views on the widest stride of the foot band (the lowest 8 %), a heel landing in front; front and back views on the lowest solid row, the foot nearer the viewer drawn lowest; and only for a body with no legs to read, the body's top line, the first row at least half as wide as the frame's widest, so a narrow ear is passed over. With `--view` (and in `video-set`, always) the view picks the signal and the swings only say whether there are legs: a side or diagonal view where the foot band swings by 15 % of the body's height or the lowest row by 1.5 %, a front or back view where the lowest row swings by 1.5 %. A short-legged walk misleads a threshold either way — from the front the foot behind lifts out of the foot band, which then swings like a side stride mid-step; from the side a short step opens the feet by less than 15 % — so the thresholds choose the signal only without `--view`: the stride where it swings by 15 % or more, otherwise the lowest row where it swings by 1.5 % or more. A body that hardly bobs turns on its legs as one that bobs does (`tests/video/test_cycle_align.py`: a drawn walker whose ears are lowest half a step off its heel strike, with and without a bob; a big-headed, short-legged walker from the front and from the side).
- **New: every view starts as the same own foot lands** (`video-cycle-align --view front --view side@right …`, one per `--loop-dir`, `--start-foot right|left`, default right; `video-set` passes each item's view and facing). A walk lands twice a cycle, half a cycle apart, and until now the turn took either. The foot is told by how the view draws it (`align.strike_foot`, with `handedness.placement` for which own side is where): front and back by which foot is drawn lower (nearer the viewer), side and diagonal by which leg is shaded (the far one). Where the cue does not follow the step (its once-a-cycle swing under a quarter of its spread) or the two strikes do not differ enough, the foot is not named rather than guessed: the loop starts on its larger strike with `start_foot: null` and `foot_why`, and the report's `warnings` say so; without `--view` the report says once that no foot was chosen. Each loop row carries `view`, `start_foot` and `foot` (the cue, its values and margin). [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md#4-one-cycle-for-a-direction-set--video-cycle-align-video-set---align-cycles-auto).

## v2.24.0 - A walk filmed from its first frame loops at one size, a frame scaled up keeps its crown, and a soft part can follow the body

- **Output change: a walk or run loop whose clip changes size by 1 % or more is cut differently.** `video-loop --anchor motion-auto` now holds the clip at its first frame's size before the cycle search (`--size-hold auto`, the default; `size_hold` in the report): the subject's fitted height over the clip is measured (`gait_fallback.scale_drift`) and, at 1 % or more (`SIZE_HOLD_MIN`), every frame is scaled back to the first frame's height about its fitted foot point (`undo_scale`). Until now that ran only in the gait fallback, after the search had failed and at 3 % or more, so a clip that grew 1–3 % and still looped was cut on frames of changing size: the frame one cycle on was larger than the first, and the loop popped at the wrap. A clip under 1 % is cut exactly as before; `--size-hold off` gives the old cut. A clip that grows 2.5 % now loops at its size where it found no cycle before (`tests/video/test_gait_fallback.py`); a front catwalk filmed from its first frame (+2.0 %) went from a seam ratio of 1.44 to 1.19. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#4-loop--period-first-seam-second-then-the-gait-floor).
- **Fix: scaling a frame back up cut off what it carried past the frame's edge.** A clip that shrinks is scaled up about its feet; when the feet also rose in the frame (a back walk going away), the crown landed above the frame and was cut in every frame it reached. Frames are now widened by the room the furthest one needs (`gait_fallback.undo_padding`, the same for every frame, `padding_ltrb` in `size_hold` and `gait_fallback`), so nothing is lost; a clip that needs no room keeps its frame size and bytes.
- **New `sprite-gen video-follow`**: a soft part of a looping body (a chest, a belly, a pouch on a strap) follows the body's motion. An ellipse in the strip's first cell (`--region cx,cy,rx,ry`, repeatable) is moved as a damped mass on the body's own bob — the crown's row and the head's middle read off the cells, the periodic steady state of x'' + 2ζωx' + ω²x = −body'' per harmonic of the cycle (`--freq` 2.4 Hz, `--zeta` 0.6, `--gain` 2.5 times the physical answer) — so it lags and settles with no kick, and the last frame leads into the first. Only the region's pixels change; a move that would fold it over is refused. The strip, GIF and WebP are rewritten and checked, `follow` is recorded in the strip meta, and the strip as it was is kept as `follow.source.png`, so a second run never moves a moved strip. The region is the caller's to give: the engine does not find it. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#6-follow-through--video-follow).
- **`video-cycle-align` clears a follow-through**: it rebuilds the strip from the cut, so it removes `follow.source.png` and the `follow` record and says `follow_cleared` in the loop's row; a new `video-loop` cut removes them too. Run `video-follow` after the alignment.

| Before / after | |
|---|---|
| A back walk going away, scaled back up: 2.23.0 cut the crown at the cell's top (red line); 2.24.0 widens the frames first | ![crown kept](https://github.com/aldegad/sprite-gen/releases/download/v2.24.0/back-crown-before-after.gif) |
| `video-follow` on a hip pouch (zoomed): as cut, and following the walk's bob as a damped mass | ![pouch follows](https://github.com/aldegad/sprite-gen/releases/download/v2.24.0/follow-pouch-before-after.gif) |

## v2.23.0 - A walking side view shows the far wrist's item each time that arm swings forward; without `--handed` every prompt is 2.22.0's

- **Without `--handed`, every prompt is 2.22.0's, byte for byte.** Clips (built-in or `--motion`, every model and pin), `video-prompt`, stills (view, facing, reference, key line, layout guide, the `--facing-fix regen` regeneration), `video --direction side` and the mid-step redraw send what 2.22.0 sent. `tests/gen/test_prompt_freeze.py` holds every prompt string in that table to `tests/fixtures/prompts-v2.22.0.json.gz`, drawn from the 2.22.0 tag's source. The engine's pieces go on through one place (`sprite_gen/gen/prompt_parts.py`, `Prompt.add`), which puts them on as 2.22.0 did; with `--handed` the prompt is that same prompt with the handed sentences put in, and nothing else changes. [docs/prompt-assembly.md](docs/prompt-assembly.md) lists the faults of the 2.22.0 prompts kept until a before-and-after comparison says to change them.
- **Fix: a watch on the far wrist was in no frame of a side walk.** 2.22.0 said every item on the far side of a side view hidden, and never named it in the clip ("the wrist in front of the body stays bare"). A walking character's far arm comes forward of the body with every step, and what its wrist or hand wears shows then. An item on a wrist, a hand, a forearm or an elbow (`handedness.limb`, one table: `SWINGING_ARM_PARTS`) on the far arm is now said to show wherever that arm is out in front of the body: in the still ("wherever the far arm reaches out in front of the body, clear of its outline, the black smartwatch shows whole on it; where the body covers that arm, it is hidden") and in a walk or run ("stays on the wrist that wears it in the image, on the arm on the far side of the body, and shows each time that arm swings forward; the arm nearer the viewer stays bare, exactly as in the image"). On the near arm the walk says the other arm stays bare "both where it shows behind the body and where it swings out in front of it". Both start "Both arms swing back and forth with each step, opposite to the legs." (`handedness.ARMS_SWING_TEXT`, the one place that sentence lives), and no sentence holds an arm still. An item anywhere else — an ear, the head, a tail, the body, a leg, a shoulder, the upper arm — gets no arm sentence and keeps the 2.22.0 sentences, as does a state that does not step (idle, attack). [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#handedness--an-item-on-one-side).
- **A side view names the near part "nearer the viewer", not "in front of the body"** (still and clip sentences): in a mid-stride still the far arm is the one in front of the body, so "the wrist in front of the body stays bare" named the wrist with the watch.
- **A clip keeps what its first frame shows**: for a walk or run with an item on the far wrist or hand of a side view, `video-prompt --json` (`still_needs`, and stderr) and `video-set` (each item's `still_needs`) say the still must be drawn mid-stride with that arm out in front of the body and the item in view. The docs give the pose sentence for the prompt.
- **One handed sentence per wrist, not per item**: with an item on each wrist a side clip said "the wrist in front of the body stays bare" and "the red ribbon stays on the wrist in front of the body" together, and with two items on one wrist it said the same sentence twice. Items on one part share a sentence, a part another item is on is not called bare, and a still says "none of it" there. A diagonal's clip sentence also reads "…, nearer the viewer, for the whole clip" (the comma was missing). A start still's prompt handed back to `gen --direction --handed` carries the handed sentences once.
- **`handed-check` passes a far wrist's item in view and fails the near arm's** (side views): 2.22.0 failed every frame that showed the far item whole ("in full view on the far side"). An item on a far wrist, hand, forearm or elbow must now be out in front of the body in every frame it shows — on the facing side of the body's centre by at least a tenth of the body's height (`far_in_front`); at or behind the centre it is on the near arm. With the new `--state walk|run` it must also show in at least a quarter of the frames (`far_shown`; unchecked without `--state` or for a state that does not step). A side view's near item must show in three quarters of the frames, not half: a far item turned over to face the other way shows in about half. Any other far item keeps the hidden-or-a-sliver rule (`far_hidden`), and a diagonal's near side keeps half.
- **The caller's own words are reported, not edited.** Text that turns the subject another way than `--facing` (or to a side in a front or back view), or puts a `--handed` item on its other side, is sent as written with a warning; text that repeats an option gets a note. `gen` prints them and records `extra.prompt_notes`; `video-prompt --json` lists conflicts in `warnings` and all of them in `notes`; `video-set` records `prompt_notes` per item. The prompt is the same with or without them.
- **`tests/gen/test_prompt_assembly.py`** draws every prompt in the option table as text — views, facings, items on a wrist or an ear, what the caller's text already says, states, clip models, the start still and the hand-offs between verbs — and checks each for the prompt without `--handed` plus the handed piece and nothing else, a handed sentence said twice, a limb called bare and dressed at once, an arm sentence where no wrist item is or no step swings it, and the notes.

## v2.22.0 - A character with an item on one side has its left-facing views drawn, not mirrored, and an attack is filmed with less room above

- **Handedness, said once** (`sprite_gen/gen/handedness.py`): `--handed "<item>=<left|right> [part]"` names an item on one of the character's own sides ("the black smartwatch=left wrist"); the engine works out where that side is in each view — the picture's right or left in a front, back or diagonal view, near or far in a side or diagonal view (`placement`) — and says it in the prompts (`text`). `sprite-gen gen --direction <view> [--facing …] --handed …` adds the engine's still view sentence and, per item, which side it is on, where that is in the view, and that the sentence outranks an attached picture; with `--handed`, `--facing-fix mirror` is refused and `regen` never falls back to a mirror. `video-prompt --handed` and `video-set --handed` end the clip prompt with where the item stays, anchored to the first frame and named once where it shows; on the far side of a side view it is not named at all — the near arm "stays bare, exactly as in the image" — since a clip prompt that names a hidden item draws it on the near arm. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#handedness--an-item-on-one-side).
- **Left-facing views drawn, not mirrored — opt-in**: `video-set --facing right,left` films every side and diagonal view both ways, each from its own still (`--base side@right=… --base side@left=…`); a turned view missing a still for one facing is refused, naming the `gen --direction … --facing …` call that draws it. Items are named `<view>-<facing>-<state>`, the table names the facing, each side still is inspected for its own facing, and gaits are aligned across all eight. With `--handed`, a front or back walk's mid-step redraw is told the item's side as well. Mirroring stays the default (drawing costs as much again); one facing keeps the item names and table as before.
- **New `sprite-gen handed-check`** checks a still or loop frame by frame: a colour-marked item (`--marker`, a saturated colour nothing else on the character has) must sit on its view's picture side of the body's centre, in one place only, showing in at least half the frames on a near side and, against a `--reference` front or back picture, no more than a sliver on the far side of a side view and more than a sliver on its near side — a side view has no picture side, so a far sliver turned over to face the other way does not count as the near item shown. Without a reference both size rules are reported unchecked (`far_hidden`, `near_whole`). `--strap --zone` adds the item's band seen without its marker (a bare strap on the other arm) within the rows it sits in. Specks of the marker's hue left on outlines by a clip's colour blocks are recorded, not judged. `--board` draws every frame with its verdict; exit 1 on a failure. Drawn test figures pass as they should look and fail turned over in every view, a side view's far sliver against `--reference` (`tests/qa/test_handed_check.py`).
- **Fix: a diagonal facing left was filmed heading right.** The diagonal walk and run sentences said "to the right" and "toward the upper right" whatever `--facing` was; they now take the facing ("heading diagonally toward the viewer and to the left"). `video-prompt` and `video-set` also cut a left-facing diagonal's loop with `--facing left` (it was always `right`), which mirrors the jump repair's hair box. Right-facing prompts are unchanged.
- **A `gen --transparent` run with `--ref` keeps the alpha it comes back with**: `auto` still plans a chroma key before the model runs (2026-09-08: codex drew a checkerboard in 5 of 6 ref runs asked for alpha), but the raw is read before the key is applied (`chroma.classify_raw_alpha`). A raw with an alpha band, at least 5 % of it at alpha 0 and at least half of its border at alpha 0 is published on its own alpha through the native check, with `alpha.strategy: "native"` and `alpha.strategy_source: "refs-attached-raw-alpha"`. Keying it read the RGB under alpha 0 as the background and took the outline and light fills off: a transparent reference is answered with alpha, and a fox redrawn from one lost its outline and cream belly. A checkerboard, a key background or an opaque RGBA (no alpha band, or no alpha 0) is keyed as before (`refs-attached`); alpha 0 that misses either bar is keyed as before with a `warning`. `alpha.raw_alpha` records the verdict and its numbers on every ref run that planned the key. `--alpha-mode chroma` keys whatever comes back. [docs/gen.md](docs/gen.md#transparent-output--strategy-per-provider).
- Measured 2026-10-04 on codex `image_gen` (subscription), one transparent front still as the reference, three takes per arm: with the planned key's prompt, a transparent reference came back with real alpha 3/3 and the same still on white came back on opaque white 3/3; asked for alpha (`--alpha-mode native`), both came back with real alpha 3/3, with no checkerboard in 6 runs. The step down is kept until a larger measurement replaces the 2026-09-08 one.
- **The step down asks for the key it plans**: a `gen --transparent --ref` prompt that names no key background gets the `--chroma-key` key's line (`chroma.KEY_BACKGROUND_TEXT`, moved from `video.batch` so a clip's mid-step redraw and this run say the same sentence); a prompt that names a key — its hex code, or "magenta/green background", "chroma key", "screen" — is left as it is, with no second line. `alpha.key_background` records `injected` and the key. A reference on white with no key in the prompt came back on opaque white (3/3) and keying it took the outline and the cream fills; with the line, five takes (three on that reference, two on the transparent one) came back on a magenta key and kept both. `--alpha-mode chroma` and native runs are unchanged. [docs/gen.md](docs/gen.md#transparent-output--strategy-per-provider).
- **`--decontam` is skipped, not refused, on a ref raw that came back transparent**: it has no key to remove, and the refusal came after the paid generation. `alpha.decontam` records what was asked and why it was skipped, and stderr says so (a warning for `palette`).
- `chroma.classify_raw_alpha` judges on the pixel counts, not the rounded percentages: a raw with alpha 0 under 0.005 % was `no-alpha` and keyed with no warning; it is `ambiguous` now.
- **An attack canvas keeps 20 % of its height above the still, not 35 %** (`STATE_CANVAS["attack"]`; 28 % in front and 20 % behind are unchanged). The extra room only made the body a smaller part of the clip, so `video-loop --body-height` scaled it up to the delivery height; with 20 % the body is larger in the clip and is scaled down. A 1024 px square still now pads to 2276×1280 instead of 2800×1575. 20 % still holds a weapon raised about a third of the body above the head: the canvas adds at least a quarter of the still's height above it, and the still's own margin over the crown does the rest (8 % of its height is enough). A still cropped at the crown needs `--headroom 0.26` or more for an overhead swing. At this headroom the 16:9 ratio, not `--lead`, sets the width of a square still's canvas. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#1-canvas--the-input-frame-decides-the-output-frame).
- **`--shape wide` keeps 35 % above** (`WIDE_OVERRIDE`, no longer the attack row): it is forced on states whose own row is another shape — `video-set --shape wide` across a batch, or a canvas widened after the subject touched a side edge — and a jump is one of them; 35 % on 16:9 keeps about the room a jump's tall row leaves above the still. A forced-wide canvas is the same as before; an attack in a `--shape wide` batch gets that row, as a cheer or a projectile already did.

## v2.21.0 - A clip made by a video MCP on your agent (ZCRE) becomes a loop: the prompt comes out, the mp4 goes in

- **New `sprite-gen video-prompt`** prints the clip prompt `video-set` sends for one (direction, state) — `build_prompt` with `--character`, `--motion` and `--model` — so an agent that makes the clip itself, with a video MCP connected to it, sends the same sentence. `--json` adds the duration (the state's own), whether the clip ends on the canvas (`last_frame`), the loop cut (`cycle`), the canvas, clip, frames and loop steps with placeholders, and for a front or back walk the mid-step redraw (`start_still`). sprite-gen never calls the MCP: the agent uploads the `video-canvas` still, quotes, asks the user, generates and saves the mp4, and `video-frames` and `video-loop` take it from there. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md#a-clip-from-a-video-mcp-on-your-agent--zcre).
- **ZCRE (지크) is that route's first source**: its remote MCP, signed in with your own ZCRE account and credits, has `grok-imagine-video-1.5` (Grok Imagine Pro) for image-to-video, with no end frame and no Lite model. `--no-last-frame` refuses what `video-set` films pinned — idle, attack, a walk or run in a diagonal — and `--unpinned` films one anyway with a warning (the loop is searched instead of cut whole and may not close). Walk, run and jump from the side, front and back are supported.
- **`video-frames` reads the picture of a clip that carries more**: it takes the first video stream that is not a cover image (`attached_pic`, `stream_index`), and records `codec`, `pix_fmt`, `audio_streams` and `cover_streams`. A ZCRE clip always has an AAC track and an mjpeg cover; the audio is never extracted and the loop outputs carry no sound.
- Measured once end to end: one side walk, 720p 3 s, 52 credits, 33.6 s to generate; the clip was 960×960 H.264 `yuv420p` with an AAC track and a cover, and it cut to a periodic 20-frame loop.
- **The places that moved or scaled a keyed picture with BICUBIC map it with coverage and colour apart** (`transform_cell`, beside `resize_cell` in `sprite_gen/util/resample.py`): the gait fallback's scaled-back frames (`video-loop --anchor motion-auto` on a walk that grows or shrinks through the clip) and a curated move, scale, rotation or shear baked on a smooth row (`curation.apply_transform`, so `compose`, the GIF, the PNG export and the cycle bake). BICUBIC over premultiplied RGBA has the same negative lobe as LANCZOS, smaller, and drew the same lighter rim and key tint at the edge. On the synthetic outlined figure (`tests/keyed_figure.py`) it left 1,088 to 1,864 pixels with a colour none of the four source pixels under them had when a frame was scaled back by 0.84x to 1.19x, and 840 to 2,102 for a half-pixel move, a 10° rotation, a 0.75x shrink, a shear and a 1.3x grow with a 15° rotation; none are left. Coverage keeps BICUBIC's edge, held to the coverage of the 2 x 2 source pixels the colour mixes from; colour is a BILINEAR mix of premultiplied colour, so it is no longer sharpened. A pixel row's curated transform (`fit.pixel_unfake`, NEAREST and re-snapped) and an identity transform are unchanged. The scene GIF's downscale stays LANCZOS: it scales opaque composited frames, where there is no coverage to divide by. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md), [docs/loop-review.md](docs/loop-review.md), [docs/curation.md](docs/curation.md), [docs/pixel-unfake.md](docs/pixel-unfake.md).

## v2.20.0 - A keyed picture is scaled without a light rim or a green tint at the edge wherever the engine scales one: a row frame, a sliced sheet, a scene layer

- **The places that scaled a keyed picture with LANCZOS now use the loop's resample** (`resize_cell`, now in `sprite_gen/util/resample.py`): a row frame shrunk into its cell (`extract`, `fit.resample: lanczos`, the default), the plain and original-quality twins beside a pixel-unfake frame (`.plain.png`, `orig/`), a sliced sheet's figures (`slice-sheet`) and a scene's layers (`scene-render`). Each used LANCZOS over premultiplied RGBA and drew the same lighter rim and key tint at the edge as the loop's strip did before 2.19.0. On the synthetic outlined figure (`tests/keyed_figure.py`), LANCZOS left 137 pixels at 0.2x, 543 at 0.5x, 978 at 0.75x and 7,315 at 2x with a colour none of the source pixels under them had; none are left at any of them. `fit.resample: lanczos` keeps its name and stays the smooth shrink (coverage is still LANCZOS); `nearest`, `kcentroid` and pixel-unfake frames are unchanged, and a picture at its own size is copied as it is. Colour is no longer sharpened, so a scaled scene layer with no transparency (a painted background) is a little softer than LANCZOS drew it. A tween's scale (`normalize_tween_scale`) is left on LANCZOS: it scales an opaque canvas before the key is removed, where there is no coverage to divide by. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md), [docs/pixel-unfake.md](docs/pixel-unfake.md), [docs/sheet-slicing.md](docs/sheet-slicing.md), [docs/scene.md](docs/scene.md).

## v2.19.0 - A loop's cells are scaled without a light rim or a green tint at the edge, and front and back stills keep their view

- **`video-loop` scales its cells with coverage and colour taken apart** (`resize_cell`, used by `build_strip`, so the strip, the GIF, the WebP and `video-cycle-align`'s rebuild all get it). The strip used LANCZOS over premultiplied RGBA, whose negative lobes weigh the colours across an edge against each other and then divide by the edge's low coverage; on a keyed frame, whose edge holds a light rim outside a dark outline and ink with a little of the key's green, a scaled cell drew a lighter rim and greener ink than the frame had. Coverage now keeps LANCZOS's edge, held between the least and the most coverage of the source pixels the colour mixes from (no halo outside the silhouette, no hole inside it); colour is a Hamming mix of premultiplied colour, which has no negative lobe, so every pixel's colour is a mix of the colours under it. On a synthetic outlined figure (`tests/video/test_strip_resample.py`), LANCZOS left 1,462 pixels at 0.97x and 1,750 at 1.03x with a colour none of the source pixels under them had, and none are left now; the strands, 0.5-1.4 px wide, keep LANCZOS's coverage. Colour is no longer sharpened, so a 1 px line inside the body is a little softer when a cell is enlarged. A cell at its own size is copied as it is, as before. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md).
- **A front or back still keeps its view when redrawn from another picture** (`STILL_VIEW_TEXT["front"]`, `["back"]`, read by `still_view_text`): redrawn from a side picture with the clip's one line, a front still could keep part of the picture's turn — feet frontal, head and chest turned a little toward the side the picture faces. The still sentence now says the whole body and head face the viewer squarely, the chest, hips and toes point straight at the viewer, the face is centred between both ears and it is not turned toward either side even when a reference picture shows the character from another angle; the back one says the same from behind, the face hidden and not looking back over the shoulder. It is used whatever the reference's view (a caller does not always know it). The clip's `VIEW_TEXT` is unchanged.
- Measured on codex `image_gen` (subscription), five humanoid characters, three takes per arm, judged blind on full-resolution crops plus an eye-offset metric: side reference to front, 15 of 15 frontal with the old line (the turn did not reproduce on this model) and 15 of 15 with the new; side reference to back, 15 of 15 straight backs either way; front reference to front, 14 of 15 old and 15 of 15 new. Method: [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md).

## v2.18.0 - A walk's jumping frames are remade by RIFE, its jolt is measured, and a direction set shares one cycle

- **Jump frames are repaired** (`video-loop --repair auto`, the default for walk and run): a step whose coverage change is 1.4x the loop's median, over the whole body or the hair behind it (`--facing left` mirrors that box), is a jump, and the frame that broke it is replaced by the RIFE frame half way between its two neighbours — at most three, worst first, never next to a frame already made; every other frame stays the video's own. A single stray frame, which breaks the step into it and out of it, is the one remade. The report's `jump_repair` names the frames, the scores before and after and the interpolator; with a frame replaced the seam gate reads the rendered cells. `--repair off` cuts as filmed. On the 2026-10-03 eight-direction set it replaces the frames the experiment did in 8 of 10 views (the other two differ by one frame).
- **RIFE** is `rife-ncnn-vulkan` 20221029 with `rife-v4.6`, found by `SPRITE_GEN_RIFE`, then `PATH`, then where `sprite-gen rife install` puts it, and located only when a frame is to be made. Colour is interpolated premultiplied and coverage separately. Measured: 0.33-0.41 s a call on an M4 Max GPU; 2.8-3.0 s in a Modal Linux container through Mesa llvmpipe, with the same frame; the binary's own CPU path (`-g -1`) is wrong and never passed. Code and weights are MIT. CI installs it with `sprite-gen rife install` and runs it on llvmpipe.
- **Install RIFE in one line**: new `sprite-gen rife install` downloads the release zip for macOS, Linux or Windows (x86-64), checks its pinned SHA-256 before unpacking, keeps the binary, the model and the licence (about 35 MB), and puts them in the user data directory (`SPRITE_GEN_DATA_DIR`, else `~/.local/share/sprite-gen` or `$XDG_DATA_HOME`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%` on Windows), staged and renamed into place. It ends with a check frame through the installed binary. `--zip` installs from a zip already downloaded, `--dir` installs elsewhere and prints the `SPRITE_GEN_RIFE` line, `--force` unpacks again.
- **A walk still cuts without RIFE, and says so**: where a frame is to be made and no RIFE is installed, `video-loop --repair auto` cuts the loop as filmed — `jump_repair` reads `applied: false` with the reason and the install line, and stderr carries one warning — and `video-set` skips that state's cycle alignment (`cycle_align.<state>.applied: false`, listed under the set report's `warnings`, one warning line). Asked for by name, it fails: `video-loop --repair on` and `video-cycle-align`. A RIFE that is found and then fails is an error in every mode. Cutting a loop again removes its `cycle.source/`, so the next alignment reads the new cut.
- **The jolt index is reported on every walk and run** (`jolt` in the loop report), read after the repair: how far each step strays from its neighbours' mean over the median step, the hair's, and the head's frame-to-frame moves sideways and up and down in % of the body height (largest, median, largest over median, range). Beyond the reference bounds (index 0.43, head sideways 0.75 %) it is a warning line and the loop is kept; `--jolt-max` / `--head-step-max` turn a bound into a gate (`video-loop: loop jolts — …`). As a default gate the bounds would have refilmed 16 of 30 Lite takes on two refusals' evidence, and the index does not separate an alternating side walk from a kept Pro walk (0.414), so they stay a reference.
- **One cycle per direction set**: new `sprite-gen video-cycle-align --loop-dir … --loop-dir …` resamples every loop to the set's median length at times k·L/L* — a time on a source frame keeps that frame, only times between frames are made by RIFE — turns each to start on a foot strike, and rebuilds its cycle, strip, GIF and WebP in place (`cycle_align` in `strip.json`). The cut as filmed is kept in `cycle.source/`, so a second run never resamples a resampled loop. `video-set` runs it for every walk or run filmed in two or more directions (`--align-cycles auto`, default). Strip metadata now records `cell_height_cap`.
- **Measured walk defaults**: `build_prompt(model=...)` calms a Lite walk (`LITE_WALK_TEXT`; Lite read "walks naturally" as a run) and holds a Lite back-diagonal walk's head (`LITE_HEAD_TEXT`; sideways sway 2.7-3.8 % fell to 1.7-3.1 %, Pro 0.88 %); Pro prompts are unchanged. A front or back walk films from its base still redrawn mid-step (`WALK_START_TEXT`, `walk_start_prompt`, `starts_mid_step`; 1 of 14 front clips walked straight from a standing still, 14 of 16 from a mid-step one); `video-set --walk-start redraw` (default) draws it with one `sprite-gen gen --ref` call.
- Method, measurements and the regression on the eight-direction set: [docs/loop-repair.md](docs/loop-repair.md).
- **`video-frames --decontam palette` adds no edge contact**: within the edge band the gate reads (4 px of the top, left and right edges), a pixel the matte left transparent stays transparent (`decontaminate(edge_band=...)`, `cutout(decontam_edge_band=...)`, `edge_band` in the report's `decontam`). A decoded frame's blur runs the subject on into the key as a smooth tail that the video fit reads as a faint blend, so a head a few pixels under the frame edge got faint flank coverage on the edge band and a clip that passes with decontam off was refused with it on, as "the key survived the matte". No floor tells that tail from a strand's side, so the rule is positional; the flank keeps what it recovers everywhere else, and the still fit is unchanged. [docs/chroma-alpha.md](docs/chroma-alpha.md#decontam--give-the-edge-the-subjects-own-colour-back).

## v2.17.0 - Two three-quarter views: a character walks down and up to the right as in an isometric game

- `front_diagonal` and `back_diagonal` join `side`, `front` and `back` (`VIEW_TEXT`): three-quarter views turned to the right, the two an isometric game adds between the side and the front or back. Turned over (`video-loop` outputs mirrored), they give the left-hand two, so five views make eight directions.
- A walk or run in a diagonal names its heading on the screen (`VIEW_MOTION_TEXT`): "heading diagonally away from the viewer toward the upper right, like a character walking up and to the right in an isometric game … It keeps the exact three-quarter back angle of the image the whole time … It never turns into a side view." It is filmed pinned to its first frame with the return sentence (`PINNED_GAIT_VIEWS`, `pins_last_frame`, used by `video-set` too) and cut as any walk. A caller's own gait in a diagonal is held to the angle (`GAIT_HOLD_TEXT`).
- A still drawn at a diagonal from another picture has its own view sentence (`STILL_VIEW_TEXT`, `still_view_text(direction, facing)`): the whole body and head turned about 45 degrees, the face hidden for the back one, the feet pointing the same way. The clip's view sentence points at the image instead ("turned exactly as in the image").
- Measured on the API, 480p 3 s, pinned, five humanoid characters twice each: the front and the back diagonal walk kept their angle in 10 of 10 each; the sentence that only said to walk "toward the way its body faces in the image" kept a back diagonal in 0 of 2. Idle and attack keep a diagonal angle (4 of 4). A diagonal run kept it in 2 of 4. Method: [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md).

## v2.16.0 - Every walk and run moves naturally in place, and a caller's own gait is held there

- Every walk and run, from every view, now says only that it moves naturally, which way it faces and that it stays in place (`MOTION_TEXT`, `VIEW_MOTION_TEXT`): the side walk "walks naturally in place, as if on a treadmill, without moving across the screen", the side run "runs naturally in place …", and the front and back runs "runs naturally in place, facing (away from) the viewer, as if on a treadmill …". The side sentences said "moves in place on a treadmill" as a place (the form that sometimes drew a treadmill under the feet) and the side walk asked for "an even left-right or front-back rhythm" (the wording that made front walks step sideways before 2.13); the front run spelled its strides out (the form that made front walks march before 2.15) and said "toward the viewer" and "without coming any closer" in one clause. On one character, 480p 3 s on the API: the front run kept facing in 2 of 2, the side run ran in 2 of 2, the back run kept facing away in the one clip whose frames passed, and the side walk walked in 2 of 2.
- A caller's own walk or run paragraph (`build_prompt(motion=...)`) is followed by `GAIT_HOLD_TEXT` for its view: it stays in place as if on a treadmill and keeps facing the viewer, away from the viewer or `{facing}` the whole time. A request interpreter can now ask for a sneak, a march or a lazy stroll and the engine keeps it on the spot and turned the way the image faces, as it already held an attack's grip (`HOLD_TEXT`). On one character, one clip each: a march lifted the knees high and stayed in place, a stroll from behind kept facing away, a tiptoe sneak kept facing the viewer but read only as a slightly hunched walk.
- `build_prompt(..., pinned=True)` gives any state the pinned template (`PINNED_LOOP_TEXT`, the return to the first pose) instead of the evenly paced repeat; left out, only `PINNED_LOOP_STATES` get it, as before. A caller that pins a walk or run (`video --last-frame`) says so; two pinned front walks closed on their first frame. Method: [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md).

## v2.15.0 - A front walk walks naturally, and a slow or drifting walk still finds its loop

- A walk seen from the front or from behind now says only that it walks naturally, which way it faces and that it stays in place (`VIEW_MOTION_TEXT`): "walks naturally in place, facing the viewer, as if on a treadmill, without coming any closer". The 2.13 sentence spelled out the steps, and some takes marched in place with the knees lifted to the waist and the arms held stiff. On one character, two clips per sentence on the API: the 2.13 sentence marched in one of two; "walks naturally" swung the arms, kept the knees low and kept facing the viewer in both. How a character walks is the caller's to say, so the default stays generic. The front run keeps its sentence. [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md).
- When `video-loop --anchor motion-auto` finds no walk or run cycle, it now looks once more (`sprite_gen/video/gait_fallback.py`). A front walk asked to stay in place sometimes comes toward the camera, and the body grows through the clip, so the same pose never matches itself in size: when a straight-line fit of the subject's height changes by 3 % or more over the clip, every frame is scaled back to the first frame's height about its foot point, and the search, the cells and the seam gate all read the scaled-back frames. A calm walk in long clothing can also take longer than half the clip per cycle, the most the first search confirms: the second search allows up to 60 % of the clip and at most 2 s (43 frames of a 3 s clip at 24 fps, where the first stops at 36).
- Only a failed search reaches this, so a clip that looped before is cut exactly as before, and every gate, the seam limit included, is unchanged. An explicit `--max-len` stays the ceiling. The report records what was tried in `gait_fallback`; when the second search fails too, the error still starts with `video-loop: no periodic cycle found` and says what was tried.
- Measured on six stored 3 s front walks that had failed with `no periodic cycle found`: four loop now. Three takes of a kimono that hides the legs walked a 27 to 39 frame cycle (one clip grew 3.9 %, two grew 11 % and 16 %), and a front horse walk grew 6.4 % with a 42 frame cycle. One of the other two still shows no repeat; the other finds a 38 frame candidate that the seam gate refuses. On the walk clips we have kept, the median height change is 0.5 % and nine in ten stay under 2.7 %. Method: [docs/loop-review.md](docs/loop-review.md).

## v2.14.0 - A single still can be drawn to a layout guide with crown and floor lines

- `gen --layout-guide` attaches the one-slot form of the row layout guide after any `--ref`: the frame, the inner safe box at the row guide's 9.4 % margin, the centre line, and an orange crown line and a teal floor line one margin inside the safe box (about 19 % and 81 % of the frame height). The prompt says what each line means: the anatomical top of the skull on the orange line, ignoring hair, hats, raised limbs and props; the supporting soles on the teal line, which is a ground-contact line and not a frame edge; nothing of the guide in the output. A still drawn from words tends to fill its frame top to bottom, and an in-place motion then has no room above the head. The lines sit inside the box because on its edges a crown line leaves hair and hats to fill the room above it and a floor line reads as a border the feet float above.
- The guide is an attached image: openai sends the call to `/images/edits` and bills the guide as image input, and `--transparent` steps down to chroma keying as with any `--ref`. Models differ in how closely they follow it; measure the one you use. The report records the guide's cell in `extra.layout_guide`. Usage: [docs/gen.md](docs/gen.md).

## v2.13.0 - A front or back walk walks straight, and a walk loop is cut in seconds

- A walk seen from the front or from behind, and a run seen from the front, now have their own sentence (`VIEW_MOTION_TEXT` in `sprite_gen/video/batch.py`): the steps lift and land straight forward and back under the body, toward the viewer or away from it "as if on a treadmill", and the body never turns to the side, steps sideways or crosses its feet. A walk also asks for a calm, natural cycle with small, even steps that never kick a leg out to the side. The walk sentence every view shared asked for "an even left-right or front-back rhythm", and a character facing the viewer read it as stepping sideways: crossed feet, side kicks, a body turned to three-quarters. A side view and a run seen from behind keep the state's sentence.
- Measured on Grok (subscription), 480p 3 s, nine humanoid characters twice each, judged on frame sheets with the variant hidden: a front walk kept its facing and walked in 7 of 18 clips with the old sentence and 16 of 18 with the new one (12 to 13 of 18 without the calm-steps clause, which still crossed its feet or kicked sideways in five); a back walk in 8 of 10 and 10 of 10; a front run in 11 of 18 and 15 of 18. "on a treadmill" said as a place was sometimes drawn under the feet; "as if on a treadmill" was not. A walk drawn at a three-quarter angle still turns to a side view in about half the clips with every sentence tried. Method: [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md).
- `video-loop --anchor motion-auto` finds its two tracking regions with FFT correlations instead of copying the masked patch out once per offset. On three 3 s walk clips at 544 px the region search took 1.0 to 1.4 s of CPU instead of 22.6 to 37.4 s, and full loops from the same frames gave the same cycle and byte-identical strips. Costs agree with the direct form to about 1e-6, so two offsets that tie within that may swap. [docs/loop-review.md](docs/loop-review.md).

## v2.12.1 - The error body is masked as the provider sent it, before it is put on one line

- The error body is now masked string by string, as the provider sent it, before sprite-gen puts it on one line. v2.12.0 masked the finished line, where JSON, `repr` and the line join had already written a line break, a tab or another control or invisible character as `\n`, `\t`, `\x..` or `\u....` (and the video verbs' JSON fallback any non-ASCII character as `\u....`). A key, a `Bearer` value or a URL query right after one of those kept a letter or digit in front of it and was printed as sent, so v2.12.0's "only a letter or digit in front makes `sk-`/`xai-`/`bearer` part of a word" was wrong there. It holds now, and the letter or digit is the provider's own: `task-…` and `xbearer …` are still words, and so is a key after the two characters `\n` or `%0A` the provider wrote itself. A `Bearer` value after a tab or a line break, and the credential the request carried when it holds a quote or a non-ASCII character, are masked too. An API key or login token of the usual shape that the request carried was masked in every case in v2.12.0 too, and still is (a number in the body equal to it included).
- A URL query is masked wherever the URL starts; after `_`, a letter or a non-ASCII letter it was printed as sent.
- Nothing else in the line changes: a body with nothing to mask prints byte for byte what v2.12.0 printed. When two field names of one error object mask to the same text, the line keeps the later one. A body nested some 500 levels deep can exceed Python 3.11's recursion limit while it is masked; the line then turns into a traceback (with no body text in it) instead of a refusal line. Provider error bodies are a few levels deep.

## v2.12.0 - A refusal names the provider's code, and says when it was the content policy

- A provider refusal line now carries the provider's own code after the status: `openai-gen: image request failed (HTTP 400) code=invalid_value; no retry or provider fallback`. The code is `error.code` (or, for xAI's synchronous errors, a top-level `code`) and is printed only when it matches `^[A-Za-z0-9_.:-]{1,64}$`. The video verbs read the code out of a failed poll's `error` object too.
- The image paths now print the provider's error body too, as received, at the end of the line: `openai-gen: image request failed (HTTP 400) code=invalid_value; no retry or provider fallback: {"error": {...}}` (JSON on one line, other text with its line breaks as `\n`, nothing for an empty body; this includes 401/403). Before, the body was dropped and a failure was hard to diagnose. Two things are masked with `[redacted]`: strings shaped like an API key (`sk-…`, `xai-…`, a `Bearer` value, the credential the request carried; a key the provider already masked is left alone) and URL query strings, where signatures live. The video verbs, which already printed what xAI said, now go through the same mask, applied before their 300-character cut so a key the cut would split is still masked. A key or a `Bearer` value right after `_` or `-` (`api_key_sk-…`, `x_Bearer …`) is masked too; only a letter or digit in front makes `sk-`/`xai-`/`bearer` part of a word. The body can repeat the prompt: a product that shows sprite-gen's stderr to its own users decides what to keep. Everything in front of the body is unchanged.
- A code the provider documents as a content-policy block turns the line into `<verb>: refused by the provider's content policy (HTTP <n>) code=<code>; no retry or provider fallback`, so a caller can tell "change the request" from an auth, quota or parameter error by one fixed phrase. OpenAI's `moderation_blocked` is the only such code (the image guide calls `error.code` the stable discriminator); its `moderation_details.moderation_stage` follows as `moderation_stage=input|output|unknown`. xAI's `invalid_argument` is not one: it covers moderation and parameter errors alike.
- An xAI clip that finishes with `video.respect_moderation: false` is now `<verb>: generation <id> refused by the provider's content policy (HTTP 200) respect_moderation=false; nothing was written` instead of "done but reported no video url". The xAI REST image answer has no moderation field, so a grok image refusal carries its code only.
- `(HTTP <n>)` is unchanged, and the body comes after it and everything else, so anything that finds the status with `\(HTTP (\d{3})\)` reads these lines as before, whatever the body says. The policy table is `sprite_gen/gen/refusal.py` `POLICY_CODES`. Line shape: [docs/gen.md](docs/gen.md).

## v2.11.0 - An attack is one strike, and its whole clip is the loop

- `MOTION_TEXT["attack"]` asks for one attack (windup, strike, held impact pose, recovery to the exact starting stance) instead of the same attack twice, and `build_prompt(motion=...)` no longer adds a repeat sentence after a caller's paragraph (`REPEAT_TEXT` is empty; `HOLD_TEXT` still follows it once). The clip is pinned to the canvas as before, so it runs stance -> strike -> stance, and `video-set` cuts it whole with `video-loop --cycle pinned`, as it already did an idle (`PINNED_LOOP_STATES` = idle, attack). The attack keeps its own template (`ACTION_TEXT_STATES`). Before, the one-shot or period search cut a two-strike clip into loops that started mid-strike, held both strikes, or kept only the held pose, and some of those passed the seam gate.
- `video-set` asks attack clips for 2 s (`STATE_DURATION_SECONDS`), down from 4. One strike at the stated timing is about 1.6 s; asked for 3 or 4 s, the clip still struck once and held the impact pose for about half of it.
- Measured on Grok (subscription), 13 characters pinned to their canvas: 13 of 13 loops at 2 s in 480p and in 720p, 13 of 13 at 3 s, 12 of 13 at 4 s (one clip touched the frame edge); every loop is the whole clip and starts on the ready stance. A 2 s clip is half the video seconds of the 4 s one.

## v2.10.3 - An attack keeps what the other hand holds where it is drawn

- What stays still in an attack is one sentence, `HOLD_TEXT["attack"]`: every grip stays the one the image shows (one hand stays one hand, both hands stay both hands, nothing let go or switched to the other hand), a hand the strike does not use stays where it is drawn with whatever it holds, and the body keeps facing the same direction. The built-in attack sentence ends with it instead of naming the grip twice, and `build_prompt(motion=...)` now adds it once after a caller's own motion paragraph, before the repeat count. A request interpreter writes its motion before the still exists, so it cannot know where a shield or a lantern is drawn; asked to keep "the shield raised in front", a clip brought a shield drawn behind the body to the front and the strike read as a shield bash. The caller writes the choreography, the engine says what stays put.
- Measured on Grok (subscription), 4 s 480p pinned to the canvas: a sword-and-shield knight whose shield is drawn behind the body kept it there in 4 of 4 clips with the built-in sentence and 6 of 7 with a caller's choreography followed by the hold sentence. Characters holding a shield and a hand axe, a spear and a shield, and a lantern and a dagger kept the second item where it was drawn, and a two-handed axe stayed two-handed. Method: [docs/video-pipeline.md](docs/video-pipeline.md).

## v2.10.2 - The spill judgment keys only the subject, and decontam keeps gold

- `video-frames --spill auto` (and `video-set`, which judges each item's `canvas.png`) keys only the subject window of the reference still instead of the whole still. A canvas padded for motion room is mostly key, and keying all of it cost about 0.14 GiB more per megapixel of canvas, past 4 GiB for a 30-megapixel canvas, while keying the frames themselves stays near 1 GiB. The window is the box of pixels the hard cut cannot erase plus one keyed pixel all round, and the painted key is read on the whole still, so it gives exactly the whole still's counts: the decision and the frames do not change. A window over 6 megapixels is judged on every n-th pixel instead; the report records `reference_size`, `reference_window` and `reference_stride`. Background-key detection now reads an image a band of rows at a time. On synthetic padded canvases the judgment peaks at 0.42 to 0.56 GiB from 8 to 39 megapixels, and a synthetic 1080p clip judged against a 39-megapixel canvas runs `video-frames --decontam palette` in 1.1 GiB.
- `--decontam` keeps gold and yellow on stills. A gold a little darker or yellower than the palette's own read as a warmer palette gold with some green mixed in, so gold edges and small gold drops came out orange and partly see-through. The still fit now counts a palette colour at the pixel's own luma as the subject's colour within the subject's own spread (how far its interior sits from the palette); a pixel deeper than a still's antialiased edge (2 px) without the key's hue, or an edge pixel with the colour of the material right behind it, is the subject's own colour; and where the matte changed such a pixel it gets its source colour and coverage back. That last part matters for `cutout`, whose RGB matte scores key tint on the channel average: that calls yellow green, so the matte unmixed gold by itself. On a synthetic gold scene every decontam path now matches the clean YCbCr matte, where `cutout --decontam palette` left 39 % of the solid gold edge see-through. On the procedural ground-truth set key contamination falls from 0.07 % to 0.03 % (RGB matte) and from 0.16 % to 0.12 % (YCbCr matte), halos shrink and recall stays the same. The video fit is unchanged. Reports gain `material_spread` and `restored_px`. Method: [docs/chroma-alpha.md](docs/chroma-alpha.md#decontam--give-the-edge-the-subjects-own-colour-back).

### Gold showcase

A synthetic gold scene (the fixture of `tests/frames/test_decontam_gold.py`) through `cutout --decontam palette` (RGB matte) and `gen`'s YCbCr matte with decontam, v2.10.1 then v2.10.2, on dark and on white.

![Gold drops and strokes before and after](https://github.com/aldegad/sprite-gen/releases/download/v2.10.2/decontam-gold.gif)

## v2.10.1 - Attacks keep the grip the image shows

- `MOTION_TEXT["attack"]` no longer names one hand. What the subject holds is kept exactly as gripped in the image — one hand stays one hand, both hands stay both hands — and is never let go, switched to the other hand or taken in an extra hand. Naming the hand nearest the viewer for a weapon the still draws in both hands made the clip let go of it on the windup and grab it again for the strike.

## v2.10.0 - Edge decontamination gives thin strands their own colour back

- Add opt-in edge decontamination, `--decontam auto|palette` (request `chroma.decontam` for the row extractor and `inspect`). After the matte, each edge pixel is explained as a blend of the locally measured key background with one colour from a palette learned on the subject's own interior. The colour written is that palette colour at the pixel's observed luma, so thin hair strands and outlines come back in the subject's hue: not green, and not the orange that despill's channel gain produces. `palette` demands the pass and fails where it cannot run; `auto` runs it wherever it applies and records why it did not elsewhere. Available on `cutout`, `gen --transparent` (chroma keying), `video-frames` / `video-set` (video fit, one palette per clip) and the row extractor. Every default stays `off`, which returns existing output byte for byte. On a procedural ground-truth set, key contamination at the edge falls from 22.5 % to 0.07 % on stills and from 33 % to 11 % on H.264 frames; halo against the true composite shrinks on dark and white backgrounds, and strand recall rises from 84 % to 99 % (stills) and from 82 % to 91 % (frames). Method, guards and limits: [docs/chroma-alpha.md](docs/chroma-alpha.md).

## v2.9.0 - Idle stands still and closes on its first frame

- `MOTION_TEXT["idle"]` keeps both feet planted flat for the whole clip, limits the motion to breathing, a settle of the arms, hair and loose cloth and one blink, and names walking and marching in place as what not to do. A side-view full body asked for "a subtle weight sway" and an evenly paced loop tended to step in place.
- Idle is pinned to end on its first frame (`PIN_LAST_FRAME_STATES`) and its template (`PINNED_LOOP_TEXT`) asks for that return instead of an evenly paced repeating motion.
- `video-loop --cycle pinned` keeps a pinned clip whole as the loop (every frame but the last, which re-renders the first) and gates it on the pin: the last frame has to land within `max(seam_max x the mean step, PIN_NOISE_MAX)` of the first. A near-still idle moves so little per frame that the seam ratio read its re-render noise as a jump. A clip that ends in another pose fails with its own message. `video-set` cuts idle this way (`PINNED_LOOP_STATES`).

### Idle showcase

Nine side-view idles before and after this release, each strip played at its own speed (`idle-nine.mp4` is the same comparison as video).

![Nine idle loops before and after](https://github.com/aldegad/sprite-gen/releases/download/v2.9.0/idle-nine.gif)

## v2.8.1 - Tight clips pass their own edge and corner checks

- `video-frames --allow-subject-edge-contact` fails only on a frame where the key alone reaches the edge. A frame where the subject touches the edge carries key-tinted pixels beside it (its antialiased fringe, or the key reflected on metal) and is accepted with them, the same reading a mixed contact already had in the refusal message. Before, a few such pixels failed a tight clip as leftover background.
- `video-loop` keeps its 8 transparent columns on both sides of every cell even when the subject reaches the frame's side edge, so no cell corner is the subject and the GIF/WebP corner check holds. A crop that stays inside the frame is unchanged.

## v2.8.0 - Tight framing for a fixed body height

- `video-canvas --fit tight` frames a still with no room for the motion: it drops the empty rows above and below the subject (keeping headroom of 4 % of the subject's height), keeps the still's width, and pads to the nearest framing the video model returns (9:16, 1:1, 16:9). The subject is never scaled or cut. A long, low subject in a square still becomes a 16:9 frame it fills, so a `--body-height` target is reached at a low clip resolution by scaling down rather than up. A motion that leaves the frame is clipped. The default `--fit state` is unchanged, and the report now says which `fit` framed it.
- `video-frames --allow-subject-edge-contact` accepts the subject at the frame edge and still refuses leftover key background there. The report's `edge_policy` names the rule that applied.
- `video-set --fit tight` frames every item that way and lets its subject reach the edge. It refuses `--shape`, and a cached canvas framed with another fit is not reused.
- `--body-height` measures the standing height on the clip's first frame, the base still's pose that every clip starts from. The tallest grounded frame, used before, is an attack's windup with the weapon overhead, and scaling that to the target made the character smaller in its attack than in its walk. The strip sidecar records `body_ref`, `body_src_h` (the standing height as filmed) and `scale`, so a strip whose cells were upscaled says so. Without a target nothing changes.

## v2.7.0 - Timed attack clips pinned to their start

- Attack clips are timed strikes pinned to their start. `MOTION_TEXT["attack"]` asks for the same attack twice, each a windup, one strike in front, a held impact pose and a recovery to the exact starting stance with approximate times, keeps what the subject holds in the hand nearest the viewer, and forbids turning. The attack template drops the "evenly paced" line, keeps what the subject holds inside the frame and asks for crisp frames. `video-set` asks attack clips for 4 s by default (other states keep 3 s; `--duration` still sets one length for all) and passes the canvas as `--last-frame`, so the clip ends where it began. The item report records the clip's `duration` and `last_frame`.
- `build_prompt(..., motion=...)` accepts a caller's own motion paragraph in place of the built-in state sentence, keeping the engine's frame, camera, background and design rules.
- `video-set --body-height N` passes one standing-height target to every state's loop (`video-loop --body-height`), so a character comes out the same size in every state even though states use differently shaped canvases.

## v2.6.0 - Automatic local gait selection and XY motion correction

- In `motion-auto`, an uncertain fine XY search-boundary match can emit the selected frames unchanged only after the existing rendered-cell seam gate and animation checks pass. Reports and the CLI expose the rejected measurement and unapplied correction. Other failures and explicit `motion` registration remain strict.

- Add explicit `video-loop --anchor motion` for reviewed fixed cuts with head and torso regions. Masked XY registration and boundary velocity determine one integer translation ramp; padded cycle PNGs retain the original keyed pixels. Reports record the inputs and shifts, and the unchanged seam threshold measures the rendered output. Automatic cycle selection and existing anchor defaults are unchanged; batch generation rejects this review-only mode before provider work.

- Add opt-in `--anchor motion-auto` for walk/run in `video-loop` and `video-set`: discover stable texture regions, separate drift during local repeat analysis, and apply the same XY boundary-velocity ramp without manual cut/region inputs. Keep source motion and pixels in padded cycle frames; gate the rendered cells. Reports expose automatic tracking, candidate selection, and correction. Existing defaults stay unchanged.

### Gait showcase

Human and animal loops produced by `video-loop --anchor motion-auto`, shown at their original playback speed.

| Humans | Animals |
